# Decisions

## 2026-11-14: harnesslang starts from tempolang's method and nanocode's durability

harnesslang takes tempolang's method (an interpreter as the executable spec, generated code checked against it, deterministic simulation from a seed, invariants as compiler checks) and applies it to nanocode's problem: making an agent harness durable on hosts that promise different things. The journal follows nanocode-turnlog's records, the effect classes are nanocode's `EffectClass`, and the simulator's invariants, fault profiles and mutation discipline are nanocode-sim's, so that nanocode is the oracle.

The compiler is one std-only Rust crate. Generated programs are std-only too. Targets are models in `src/target/` that the checker, the lowering and the simulator share.

## 2026-11-14: the core: parser, checker, IR, replay interpreter, target models and simulator

`src/syntax` lexes and parses the language of `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` with an explicit nesting bound (`NESTING_DEPTH_MAX` = 64, policy: deeper than any harness we write, shallow enough for the default thread stack). `src/check` resolves names and types, proves matches exhaustive (a `maybe` match without `unknown` names the reason), requires every loop and list capacity to be a named constant and lowers straight to `src/ir`. `src/ir` holds the lowered program, its validation (pair assertions: every site placed exactly once, durability follows class) and the worst-case walk. `src/run` is the replay interpreter, the journal (JSON lines with CRC-32, own JSON codec and CRC) and the session fold. `src/target` holds the five target models and the storage media the simulator drives. `src/sim` is `hl sim`. `hl emit` exists and fails with "not yet" until `src/emit` is built.

Decisions the spec left open:

- Journal lines are `{"crc":N,"record":R}`; `R` follows nanocode-turnlog's `Record` field for field so serde decodes it: `admitted` (digest = handler name, first = 1, the first sequence number of a turn, hand = null, metadata = {handler}, prompt = the arguments by name), `call`, `result`, `model`, `recovery`, `status` (always `finished`, outcome `completed` or `failed`), `checkpoint` (payload is the state as JSON text, state is the harness name, revision is the turn it follows). `call`, `result` and `model` add `seq`, which serde ignores. Call ids are `turn/seq`.
- The model writes only a `model` record; `now` and `random` write `call` and `result` with tool names `now` and `random`. Worst-case records per effect are 2 (model 1), plus admitted, status and checkpoint per turn; recovery records are one per restart and are reported apart.
- Effects are hoisted out of expressions: a statement's effects run first, left to right. `and`/`or` evaluate both sides. No shadowing. A variant constructor takes its type from the expected type, else from the one sum that has it, else it is an error naming the candidates. State fields need a zero value (u64, bool, text, list). Tools return u64, bool or text so the simulator can produce results. `reply` and `entry` need a model.
- Every finish writes a checkpoint in the same flush. State before a turn is the latest checkpoint plus replay of any finished turns after it (a torn checkpoint line is recovered by replay). The simulator also checks that replaying the whole journal from nothing gives the same state.
- Recovery is `AtLoad` (the runner turns open external calls into a `recovery` record before the handler runs) everywhere but Vercel, where it is `InStep`: the external step reads a durable attempt counter and returns `unknown` on any attempt after the first, writing a `recovery` record for that one call. Recovery attempts number per turn.
- Cloudflare commits before every outbound request (the output gate) and before the response; Vercel also commits each external step's result when the step returns; Postgres takes a new owner epoch on every run and refuses a flush from an older epoch; JSONL fsyncs on open before trusting what the page cache shows, then truncates a torn tail.
- Acknowledging a finish checks that its status is durable at that moment; that is how `finish_once` sees an early acknowledgement whose outcome happens to repeat.
- `world.medium.durable().ok()?` in the simulator treats an unreadable durable journal as "no durable status" when an acknowledgement is checked, which reports the acknowledgement as a `finish_once` violation; the unreadable journal itself is reported by `liveness`.

Simulator policy constants (src/sim): `TURNS_PER_SESSION_MAX` 4, `RESTARTS_PER_TURN_MAX` 12 (after the budget a run is fault free, so `liveness` fails only on a bug), `RUNS_PER_TURN_MAX` = restarts + 2 (the clean run and a fenced old owner), crash probability per hook 2% to 25% per seed, torn writes and crashes between write and fsync up to 30%, power loss on a crash 30% to 100% in the async and lying profiles, half of unsynced 512-byte sectors persisted in the lying profile, lease expiry 2% to 10% per hook in the Postgres lease profile, model answers 35% of the time, 25% of requested tools undeclared, 0.5% of replies over the calls bound (the handler then fails with `bound`), 15% of tool runs fail. Profiles: 3 in 10 seeds clean, the rest errors (JSONL: also async 2 in 10 and lying 2 in 10; Postgres: lease 4 in 10). JSONL "lying" here means a power loss that persists unsynced sectors out of order, the unpersisted ones reading as zeros.

Mutations (`hl sim --mutation`, `scripts/mutations.sh` runs each with `--twice` on seeds 0..200 of examples/agent.hl) and the first catching seed:

| Mutation | Targets | Caught by | Seed |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| flush_after_dispatch | jsonl, sqlite, postgres | external_once | 0 |
| recovery_redispatches_external | jsonl, sqlite, postgres, cloudflare | external_once | 0 |
| vercel_retry_redispatches | vercel | external_once | 0 |
| postgres_no_fence | postgres | external_once | 4 |
| jsonl_no_checksum | jsonl | liveness (the torn tail is decoded and fails) | 0 |
| finish_before_flush | jsonl, sqlite, postgres, vercel | finish_once | 0 |
| idempotent_reported_unknown | jsonl, cloudflare | idempotent_known | 44 on jsonl, 0 on cloudflare |
| gate_releases_early | cloudflare | external_once | 0 |
| recovery_reports_failed | jsonl, sqlite, postgres, cloudflare | unknown_reported | 0 |
| rerun_rewrites_call | jsonl, cloudflare | seq_unique | 44 on jsonl, 0 on cloudflare |
| checkpoint_hash_order | all | replay_equal (with --twice) | 0 or 1 |

A mutation lists only the targets where it has a path to manifest. flush_after_dispatch and finish_before_flush are lowering bugs that Cloudflare's output gate makes harmless, and the simulator agrees. idempotent_reported_unknown and rerun_rewrites_call need a none or idempotent `call` record durable without its result; on SQLite, Postgres and Vercel the two share one transaction or step, so it never happens, while a torn JSONL write and Cloudflare's commit before each outbound request produce it. checkpoint_hash_order writes the checkpoint through a std `HashMap`; its catching seed depends on the process's hash keys, the one deliberate nondeterminism in the crate.

Platform facts in `src/target/mod.rs`, all read through web search summaries of the vendor docs on 2026-11-14 rather than from the pages themselves, so treat them as unverified until someone reads the pages:

- Cloudflare Workers Paid: 10,000 subrequests per invocation by default, configurable to 10,000,000; Free: 50 (developers.cloudflare.com/workers/platform/limits, changelog 2026-02-11 "Workers are no longer limited to 1000 subrequests"). The check uses 10,000.
- Cloudflare Durable Objects: 128 MB memory per isolate, shared by objects in it (developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/platform/limits). Stored as 128 MiB; whether "MB" means MiB is unverified. Not checked, since text values carry no bound.
- Vercel Functions with fluid compute: 300 s default, 800 s maximum on Pro and Enterprise, 300 s on Hobby, up to 1,800 s in beta (vercel.com/docs/functions/limitations). Stored as 800 s and reported, not checked: effect durations are not modelled.
- Vercel Workflow steps: `maxRetries` defaults to 3, so 4 attempts (workflow-sdk.dev/docs/foundations/errors-and-retries). Unverified and assumed: the attempt number is durable before a step body runs, so a retried external step can tell it may have run.
- Cloudflare output gate semantics and alarm restart after a reset are taken from the spec, not re-verified. The alarm is not modelled: a restart always follows a reset.
- 512-byte sectors: the common Linux logical block size, used only by the simulator's lying profile.

Measurements (5-core Linux Hand, rustc 1.100.0):

- `cargo build --release --locked` from clean: 3.5 s; `target/release/hl` 9.5 MB with `debug = 1`, 1.0 MB stripped; Cargo.lock lists harnesslang alone; 7,382 lines of Rust in src.
- examples/agent.hl, 2,000 seeds: native-jsonl 340 seeds/s, native-sqlite 409, server-postgres 450, cloudflare 365, vercel 393 (one core each).
- Sweeps that passed: agent.hl seeds 0..20000 on every target (jsonl: 49,772 turns, 201,237 crashes, 18,852 power losses, 27,409 torn writes, 71,723 unknown results; postgres: 13,065 takeovers, 8,669 fenced flushes); reviewer.hl seeds 0..5000 on every target; every example seeds 0..2000 on every target and agent.hl 0..300 `--twice` in `scripts/check.sh`.
- `scripts/check.sh` end to end: 15 s (sweeps run one target per core).

Commands: `scripts/check.sh`, `scripts/mutations.sh`, `target/release/hl sim --target T --seeds A..B [--twice] [--mutation M] FILE`, `target/release/hl check --target T FILE`, `target/release/hl run FILE --handler NAME --arg VALUE`.

## 2026-11-14: the nanocode oracle, the panic invariant and recovery_skipped

`oracle/` is its own Cargo workspace (`oracle/target` ignored) and the one place with outside dependencies: `nanocode-turnlog` by git from https://github.com/nanocodexyz/nanocode at 395967ffd227ef2158ea824df6ee4c6054d30293 (the reference checkout's HEAD; GitHub's HEAD was 83489e4 the same day), `serde`, `serde_json` (raw_value) and `crc` (its CRC_32_ISO_HDLC catalog entry, so the checksum is computed by code that is not ours). `oracle/Cargo.lock` was seeded from nanocode's own `Cargo.lock`, so the oracle builds the versions nanocode pins (serde 1.0.228, serde_json 1.0.150, tokio 1.52.3), and `[patch.crates-io] serde_derive` points at nanocode's own derive crate as nanocode's workspace does: a git dependency's patches do not reach the depending workspace, and without the patch Cargo resolved serde 1.0.229 with crates.io's serde_derive, syn, quote and proc-macro2 (38 packages instead of 34). tokio, mio, socket2 and the futures crates arrive through nanocode-turnlog's own dependencies; the oracle uses none of them.

What it checks (`hl sim --dump-journal DIR` writes `<example>-<target>-<seed>.jsonl` and `.summary.jsonl`, the interpreter's `run::session` fold after every record; `hl-oracle DIR...` compares):

- Line rules: the oracle's own reader (serde envelope, `crc` crate, stop at the first failing or unterminated line; for rows, every line must be JSON) must read exactly the records the interpreter's `decode_lines` reads.
- Every record decodes as `nanocode_turnlog::Record` and applies to a `nanocode_turnlog::Fold` (with no row, so `next_attempt` is attempt + 1) exactly when `session::apply` accepts it.
- After every record, for the turn it touched: phase (nanocode's Queued or Running is our running), outcome, last, result, digest and prompt, recovery attempt, model replies, open calls with classes, the external open calls a recovery must report unknown (`Fold::unknown` of that turn), and every call's class. The comparison runs at every prefix because every load the interpreter makes is a prefix of the final journal (JSONL truncates a torn tail and appends; rows only append).
- Before a call record: its class is nanocode's `Effects::default()` over the program's declarations plus `now` and `random` as none (`Effects::builtin()` is nanocode's own tool list and does not apply). Before a recovery: its attempt is `next_attempt`; at load it lists exactly the open external calls of its turn, in step (Vercel) exactly one of them. Before a status: nanocode holds no call of that turn open. At the end of a passing seed every turn is finished and nothing is open.

Measurements (5-core Linux Hand shared with four agents, rustc 1.100.0):

- Clean `cargo build --release --locked -j2` in `oracle/`: 23.8 s, first fetch about 10 s; 34 packages in `oracle/Cargo.lock` (8 nanocode crates plus nanocode's serde_derive); `hl-oracle` 1.4 MB; 786 lines of Rust in oracle/src, 222 in src/sim/dump.rs.
- `ORACLE_SEEDS=0..2000 scripts/oracle.sh`: 30,000 journals (3 examples x 5 targets x 2,000 seeds), 1,631,381 records, 75,480 turns (451 failed), calls external 326,551, idempotent 106,699, none 188,458, 162,317 model replies, 65,267 recoveries reporting 65,267 unknown calls, 326,551 prefixes with an open external call: 0 disagreements. Journals and summaries take 1.2 GB in output/; `hl-oracle` reads them in 7.5 s on one core.
- Default `scripts/oracle.sh` (seeds 0..500, 7,500 journals, 409,107 records, then five mutation runs of agent.hl seeds 0..200): 8.6 s. `HL_ORACLE=1 scripts/check.sh`: 43 s end to end.
- Disagreements on unmutated journals: none, so nothing to fix on either side. Under mutations the oracle disagrees with postgres_no_fence (67), idempotent_reported_unknown (38 on jsonl, 240 on cloudflare) and rerun_rewrites_call (38, 240), which `scripts/oracle.sh` requires; jsonl_no_checksum's torn journals are refused by both readers alike (39 of seeds 0..100). The other seven mutations write journals both folds read alike: their bugs show only against the world (dispatch counts, acknowledgements), which a journal does not hold, so the simulator's invariants remain the only check for them.
- Every mutation disagreement is nanocode's fold being more permissive than ours, not a bug on either side, so nothing was reported upstream: `Fold::apply` accepts a result for a call that is not open or whose turn finished, a second `admitted` for a finished turn id (it replaces the turn), a `call` for an id already open (it overwrites it) and a recovery listing calls that are not open external (it removes them). nanocode's single writer is what keeps those out of its logs; harnesslang's session fold refuses them.

nanocode-sim's turn scenario against ours. Invariants: it has external_once, finish_once, idempotent_known, in_object_once, liveness, panic and seq_unique. in_object_once (a workspace write or spawn committed inside the object's transaction applied once) has no harnesslang counterpart until there is an in-object effect such as `spawn`. panic was missing: a broken assertion aborted `hl sim` with no seed. `run_seed_traced` now catches the panic at the seed boundary and the sweep reports `FAIL invariant=panic` with the seed and replay command; the assertion still panics, as TigerStyle wants, and the default hook still prints where. Mutations: finish_before_row and durable_seq_before_commit (telling a client something is durable before its commit) are our finish_before_flush; gate_releases_early and idempotent_reported_unknown are ours already; retire_skipped and seal_skipped are compaction, which harnesslang does not have yet; tick_not_atomic has no counterpart that could be caught, by argument rather than simulation: our flushes are ordered and any prefix of one is a state a torn JSONL group commit already produces. recovery_skips_clientless becomes `recovery_skipped`: at-load recovery reports nothing (every harnesslang rerun is a restart without a client, so it skips all of them). It shares `recover`'s path with recovery_redispatches_external but is not exempt from the engine's assertion that recovery turns every open external call unknown before the turn runs again, so the panic invariant catches it at seed 0 on native-jsonl, native-sqlite, server-postgres and cloudflare (not Vercel, whose recovery is in step). `scripts/mutations.sh` runs it and now prints each mutation's FAIL line rather than the first line of output.

Commands: `hl sim --target T --seeds A..B [--mutation M] --dump-journal DIR FILE`, `oracle/target/release/hl-oracle DIR...`, `scripts/oracle.sh` (ORACLE_SEEDS, ORACLE_MUTATION_SEEDS), `HL_ORACLE=1 scripts/check.sh`.

## 2026-11-14: a kill -9 crash drill for native-jsonl generated code

`scripts/rust-crash-drill.sh` emits examples/agent.hl for native-jsonl, compiles it with `scripts/rust/drill_host.rs` (a host whose tools append a synced ledger line before they run and whose acknowledgements append a synced line) and runs each session's process repeatedly, sending it kill -9 after 0 to `DRILL_DELAY_MS_MAX` ms until a run exits 0. Then it checks the real files: external_once, unknown_reported, idempotent_known, finish_once, every acknowledgement matching its status. Defaults: 16 sessions of 6 turns, 12 ms, 400 kills per session (policy: about 7 kills a session on this Hand, well under the cap). `DRILL_SEED` offsets the seeds, and any failure exits non-zero and prints `drill: FAILED` on stdout, so a pipe hides nothing. `HL_DRILL=1 scripts/check.sh` runs 4 sessions, then requires `DRILL_MUTATION=flush_after_dispatch` (the generated code with its flush before an external effect removed by sed) to fail with an external call that ran before its call record was durable, or whose id was reused for another tool after that record was lost. It caught the mutation in 32 of 32 trials, at session 1 or 2.

The first version failed with "idempotent 6/37 ran before any call record". This was the drill's check, not the generated code. In a killed run, idempotent `edit` ran at 6/37 after 6/35 and the model at 6/36, with none of their records flushed, which durability point 3 allows. The kill lost them, and after recovery the model ran again and answered, so 6/37 was never asked for again. The drill's model is seeded from the clock, so a model that is run again can answer differently, as a real one can. The check now models the spec exactly. Each run writes a `run` marker to the ledger. External dispatches, from any run, need a durable call record for the same tool and run once. None and idempotent dispatches in the run that exited 0 need a durable call record for the same tool, because the status flush covers every buffered record (point 2). Those in killed runs may be missing (point 3). Every none or idempotent call record has a result, and that result has a dispatch of its id and tool in some run (`now` and `random` excepted). The engine already asserts that a call record is durable before its external effect runs, and `flush_after_dispatch` covers that in `hl sim`, so no new mutation was needed.

Measurements (5-core Linux Hand shared with another agent): 12 full default drills with seed bases 0 to 1200, all passing, 1,033 kill -9s over 1,225 runs, 41,779 records, 8,962 external calls (616 reported unknown), 8,611 none or idempotent calls, and 72 none or idempotent dispatches lost in a kill and never recorded. A default drill takes about 1.5 s; `HL_DRILL=1 scripts/check.sh` took 21 s end to end. Known limit: `run_jsonl` reopens and folds the whole journal for each turn, and every checkpoint holds the whole state. At 24 turns, one restart takes 39 ms just to pass 12 finished turns, so a 30 ms kill delay stops all progress (400 kills, stuck at turn 12). This is restart cost, not durability. It needs a checkpoint-bounded open before longer drills.

## 2026-11-14: the prover: push_bound is an error, an independent worst case, prove-mutations and a soundness audit

`src/prove` (committed in f3d94d4 by an agent whose notes were lost) proves, per handler and target, the properties `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` lists under Proofs. This entry records what was established on top of it.

- **push_bound is an error.** It was a warning, so `examples/minimal.hl` "proved" while every push in it could trap with `bound(history)` (counterexample `len(history) = 256`), and the report printed the handler's arithmetic as proved. That contradicts the goal that a proving program does not trap in its own code: `no_overflow` was already an error, and a full list is the same kind of failure. A push is now an obligation like any other, `Severity` is gone (every finding is an error), and `examples/minimal.hl` gains `requires len(history) + 1 + steps_max * (1 + calls_max) <= history_max` (41 of 256 entries per turn), the form agent.hl and reviewer.hl already use. Once history lacks room the handler refuses the turn with `requires(ask)`, which is the honest behaviour until compaction exists. `examples/invalid/over_cloudflare.hl` gains the same kind of `requires` so it still proves on every target but Cloudflare, which is what it is for.
- **worst_case was not the prover's own.** It compared `ir::worst`'s count with the limit, so the property was reported proved from a number the prover never derived. The prover now counts the longest path of outbound runs in its own plan (choices take the widest arm, loops multiply by their bound) and asserts that `ir::worst` is not below it, a pair assertion between two computations of one bound. They agree on every example: 1,088 (agent), 40 (minimal), 57 and 0 (reviewer), 17,408 (over_cloudflare).
- **The audit found no other unsound proof.** Read: the plan walk (every choice entered from every state, loops iterated to a fixpoint within their bound, `End` checks the phase), the finish walk, loop summaries (the body runs once with the changed quantities free; `modified` covers every way a quantity changes, since locals are never reassigned and `push` targets fields only; `for` counts the list's length at entry, matching the interpreter's snapshot; a body that never falls through yields no exit for `loop` and an empty list for `for`), joins (only facts both sides imply, plus hulls), and the solver (gcd tightening rounds the constant up, box subsumption only drops implied constraints, every `Overflow` or `Budget` result is taken as "not proved" or "feasible", every failed projection drops facts). Traps the interpreter can raise are `requires` (state unchanged), overflow and full pushes (both obligations now) and an undecodable model reply (invariants checked there). Then probed with adversarial programs; each class now has an invalid example that `scripts/prove-mutations.sh` requires to fail with its message: `overflow_counter`, `underflow_elapsed`, `loop_overflow` (only the seventh addition overflows), `product_overflow`, `branch_overflow` (overflow on one side of a join), `push_unbounded` (the old minimal.hl), `push_unknown_arm` (the `unknown` arm of a dispatch pushes twice), `push_self_iteration` (a `for` over the list it pushes onto), `push_early_return` (a loop that returns on some paths), `invariant_return` (nested loops), `invariant_trap` (a counter restored before return but left raised when the model reply fails to decode), `invariant_initial` and `invariant_unsupported` (an invariant over a `bool` fails instead of being assumed). Durability and finishing bugs cannot be written in a program, since the compiler places every flush and `hl check` already refuses a handler that can reach its end; the mutations cover them.
- **Known imprecision, not unsoundness.** Counterexamples are solutions of the prover's facts, not replays: `branch_overflow` reports `base = 18446744073709551606` from the hull of 5 and 18446744073709551610, and a loop's summary can report values no run reaches. The loop summary keeps a per-iteration change, not a closed form, so `x = x + step` over `loop step below 8` bounds `x` by `[0, 56]` rather than 28, and `x - 28` after it fails.
- **scripts/prove-mutations.sh**, run by `scripts/check.sh` right after `scripts/mutations.sh`: every example proves; for every mutation `hl prove` lists (16: the simulator's 12 lowering mutations and `drop_flush`, `drop_finish`, `drop_requires`, `repeat_assign`), proving agent.hl and reviewer.hl fails on every target it applies to, with exit 1 and the property the script expects for it (a mutation without one fails the script); and the 13 invalid examples fail with their exact message. Mutations run in parallel, one process per mutation.

Measurements (5-core Linux Hand shared with another agent, rustc 1.100.0, release build, mean of 20 runs, all five targets): `hl prove` examples/minimal.hl 6.6 ms, examples/agent.hl 11.3 ms, examples/reviewer.hl 194 ms (186 ms on one target: the arithmetic proof, 70,913 elimination steps for `review_change`, dominates and is target independent; agent.hl takes 4,098 steps, minimal.hl 3,227). `scripts/prove-mutations.sh`: 11.1 s sequential, 3.3 s with one process per mutation.

Commands: `target/release/hl prove [--target T] [--mutation M] FILE`, `scripts/prove-mutations.sh`.

## 2026-11-14: code mode design (docs/CODE_MODE.md)

Code mode is specified, not built: `docs/CODE_MODE.md` holds the `code` declaration, the IR additions, the cell journal, recovery and placement per target, the simulator's faults and invariants, the prover's obligations, the mutations and the oracle's checks. It follows nanocode at 395967f, read on 2026-11-14 in /home/hand/ref/nanocode: `crates/runtime/code-mode/src/code_mode/replay.rs` (`CellJournal`, `CellJournalUpdate`, `JournaledCall`, `CellJournal::fold`, `UNKNOWN_OUTCOME`, `unknown_outcome()`, `divergence_text`), `code_mode/mod.rs` (`Nested::Recover` falling back to the unknown outcome, `resume_replayed`: queued calls dispatched, running calls without an outcome recovered, `CellGuard`, `Limits.nested`), `crates/runtime/code-mode-engine/src/journal.rs` (`JournalEntry`, `same_step`, `JOURNAL_LIMIT_BYTES` 32 MiB), `crates/runtime/agent/src/one_log.rs` (`OneLogJournals::save`: a row commits when an update carries a running call whose tool `needs_row()`, or a `Timeout` while the header is new or unsaved), `crates/core/turnlog/src/{record,fold}.rs` (`Record::Cell`, `Record::Forget`, `Fold::unknown` = every open external call) and nanocode's DECISIONS.md entry of 2026-11-14 on first saves (rows per turn: reply 2, `text("ok")` cell 2, timer cell 3, `tools.charge` cell 3). `code_mode_policy.rs` in the Claude and Codex backends only routes workspace tools and marks writers exclusive, which harnesslang's nested tools do not need. durable-cloudflare runs cells in the session's Durable Object (nanocode-ts's djs wasm, cell memory 32 MiB, half of the 64 MiB runtime budget); durable-vercel in the Function; both moved to the first-save rule on 2026-11-14 and their drills pass.

Decisions the design takes:

- The engine is an interface (nanocode's journal protocol), not part of harnesslang: a std-only compiler cannot embed djs. Native hosts bind djs; the simulator has its own deterministic engine whose cells also carry their JavaScript, so `oracle/` runs them in djs and compares the `JournalEntry` streams.
- Cloudflare runs a cell in a Worker Loader isolate with `globalOutbound: null`, so the isolate cannot send anything the output gate does not hold; Vercel runs it in the re-executed workflow function with each nested call a step. Both rest on platform facts not yet read from the vendor pages (listed in CODE_MODE.md); where one is false the target refuses `code` rather than weaken at-most-once.
- The code site's own `call` record is class `none`: the cell is replayable and its external work is journaled per nested call. nanocode's `Effects` must declare code tools `none` in the oracle.
- `calls` bounds nested calls across all tools (nanocode's guard is per tool), so the prover multiplies one number.
- harnesslang writes the nested `call` record's `turn`, which nanocode leaves `None`; no `forget` or `stored` records.
- No yield or `wait` yet: a turn blocks on its cell; the header carries `yield_time_ms` for nanocode's fold.
- Simulator policy: cells in 20% of tool-call replies, engine divergence in 1% of replays.

No code changed, so there is nothing to measure; `scripts/check.sh` passes on this commit as on its parent.

## 2026-11-14: native-jsonl opens from the last trusted checkpoint, and the drill client resumes from its last acknowledgement

The generated native-jsonl runtime no longer reads and folds the whole journal on every turn. `jsonl_open_tail` fsyncs, reads backward from the end in windows that start at `PAGE_BYTES` (4096, the Linux page size, the unit the page cache reads) and double, and stops at the last checkpoint line that has a valid CRC and any byte after its newline. It decodes forward from that line with the same line rule as before (a failing or unterminated line ends the journal and is truncated, then synced) and folds from the checkpoint (`Start::Checkpoint`, `fold_from`, `run_turn_from`), so an open costs the checkpoint plus the work after it. `JsonlJournal` keeps that tail in memory across the turns of one process: after a finished turn its tail is the checkpoint it just synced, so a process running many turns never reopens. A request for a turn at or before the tail's checkpoint reads the whole journal (`jsonl_open_for`, and `JsonlJournal::run`), since only the whole journal holds that turn's status. The durability points, flushes, records and bytes written are unchanged: `drill_host bench` writes the same records with the same fsyncs before and after.

Why a checkpoint needs a byte after it. Every flush is one append then one fsync, a failed fsync ends the run, and an open syncs and truncates to the valid prefix before anything is appended, so only the last flush group can hold a torn, zeroed or out-of-order line (the lying profile in `src/target/medium.rs` loses only unsynced sectors). A checkpoint is always the last record of its finish flush, so any byte after it was appended after its group's fsync returned: that checkpoint and everything before it are durable and valid, and reading from it gives exactly what the whole read gives. The last che

## 2026-11-14: wait(ms) and suspend(key): durable timers and parked turns on every target

What this establishes (branch suspend-wait, from master e4ee440, merged with master at c131d0c), closing the last two gaps (7 and 8) of the earlier GAPS.md:

- **wait(c).** `wait(c)`, `c` a named constant in milliseconds, is a `none` effect site `SiteKind::Wait(ConstId)` with durability `FlushBeforeRun`: the runtime reads the world clock (a new `World::clock`), appends `{"call":...,"input":{"ms":c,"until":T}}`, flushes everything buffered (code mode's C2, for a top-level wait), sleeps (`World::sleep(turn, seq, until)`) and records the clock at its wake. A rerun that finds the call open sleeps only until the recorded `until`; replay compares the call without `until`. The worst case gains a fourth measure, milliseconds of waits, multiplied by loops (`ir/worst.rs`), printed by `hl check` only when nonzero so existing quoted output does not move.
- **Wall-time limits, with sources** (`target::WaitLimit`): Cloudflare 900,000 ms of waits per run, summed, from the alarm handler's 15-minute wall time (developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/platform/limits), since a turn a reset leaves unfinished resumes in the alarm; Vercel 800,000 ms per wait, from a Function's 800 s (vercel.com/docs/functions/limitations), checked per wait because a wait's flush ends a step and a step holds at most one wait; native and Postgres none. `hl check --target` refuses (`examples/invalid/over_wait.hl`, `long_wait.hl`) and the prover's `worst_case` counts the plan's `Sleep` ops against the same limit.
- **suspend(key).** Needs `on resume`. A fresh suspend appends its `call` (`{"key":K}`, class `none`), flushes and ends the run without a status: `Ran::Suspended { turn, seq, key }`. The waiting status is the durable open call, which nanocode's fold reads as an open call; no new record kind, so the SQL kinds and the oracle are untouched. The host delivers a resume `{key, answer}` through `World::resume`, read when a run reaches the open suspend (not at run start: Vercel reruns the turn in a new step after the suspend's flush, and only the suspend site sees the same world calls on every backend); a matching key appends the result, flushes it and then `World::resumed` answers the sender; another key answers unmatched and the turn waits again. Delivery paths: `Host::resume` (Rust), `POST /resume` on the Durable Object (which disarms its alarm when a turn suspends), a workflow hook with token `session:turn:seq:resume` on Vercel.
- **Examples.** `examples/hooks.hl` gains an `"ask"` permission mode (a non-Read call waits for `"allow"`; any other answer is the denial's reason) and gives the Stop hook's block and stop reasons through suspends. `examples/subagents.hl` (and its inlined twin) waits `wait_agent_ms` before reading a child's status; nanocode's `DEFAULT_WAIT_TIMEOUT` is 30 s, but 64 rounds of 16 calls leave 878 ms per wait under Cloudflare's alarm, so the port waits 800 ms. `docs/snippets/park.hl` is the small program LANGUAGE.md, the exhaustive sim and the prover mutations use.
- **Simulator.** A seeded clock that a wait advances to its deadline, crashes in the middle of a wait (the clock stops at a random point before the deadline), resumes arriving up to `SUSPEND_DELAY_MS_MAX` (8 h) after a turn reported waiting with another key at `WRONG_KEY_PPM` (one in five), and `RUNS_PER_SUSPENSION` (2) extra runs per worst-case effect. New invariants (31 in all), each caught by a planned mutation: `suspend_durable` (`suspend_before_flush`), `resume_matched` (`resume_unmatched`), `wait_until` (`wait_rerun_skips`, `wait_oversleeps`), `wall_limit` (`over_wait.hl` on cloudflare and `long_wait.hl` on vercel past the checker, in `scripts/strict.sh`). `resume_replays_suspend` is caught by `seq_unique`, `wait_unjournaled` by `idempotent_known` (on park.hl by `liveness`). `scripts/exhaustive.sh` explores park.hl at depth 1 on every target and requires all six mutations caught on all five.
- **Prover.** A suspend lowers to a choice of parking (`Op::Park`, which must find nothing buffered and its call durable: new property `suspend_durable`), parking again when reopened, taking a resume (result appended and flushed), being resumed inline (so the plan's longest record path still equals the IR's count) and replay (a second call is `seq_unique`). A wait lowers to `Op::Sleep`, which must follow the flush of its call (new property `durable_before_wait`). `suspend_before_flush` and `resume_replays_suspend` are lowering mutations; `scripts/prove-mutations.sh` requires them to fail `hooks.hl` and leave agent.hl and reviewer.hl proved.
- **Generated code.** Rust: `Kind::Wait`/`Kind::Suspend`, `Run::Wait`, `End::Suspended`, `Ran::Suspended`, `Host::clock/sleep/resume/resumed` with defaults (a real `std::thread::sleep`), forwarded by `JsonlWorld` and `SqlWorld`; `hl-agent` reports a suspended turn as an error since it never resumes. JavaScript: `waitSite`/`suspendSite` in engine.js, the site literal carries `ms`, Vercel steps return `suspended` and `resumed` and `driveTurn` waits on `platform.resume` (`STEPS_PER_SUSPENSION_MAX` = 2 more steps per effect). The differential drivers read `clock`, `sleep` and `resumed` events and peek for `resume` (the interpreter traces a resume only when one is delivered).
- **GAPS.md** now lists what a fresh read of nanocode c7679e3's `agent.rs` still does that harnesslang cannot express (token accounting, summarizing compaction, recovery by stop reason, retry and backoff, uniform hooks, changing catalogs, subagents and forks, rich content), with master's gaps 9 to 11 from `claude_code.hl` after them.

Losses and departures, kept:

- On Vercel a wait runs inside the step (Function time) rather than as a workflow `sleep()` between steps, which would cost no Function time and have no limit; on Cloudflare a wait blocks the event rather than arming the alarm at its deadline and ending the invocation. Both are in ARCHITECTURE.md's Next. The Cloudflare sum is conservative: a suspend ends the invocation, but waits on both sides of it are added up.
- `wait` takes a constant, so nanocode's model-chosen `timeout_ms` and backoff delays are not expressible (GAPS.md 4).
- The suspend's answer is text: `hooks.hl` takes the Stop hook's decision from the text-enum tool and only its reason from the resume.
- No JavaScript (`scripts/js-mutations.sh`) or sed differential plants were added for the new paths; they are covered by the interpreter mutations, the exhaustive sim and the differential runs of hooks.hl, subagents.hl and park.hl-like programs.
- A mistake on the way: rustfmt was first run over `src/emit/rust/runtime/*.rs`, which master keeps unformatted because they are emitted text (LANGUAGE.md quotes them); they were restored to master's formatting with the semantic diff re-applied. Run rustfmt on `git ls-files 'src/*.rs' | grep -v emit/rust/runtime/`.

Measurements (5-core Linux Hand shared with two other agents, `nice`, load average 8 to 20):

- `hl sim --seeds 0..300` passes hooks.hl on every target (native-jsonl: 747 turns, 3,715 runs, 2,102 crashes, 866 suspensions) and subagents.hl (native-jsonl: 4,344 runs, 1,932 sleeps); a scratch approvals program with a wait per round and a suspend per call passes seeds 0..300 on every target (native-jsonl 2,683 sleeps, ~3,600 suspensions per target).
- Generated Rust matches the interpreter on that program seeds 0..500 (`RUST_DIFF_OPT=0`) on the three Rust targets, and generated JavaScript on seeds 0..500 on cloudflare (12,540 runs, 3,813 sleeps, 5,540 resumes) and vercel (29,980 steps). In check.sh: hooks.hl seeds 0..300 on Rust (native-jsonl 814 resumes) and 0..100 on JS (vercel 2,998 steps, 287 resumes); subagents.hl 0..300 Rust (1,932 sleeps) and 0..100 JS.
- Mutations on hooks.hl and subagents.hl seeds 0..200: all six first fail at seed 0 on every target, except `resume_replays_suspend` on cloudflare (seed 16) and `wait_rerun_skips` on cloudflare (seed 5).
- `hl sim --exhaustive --depth 1 --seeds 0..3 docs/snippets/park.hl`: native-jsonl 636 states, 1,386 runs, 231 crashes, 512 torn writes; server-postgres 1,036 states, 231 takeovers; vercel 546 states.
- `CHECK_JOBS=3 nice scripts/check.sh` passed on cbf45a9 (before the merge) in 1,031 s of its own timing: strict 831 s, sim 60 jobs 588 s wall, js-diff 262 s, docs 100 s, rust-diff 74 s, exhaustive 16 s.

Commands: `hl check --target cloudflare examples/invalid/over_wait.hl`, `hl sim --target T --seeds 0..300 examples/hooks.hl`, `hl sim --target T --seeds 0..200 --mutation M examples/subagents.hl`, `hl sim --exhaustive --depth 1 --target T --seeds 0..3 docs/snippets/park.hl`, `hl prove --target T --mutation suspend_before_flush docs/snippets/park.hl`, `RUST_DIFF_FILES=F RUST_DIFF_SEEDS=0..500 RUST_DIFF_OPT=0 scripts/rust-diff.sh`, `JS_DIFF_FILES=F JS_DIFF_SEEDS=0..500 scripts/js-diff.sh`, `scripts/mutations.sh --census`, `scripts/exhaustive.sh`, `scripts/prove-mutations.sh`, `CHECK_JOBS=3 nice scripts/check.sh`.

## 2026-11-14: tool parameter defaults, model call modes and text builtins; claude_code.hl owns what hl-agent's host did, and hl-agent streams

GAPS.md's gaps 9, 10 and 11 are closed and removed. Each is the smallest addition that let `examples/claude_code.hl` take over behaviour `agent/`'s host had approximated.

- **Defaults (gap 9).** `param: type = value` on `tool` and `tools from listing` parameters; the value is a literal of the type or, for `u64`, a named constant (`ir::Literal`, one slot per parameter in `Tool::defaults` and `Listing::defaults`). The input gate fills each missing defaulted parameter before it checks the input, identically in the interpreter (`value::typed_input`), the generated Rust (`input_default` lines in each `input_N`, which now take `&mut Json`) and the generated JavaScript (`toolInput`), so the `call` record, the host and replays see the filled object. A program's direct call still passes every argument. The simulator omits each defaulted parameter with `DEFAULT_OMITTED_PPM` = 500,000 (policy: half the calls exercise the fill), drawn after the values and only for defaulted parameters, so programs without defaults draw exactly as before. Mutation `default_unfilled` accepts the input without filling it and is caught by `tool_admitted` at seed 0 on every target (`docs/snippets/defaults.hl`, `examples/claude_code.hl`). Not covered: a code cell's nested calls, whose input no backend gates by type yet.
- **Modes (gap 10).** `model m calls N [reply R] modes a, b` and `m(context) mode a`. `ir::Site::mode` carries the index; the checker refuses an undeclared mode and a mode after anything but the model. The host receives it (`ModelRequest::mode` in the interpreter and the Rust runtime, `mode` in the JavaScript request and `modelRequestJson`), and the `model` record carries `"mode"` only when the call has one, so every existing journal encodes byte for byte as before and nanocode's `Record::Model` (no `deny_unknown_fields`) still folds it. A replay whose site's mode differs from the record's is a divergence (`SiteLog::Reply` now holds the mode). New invariant `mode_sent`: each journaled reply must have answered a request sent in its record's mode; mutation `mode_dropped` sends the request without its mode and is caught at seed 0 on every target. The first version compared only the last request that returned the journaled reply; `scripts/check.sh` failed on `hl sim --target server-postgres --seeds 1056..1057 examples/claude_code.hl`, where after a lease takeover two requests at the same sequence number, one in `text_only` and one not, both returned the fieldless `context_full`. The invariant now accepts any request that returned the reply in the record's mode; `mode_dropped` sends none, so it is still caught.
- **Text (gap 11).** `contains`, `before`, `after` and `trim` (`ir::TextOp`, `Expr::TextBuiltin`): pure, total, and none counts or indexes characters, so Rust's bytes and JavaScript's UTF-16 units cannot disagree; `trim` strips ASCII whitespace only, since Rust's `char::is_whitespace` and JavaScript's `trim` differ on U+FEFF. The prover treats them as opaque text (a condition over them adds no fact), and they add no effect, so no worst case changes. A planted divergence in each backend's implementation (`rust text_before_whole`, `js text_after_kept`) is caught by `backend_equal` on `docs/snippets/text.hl`.
- **examples/claude_code.hl.** Bash, Read and Edit declare their optional parameters with the defaults the host used (`bash_timeout_ms` = 120,000, `read_offset_first` = 1, `read_limit_whole` = 0). Compaction asks first with the tools offered and, when the summary calls a tool, once more `mode text_only`, as nanocode's `force_text` retry does; the summary then goes through `summarized` and `summary_tagged`, which strip `<analysis>` and rewrite `<summary>` as `compact_summary` does, without its collapse of three or more newlines (no builtin loops over text). The two compaction checks at the top of a round became `if ... else if`: with the retry, two compactions per round put the worst case at 10,752 outbound requests, over Cloudflare's 10,000; a compaction resets `context_tokens`, so the second check never fired after the first anyway. Worst case 9,728 effects, 17,923 records and 9,728 outbound requests, as on master; prover steps 86,703 on master's file, 172,563 now (the retry path doubles the compaction paths).
- **agent/.** The host no longer fills defaults (`tools::with_defaults` and `optional_fields` are gone): the journal holds what the harness filled, for example `{"allow_large":false,"file_path":...,"limit":0,"offset":1,"pages":""}` for a Read the model sent with `file_path` alone. `mode text_only` becomes `tool_choice: {"type": "none"}`; an unknown mode is a failed model call naming it. Every request streams (`"stream": true`, `agent/src/stream.rs` assembles the server-sent events into the message the old path parsed), so no request meets the API's limit on non-streaming generations; curl's `--max-time 600` became `--speed-limit 1 --speed-time 600` (`STREAM_IDLE_SECONDS_MAX`), which ends only a stalled stream. The stream's bytes are written as they arrive to `state/stream-T-S.sse`, removed once the call returns; a run that finds one left by a crash logs `stream_discarded` with its size to the usage ledger and requests again, and nothing partial ever reaches the engine, whose `model` record is written only from a whole reply. The mock server streams its scripted replies, spreading them over its delay, and `scripts/agent-drill.sh` requires every cut stream's call to end with a journaled reply and no `.sse` file to survive the turn.

Measurements (5-core Hand shared with two other agents, rustc 1.100.0, release build, 2026-11-14):

- `hl sim --seeds 0..2000 examples/claude_code.hl`: native-jsonl 14,514 runs, 9,482 crashes, 11,775 dispatches; native-sqlite 13,009 runs; server-postgres 979 takeovers, 645 fenced; cloudflare 14,969 runs; vercel 16,208 runs, 2,959 unknown. `docs/snippets/text.hl`, `defaults.hl` and `modes.hl` pass seeds 0..200 on every target.
- Differential runs: `RUST_DIFF_FILES=examples/claude_code.hl RUST_DIFF_SEEDS=0..60 scripts/rust-diff.sh` and `JS_DIFF_FILES=examples/claude_code.hl JS_DIFF_SEEDS=0..60 scripts/js-diff.sh` match the interpreter on all five targets (server-postgres 5,762 trace events; vercel 1,117 steps). The three snippets pass seeds 0..100 in check.sh's plant runs unplanted.
- Plants: `scripts/mutations.sh --diff` catches rust default_unfilled, text_before_whole (backend_equal) and mode_dropped (the driver's mode assertion, panic), and js default_unfilled, mode_dropped and text_after_kept (backend_equal), in seeds 0..100.
- nanoeval at 556e56b, claude-haiku-4-5, each task in a scratch copy of its environment then its `tests/test.sh`: extract-todos reward 1 (5.6 s, 3 requests, 2 tool calls, 10,515 input and 290 output tokens), uppercase-message 1 (5.6 s, 3, 2, 10,415 and 290), write-greeting 1 (2.5 s, 2, 1, 6,792 and 152): the same requests and tool calls as the non-streaming host's 5.8, 5.4 and 3.3 s.
- `scripts/agent-drill.sh` (mock): 8 kills (5 during model calls), 4 streams cut and requested again, 7 external dispatches for 7 calls, 6 recovered unknown. `AGENT_DRILL_MODE=real scripts/agent-drill.sh` (claude-haiku-4-5): 12 kills, all during streams, 12 cut and requested again, 7 external dispatches for 7 calls, finished completed.
- `tool_choice: {"type": "none"}` with tools offered and a streamed request is accepted by the API (one request by hand, `stop_reason` end_turn, no tool use).
- `nice scripts/check.sh`: passes in 933 s on c033e1e (after the merge of master at 518b087), with the stages strict 734 s, sim 453 s wall (1,723 s summed), rust-diff 206 s and the agent build 48 s; the first run on the merge failed in the sim stage on the mode_sent case above.

Commands: `hl sim --target T --seeds 0..2000 examples/claude_code.hl`, `hl sim --target T --seeds 0..200 --twice --mutation default_unfilled|mode_dropped F`, `scripts/mutations.sh --diff BACKEND:PLANT:TARGET`, `scripts/rust-diff.sh`, `scripts/js-diff.sh`, `scripts/agent-drill.sh`, `AGENT_DRILL_MODE=real scripts/agent-drill.sh`, `scripts/docs-check.sh`, `scripts/check.sh`.

## 2026-11-14: a model's window, the attempt and stop builtins, text cuts, keep after, with and bounded waits; claude_code.hl owns compaction, cuts, stop recovery and backoff

What this establishes (branch gaps-1-4, from master 518b087, merged with master at c80d922), closing GAPS.md gaps 1 to 4:

- **Usage, stop and model errors (gaps 1, 3, 4).** `model name calls M [reply R] window W` makes every model call return the builtin sum `attempt = ok(reply, stop, u64, u64, u64, u64) | throttled(text, u64) | transient(text, u64) | rejected(text)`, registered after the builtin sums only for a program with a window, so no other program's sum ids, emitted code or quoted output move. `stop` is the builtin text enum `end | tool_use | max_tokens | refusal | context_exceeded | pause`; it rides on every reply, so a partial answer or partial calls come with `max_tokens` and `refusal`. Departure from GAPS.md's "error enum on a failure": the errors are flat variants of one sum beside `ok`, so one match handles reply and failure. The whole attempt is the unchanged `model` record. Each usage count is at most W: a count past it, a stop outside the enum or a reply that does not fit becomes `rejected("model reply: ...")` in the interpreter, the Rust runtime (`attempt_reply`, `usage_over`) and the JavaScript runtime (`attemptOutcome`, `usageOver`), and the prover binds the four usage fields of an `ok` arm at most W, so summing them is proved.
- **Text length and cuts (gap 1).** `len(t)` of a text is UTF-16 code units (`Expr::TextLen`, JavaScript's `.length`); `truncate_middle(t, n)`, `n` a constant, is nanocode's `truncate_middle` byte for byte (`ir::truncate_middle`, the Rust runtime's copy, JavaScript's `truncateMiddle` over code points). A cut is at most `4n + 39` units (one ellipsis, 20 digits, " tokens truncated…"), asserted where it is made; the prover gives a local bound to a cut (`Quantity::TextLen`) that bound, and `docs/snippets/recovery.hl` proves `pending_units <= calls_max * 100039` and is refused at 100038.
- **Summarizing compaction (gap 2).** Two primitives instead of GAPS.md's `compact list with model keeping last N`: `keep L last n after e` (`Kept::LastAfter`, the summary goes first, traps with `bound(L)` where it would not fit, proved as a keep then a push) and `context with e` as a model call's argument (`Expr::With`, an entry the list never holds; an error anywhere else). The summary is an ordinary model call, so throttling, refusals and calls in it are the harness's to handle, which the single construct would have hidden.
- **Bounded waits (gap 4).** `wait(e) at most c` (parsed into wait's second argument): the worst case counts `c`, the prover proves `e <= c` (new property `wait_bound`, `examples/invalid/wait_uncapped.hl`), every backend traps with `bound(c)` before the call record, and the journal still holds `{"ms":e,"until":T}`. `wait(c)` is `wait(c) at most c`.
- **Cloudflare waits are limited per wait, not per run.** The per-run sum refused any harness that backs off inside its rounds (claude_code.hl: 512 rounds x 60 s). A turn keeps its alarm armed from its first wait or external call until its status (`arm()` in `beforeExternal`, re-armed while busy), so an alarm run the 15-minute wall time cuts resumes from the alarm past every wait whose deadline it recorded; what must fit one alarm invocation is one wait. `WaitScope::Run` is gone; `over_wait.hl` is now accepted on Cloudflare and `long_wait.hl` is refused per wait there (check.sh, strict.sh and LANGUAGE.md follow).
- **Parallel arms may bind and assign.** The arms of a `for ... parallel` may now `let`, assign and `if` as well as push, with no effects and no event reads (checked), since they run in list order after the batch; claude_code.hl cuts each result and sums its length there.
- **Simulator faults.** For a windowed model: throttled `MODEL_THROTTLED_PPM` (100,000), transient `MODEL_TRANSIENT_PPM` (50,000), rejected `MODEL_REJECTED_PPM` (20,000), Retry-After absent half the time else up to `RETRY_AFTER_MS_MAX` (120 s, past claude_code.hl's 60 s guard), early stops `STOP_FAULT_PPM` (60,000), invalid stops at the text-enum rate, usage at or one past the window `USAGE_EDGE_PPM` (10,000) per count, extreme values in strict mode. Tool results long enough to be cut, mixing 1-, 2-, 3- and 4-byte characters, in `LONG_TEXT_PPM` (20,000) of a cutting program's text results. Draws happen only for programs that have a window or a cut, so every other program's seeds and quoted outputs are unchanged.
- **Mutations** (scripts/mutations.sh, docs/snippets/recovery.hl, all five targets): `truncate_uncut` (checkpoint_compacted, first at seed 39 on jsonl and cloudflare, 14 on sqlite, postgres and vercel), `usage_unchecked` (replay_equal, seed 3 on all), `retry_after_unjournaled` journals an attempt with Retry-After zeroed (mode_sent since the merge with master's modes, seed 4 on all; replay_equal before it). No new simulator invariant: the new properties are proof obligations (`wait_bound`, the cut bound, the usage bound) and their planted bugs are caught by existing invariants.
- **examples/claude_code.hl** now holds agent.rs's behaviour instead of agent/'s host: compaction when usage of the last reply plus a quarter of the tool results' units since reaches 200,000 - 33,000 tokens, or the context nears its capacity, or the provider reported `context_exceeded` (once per turn); the summary keeps the last round's entries after it and runs master's text cleanup and `text_only` retry; tool results cut head and tail at 12,000 tokens for `exec`/`wait` and 25,000 for the rest; `max_tokens` continues with nanocode's prompt at most 3 times (calls under `max_tokens` run and reset the count); one refusal retry keeping the finished text with `REFUSAL_RECOVERY`; backoff 500 ms doubling to 32 s, max(backoff, Retry-After), Retry-After over 60 s fails, 10 throttled attempts within 180 s, 11 for other transient errors, 3 for compaction. agent/ no longer cuts tool results or sleeps between attempts: `interpret`/`interpret_stream` map HTTP and SSE answers to attempts with Retry-After (`retry-after-ms` or `retry-after` seconds; curl's `dump-header` and the plain client's header), and the mock serves a scripted 429 with Retry-After and a `max_tokens` step, which scripts/agent-drill.sh now exercises.

Losses and departures, kept:

- No jitter in claude_code.hl's backoff: nanocode's is a deterministic remainder, and harnesslang has no `%`.
- nanocode retains the messages from the last assistant turn and summarizes without them; claude_code.hl keeps the last round's entries but the model sees them while summarizing (the kept-messages note says so).
- In claude_code.hl the per-round context reserve is `4 + calls` rather than `1 + calls`, and the length check before a round is written twice, because the prover does not track booleans across the `calls_run` and `compact_pending` flags.
- The truncation marker bound (39 units) is quoted by harnesses as a number (`100039`), not a builtin constant.
- `wait_uncapped` was tried as a planted bug and dropped: in a program the prover accepts the cap is never exceeded, so it plants nothing observable; the cap is covered by the checker (`wait_uncapped.hl`) and the differential runs.

Measurements (5-core Linux Hand shared with two other agents, `nice`):

- `hl sim --seeds 0..2000 docs/snippets/recovery.hl` passes on every target (native-jsonl: 5,032 turns, 13,253 runs, 8,221 crashes, 803 sleeps; server-postgres 860 takeovers) and `--strict` seeds 0..300 on every target.
- Generated Rust matches the interpreter on recovery.hl seeds 0..200 on the three Rust targets (native-jsonl 12,601 events, 63 sleeps), generated JavaScript on cloudflare and vercel (vercel 2,094 steps).
- `hl check examples/claude_code.hl`: 3 s, proved on all five targets (master's version 1 s); a first port with the compaction loop inlined three times ran the prover out of memory, and nested summary helpers ran out of its 4,000,000-step budget, so the port assigns the summary to a state field and compacts from one site.
- nanoeval 556e56b, claude-haiku-4-5, each task in a scratch copy of its environment then its `tests/test.sh`: extract-todos reward 1 (6.1 s, 3 requests, 2 tool calls, 10,535 input and 318 output tokens), uppercase-message 1 (5.7 s, 3, 2, 10,403 and 267), write-greeting 1 (3.8 s, 2, 1, 6,792 and 152).
- `scripts/agent-drill.sh` (mock, seed 1): 9 kills (6 in a model call, 3 in a tool), 7 external dispatches, none repeated, 4 recovered unknown, the scripted 429 with Retry-After 1 s honoured, the `max_tokens` step continued; `batch_flush_after_dispatch` is still caught (external calls dispatched more than once). A first version throttled every other step and its 1 s waits drew every kill into model calls, so the planted bug survived; the drill now throttles step 1 only.

Commands: `hl check docs/snippets/recovery.hl`, `hl sim --target T --seeds 0..2000 docs/snippets/recovery.hl`, `hl sim --target T --seeds 0..300 --strict docs/snippets/recovery.hl`, `MUTATIONS=M scripts/mutations.sh`, `RUST_DIFF_FILES=docs/snippets/recovery.hl RUST_DIFF_SEEDS=0..200 RUST_DIFF_OPT=0 scripts/rust-diff.sh`, `JS_DIFF_FILES=docs/snippets/recovery.hl JS_DIFF_SEEDS=0..200 scripts/js-diff.sh`, `hl-agent run --instruction tasks/T/instruction.md --cwd SCRATCH --model claude-haiku-4-5 --journal J` then `tests/test.sh`, `scripts/agent-drill.sh`, `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh`.

## 2026-11-14: listing notices, tool search and blobs; claude_code.hl reads images, evicts them as nanocode does, rereads its MCP listing each round and finds deferred tools

What this establishes (branch gaps-6-8, from master 8549e8a), closing GAPS.md gap 6 and the images and structured results of gap 8:

- **Listing notices (gap 6).** `name(context)` reads a listing and, when its fingerprints differ from the turn's previous successful read, pushes the builtin entry `notice(text)` ("tools of listing mcp changed: added ...; changed ...; removed ..." in sorted order). The checker lowers it to the listing effect then `if ListingNotice(l) != "" { push context notice(ListingNotice(l)) }` (`ir::Expr::ListingNotice`), so the prover counts the push like any other and no new proof is needed. The first read of a turn and a failed read notice nothing: nanocode compares against the cached prefix across turns, harnesslang only within a turn, since admission is per turn. The `notice` variant is appended to `entry` in every program with a model and a listing (only codex_agent.hl among the examples; no quoted output moved).
- **Tool search (gap 6).** `tool T(...) -> text effect none|idempotent finds L`. A search's ok text is names, one per line, each `L__NAME` listed by the turn's latest read of `L`, at most `L`'s bound; otherwise the result is recorded as the call's failure (`value::checked_search`). A listing some tool finds is deferred: `dispatch` refuses its tools until a search of the turn found them (`not_discovered`), the discovered set rebuilt from journaled search results on replay. Departures: names are text lines, not nanocode's `tool_reference` blocks; an external search is refused by the checker (a search changes nothing, and the unknown-outcome check of external calls compares host outputs that a search check may turn into failures).
- **Blobs (gap 8).** `blob<N>`, `N` named bytes. A tool returns `blob<N>` or `text or blob<N>` (dispatch only, as nanocode's `Read` returns text or an image); every blob tool shares one bound. A host returns `{"data": base64}`; non-base64 data or more than `N` bytes is the call's failure. The result record holds `{"blob": sha256, "data": base64}` the first time the session journals those bytes and `{"blob": sha256}` after; values everywhere else (state, checkpoints, model contexts) are `{"blob": sha256}`. Departure from GAPS.md's "journaled by digest" as a separate store: the bytes ride in the first result that holds them, so no target gained a record kind or a table; a load reads every blob-capable result, refuses data whose digest differs and a state holding a blob no record holds. SHA-256 and base64 are written once per backend (`src/run/blob.rs`, the Rust runtime's `blob.rs`, `src/emit/js/blob.js`), each checked against the "abc" vector at start.
- **dispatch in a blob program** returns the builtin sum `dispatched = ok(text) | failed(text) | unknown | blob(blob<N>)` (ids of ok, failed and unknown unchanged) instead of `maybe<text>`, so a match must take the blob; `entry` gains `attached(call, blob<N>)`. `Type::reports_unknown` replaces the `Type::Maybe` tests where unknown outcomes are decided.
- **keep L blobs last N** (`ir::Stmt::KeepBlobs`) turns the oldest attached entries into `result(call, "[image omitted from this request, which keeps only the newest N images: sha256:...]")`, nanocode's `ImageWindow::evicted` batches (half of N, rounded up) and `omitted` text. It is destructive where nanocode rewrites only the request; the list's length does not change, so the prover needs nothing new.
- **Worst case and targets.** The worst case gains a fifth measure, blob bytes journaled (printed only when nonzero, so no quoted output moved; images.hl 33,554,432 = 8 rounds x 4 calls x 1 MiB). `Limits.record_bytes`: Cloudflare 2,000,000 bytes per row (SQLite-backed Durable Objects), SQLite 1,000,000,000 (SQLITE_MAX_LENGTH) and Postgres 1 GiB per field; a blob tool whose result record (base64 of N plus the record around it, computed by encoding the largest record) exceeds it is refused (`examples/invalid/blob_row.hl`: 2 MiB is 2,796,399 bytes on Cloudflare). The line is printed only for programs with blobs.
- **native-jsonl** opens the whole journal for a program with blobs (no checkpoint tail), so the blob table and the first-record-holds-data rule see every record: an O(journal) open for such programs.
- **Simulator.** Blob tools answer short repeating byte strings (`BLOB_SHORT_BYTES_MAX` = 4, so digests repeat and dedupe runs), whole blobs at the bound `BLOB_WHOLE_PPM` = 10,000, one byte over `BLOB_OVER_PPM` = 20,000, not base64 `BLOB_INVALID_PPM` = 50,000; `text or blob` tools answer a blob in `BLOB_ATTACHED_PPM` = 250,000; searches up to `FOUND_TOOLS_MAX` = 3 names, `UNLISTED_TOOL_PPM` of them unlisted. Draws happen only for programs with these tools, and drawn states skip variants holding blobs, so other programs' seeds are unchanged.
- **New invariants and mutations** (scripts/mutations.sh, all five targets, seeds 0..200): `blob_journaled` (data hashes to its digest, a digest-only record follows one with data, checkpoints' blobs are held) catches `blob_unjournaled` at seed 0 everywhere; `search_listed` catches `search_unchecked` (seeds 0 to 5); `tool_admitted` now also requires a deferred tool to have been found and catches `discovery_unchecked` (seeds 0 to 2); `notice_dropped` and `blobs_unevicted` (live runs only) are caught by `checkpoint_compacted` (seeds 0 to 10). Differential plants `rust blob_unjournaled` and `js blob_unjournaled` (backend_equal, every Rust and JavaScript target).
- **examples/claude_code.hl** declares `Read -> text or blob<image_bytes_max>`, `ToolSearch(query, max_results = 5) finds mcp` (nanocode's default) and `tools from listing(mcp, mcp_tools_max)`, reads `mcp(context)` before every request as `refresh_dynamic_tools` does, pushes `attached` for image results and runs `keep context blobs last images_kept` (100, `ImageWindow::CLAUDE`) every round; the per-round context reserve is 5 + calls for the notice. `image_bytes_max` = 1 MiB: nanocode's `MAX_IMAGE_BYTES` is 5 MiB, but a 5 MiB result does not fit a Cloudflare row, and 1 MiB is the largest whole MiB that does.
- **agent/** (hl-agent): Read returns png, jpeg, gif and webp files (by signature) as blobs up to the program's bound, with nanocode's "image content omitted because ..." texts past it or for unreadable data; the context renders `attached` as a tool_result holding a base64 image block from the model request's blob table and `notice` as user text; the listing answers `{}` (hl-agent has no MCP servers) and ToolSearch is not offered. scripts/agent-drill.sh's mock run now reads a PNG and requires exactly one journaled blob result holding its data.

Losses, kept:

- Prompt cache lifetime tracking (`agent/keep.rs`) is not written; it stays in GAPS.md as gap 8.
- hl-agent does not resize images, so images over 1 MiB fail where nanocode would downscale them; PDFs are still refused.
- Notices do not carry tool definitions, as nanocode's `tool_addition` blocks do; a host renders the text.
- The model request carries the session's whole blob table on every call.

Measurements (5-core Linux Hand shared with two other agents, `nice`, 2 check jobs):

- `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh` on 4772275: ok in 1,091 s (strict 350 s, sim 406 s wall / 726 s busy, js-diff 234 s, rust-diff 152 s, diff-plants 129 s, agent 26 s).
- `hl sim --seeds 0..2000`: claude_code.hl native-jsonl 18,936 runs, 13,904 crashes, 12,540 dispatches; server-postgres 1,168 takeovers, 707 fenced; cloudflare 19,414 runs; vercel 18,815 runs. catalog.hl native-jsonl 17,886 runs, 12,854 crashes; images.hl native-jsonl 17,671 runs, 12,639 crashes, 20,149 dispatches, on every target.
- Differential runs in check.sh: claude_code.hl Rust seeds 0..300 (native-jsonl 37,360 events, server-postgres 171 owners) and JavaScript seeds 0..100 (vercel 2,022 steps) match the interpreter; catalog.hl and images.hl seeds 0..200 on all five targets (the backend lanes' runs).
- `hl check examples/claude_code.hl`: proved on all five targets, 1,729,587 prover steps (the master version, with this compiler, 1,561,172).
- nanoeval 556e56b, claude-haiku-4-5, each task in a scratch copy of its environment then `tests/test.sh`: extract-todos reward 1 (6.5 s, 3 requests, 2 tool calls, 10,529 input and 309 output tokens), uppercase-message 1 (5.6 s, 3, 2, 10,432 and 282), write-greeting 1 (3.7 s, 2, 1, 6,800 and 154). A 16x16 red PNG read by hand: the model wrote "red" (3 requests, 10,310 input tokens), the journal holding one blob with its data.
- `scripts/agent-drill.sh` (mock, seed 1): 11 kills (8 in a model call, 3 in a tool), 7 external dispatches, none repeated, 1 blob journaled with its data; `batch_flush_after_dispatch` still caught.

Commands: `hl sim --target T --seeds 0..2000 F`, `MUTATIONS=M scripts/mutations.sh`, `scripts/mutations.sh --diff BACKEND:blob_unjournaled:TARGET`, `RUST_DIFF_FILES=F RUST_DIFF_SEEDS=0..200 scripts/rust-diff.sh`, `JS_DIFF_FILES=F JS_DIFF_SEEDS=0..200 scripts/js-diff.sh`, `hl-agent run --instruction tasks/T/instruction.md --cwd SCRATCH --model claude-haiku-4-5 --journal J` then `tests/test.sh`, `AGENT_DRILL_SEED=1 scripts/agent-drill.sh`, `scripts/docs-check.sh`, `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh`.

## 2026-11-14: hl-agent against nanocode's own Claude agent on 20 tasks, same model, same containers

`evals/` runs a suite of nanoeval-format tasks (`instruction.md`, `environment/`, `tests/test.sh`, unchanged) with two agents and compares them. It is outside the compiler build (Python standard library and shell, like `oracle/` is outside it).

- **Why not nanoeval itself.** nanoeval at 556e56b runs its own harness (nanocode-ts in a Cloudflare Worker) and, as installed agents, only Codex CLI and Claude Code; neither nanocode's Rust CLI nor hl-agent is an agent it can run, and a run needs a Worker. The runner here keeps nanoeval's task format, its verifier invocation (`/tests/test.sh` under bash or sh in the task's working directory, `/logs/verifier/reward.txt`) and its outcome rule (reward 1 passes; no reward, or no model request that succeeded, is an error and not a score; an attempt past its agent timeout is still verified, as nanoeval's `agent_timeout` is).
- **Fairness.** Each attempt runs in a fresh container of the task's image (`docker_image` from `task.toml`, else its Dockerfile). Both agents run inside it, as the image's default user, with the task's working directory and agent timeout. The binaries are bind-mounted from `output/eval/agent-root` with their ELF interpreter and RUNPATH pointed at a copy of the host's glibc (patchelf 0.18.0), so one glibc build runs in Alpine and Debian images alike and nothing leaks into the processes the agents start. Neither agent holds the API key: both send plain HTTP to `evals/meter.py` on the docker bridge, which adds the key, forwards to api.anthropic.com and records every request (parameters, status, usage, tool_use blocks, the last request body and each assembled response), so tokens, requests and cost come from one meter for both. nanocode is `nanocode exec --model M --no-tempo-tools` at c7679e3 (`NANOCODE_ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`), hl-agent is `hl-agent run` (`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`).
- **What the meter showed nanocode sends** for claude-sonnet-4-6: `stream: true`, `max_tokens` 128,000, `thinking` adaptive with summarized display, `output_config.effort` high, tools `exec` and `wait` (Code Mode), a 6,396-character system prompt. hl-agent was changed on its branch to send the same thinking, effort and `max_tokens` (f900296) before the measured run; it sends Read, Edit, Write and Bash directly and did not stream.
- **Suite** (`evals/suite.txt`): nanoeval's three local tasks and 17 Terminal-Bench 2.1 tasks fetched from the Harbor registry by `evals/fetch.py`, which checks the dataset's task digest (`a522795…`) and each package's checksum the way nanoeval's adapter does. The 17 were chosen before any run: 900 s or shorter agent timeouts where possible, one CPU, at most 4 GiB, published images, easy to hard.

Measured on the hosted Hand (Linux 6.18.56 x86_64, 5 cores, 14 GB, shared with two other agents), claude-sonnet-4-6, 3 attempts per task and agent, prices from Anthropic's pricing page read 2026-11-14 ($3 input, $15 output, $3.75 cache write, $0.30 cache read per million tokens). hl-agent is the binary built at f900296 (sha256 of the unpatched binary 48faf7b3…), the Bash fix below excepted.

| Agent | Attempts | Passed | Failed | Errored | Pass rate (95% Wilson) | Cost | Cost/attempt | Wall/attempt | Requests/attempt | Model tool calls/attempt | Inner tool calls/attempt | Prompt tokens/attempt | Output tokens/attempt |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| nanocode | 60 | 52 | 8 | 0 | 86.7% (75.8-93.1%) | $12.15 | $0.202 | 168 s | 8.6 | 7.4 | 13.3 | 212,007 | 6,599 |
| hl-agent | 60 | 52 | 7 | 1 | 88.1% (77.5-94.1%) | $13.79 | $0.230 | 272 s | 10.6 | 11.3 | 12.2 | 286,705 | 6,714 |

Prompt tokens are input plus cache reads plus cache writes. Model tool calls are tool_use blocks in responses (for nanocode mostly `exec` cells); inner tool calls are nanocode's `· Tool` lines on stderr other than exec and wait, and hl-agent's own count.

| Task | nanocode pass | $ | s | req | hl-agent pass | $ | s | req |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |

| uppercase-message | 3/3 | 0.028 | 9 | 3.0 | 3/3 | 0.012 | 9 | 3.0 |
| write-greeting | 3/3 | 0.012 | 6 | 2.0 | 3/3 | 0.007 | 5 | 2.0 |
| build-cython-ext | 3/3 | 1.076 | 399 | 53.0 | 3/3 | 1.839 | 464 | 68.7 |
| cancel-async-tasks | 0/3 | 0.102 | 72 | 4.3 | 0/3 | 0.075 | 65 | 3.0 |
| cobol-modernization | 2/3 | 0.398 | 549 | 11.3 | 3/3 | 0.541 | 451 | 21.0 |
| fix-git | 2/3 | 0.058 | 36 | 6.0 | 3/3 | 0.071 | 51 | 9.7 |
| git-leak-recovery | 3/3 | 0.057 | 36 | 5.0 | 3/3 | 0.052 | 40 | 8.7 |
| kv-store-grpc | 3/3 | 0.062 | 44 | 5.3 | 3/3 | 0.034 | 910 | 3.7 |
| largest-eigenval | 0/3 | 0.525 | 910 | 15.3 | 0/3 | 0.294 | 910 | 8.7 |
| log-summary-date-ranges | 3/3 | 0.071 | 36 | 4.3 | 3/3 | 0.064 | 39 | 6.0 |

| openssl-selfsigned-cert | 3/3 | 0.066 | 41 | 4.0 | 3/3 | 0.083 | 62 | 9.7 |
