The pass rates are not distinguishable: the intervals overlap almost entirely, and the per-task differences (cobol-modernization and fix-git one attempt better for hl-agent, polyglot-c-py one worse) are single attempts. Both fail cancel-async-tasks and largest-eigenval every time.

Where hl-agent lost, read from both transcripts (`last-request.json` and `responses.jsonl` per row):

1. **Bash hung on a backgrounded server** (kv-store-grpc and pypi-server, all six attempts ran to the 900 s agent timeout; the verifier passed them because the server the agent started was up). The model ran `pypi-server run -p 8080 … &` and the tool waited for EOF on the pipe the server inherited; the per-call `timeout` (120,000 ms) was not enforced. Fixed on the real-agent branch in 0ad1c37 (wait for the shell, output to files, kill the group only at the timeout). The same three tasks with that binary, 3 attempts each (`output/eval/fix`): 9 of 9 passed, 60 s and $0.073 per attempt (kv-store-grpc 58 s, pypi-server 65 s, openssl 58 s against nanocode's 44, 55 and 41 s).
2. **Context and cost.** build-cython-ext costs $1.84 per attempt against $1.08: whole-file Read results (pyknotid's `invariants.py` 62,365 characters, `spacecurve.py` 50,797) stay in the conversation and are read from cache by every later request (attempt 1: 5.46 M cache-read tokens against nanocode's 2.36 M; tool results 259 k characters against 98 k, nanocode's largest 9.9 k, since a Code Mode cell returns only what the model prints). Across the suite hl-agent reads 35% more prompt tokens per attempt. The fix is Code Mode for the Claude harness, or Read and Bash outputs bounded the way Claude Code bounds them.
3. **Non-streamed requests and dropped connections.** This Hand's egress dropped some long responses for both agents (nanocode 4 rows, hl-agent 8 rows had a request end without a response). A dropped non-streamed request cost hl-agent the whole wait: polyglot-c-py attempt 2 sent three requests that ended after 796, 189 and 167 s with no response and the attempt errored. master's hl-agent now streams; this run predates it.
4. **polyglot-c-py attempt 3** failed on the model's answer, not the harness: the image has no python3, so neither agent could run the Python half; hl-agent's file left the C part inside a `"""` string that Python's tokenizer rejects, which nanocode's three answers did not. One attempt; no harness cause found.

Losses and limits, kept:

- Docker on this Hand cannot apply cgroup limits (`--cpus` and `--memory` fail: the docker cgroup is threaded), so attempts ran without the tasks' CPU and memory limits (`--limits none`). `--limits task` applies them where Docker can.
- Containers have no direct route out; the runner relays the host's egress proxy onto the docker bridge (`evals/relay.py`) and sets `http_proxy`, `https_proxy` and `no_proxy` in each container for agents and verifiers alike. That is host policy visible to both agents.
- The agents run inside the task's container, not outside it as nanoeval's harness does, so an agent's own processes share the task's environment.
- The first runs of kv-store-grpc, openssl-selfsigned-cert and pypi-server attempt 3 errored because two runners named their containers alike and removed each other's (`No such container`). Container names now include a digest of the run directory; those six rows (three per arm) were moved to `output/eval/collided` and run again, and the tables above hold the reruns.
- The nanocode and hl-agent arms ran at the same time on separate runners, attempts in order, not interleaved row by row; the meter shows the same upstream drops in both.
- Spend: $25.94 for the two measured arms, $0.66 for the Bash-fix arm, a few cents of claude-haiku-4-5 while developing.

Commands: `NANOCODE_DIR=<nanocode checkout> HL_AGENT=<hl-agent binary> sh evals/setup.sh`, then `python3 evals/run.py --suite evals/suite.txt --tasks output/eval/tasks --agent-root output/eval/agent-root --out output/eval/main --agents nanocode,hl-agent --attempts 3 --model claude-sonnet-4-6 --parallel 3 --limits none` and `python3 evals/report.py output/eval/main`. A second runner at once needs its own `--meter-port` and `--relay-port`.

## 2026-11-14: before tool, after tool and lifecycle hooks; spawn and join

GAPS.md's gaps 5 and 7 are closed and removed (branch gaps-5-7, from master 21d98c9, merged with master at 8549e8a, 7fa419b and 6dea898).

What this establishes:

- **Tool hooks.** `before tool(c: call)` and `after tool(c: call, outcome: maybe<text>)` are inlined by the checker at every `match dispatch(...)`, nanocode's `hooked_handler` (`agent.rs:562-621`). `deny(reason)` in `before tool` is a new site kind, `SiteKind::Deny`, class `none`: it appends a `call` record with tool `deny` and input `{"id","input","reason","tool"}` and the result `failed(reason)`, never contacting the host, and the match takes its `failed` arm. `allow(input)` dispatches the call with a rewritten input text, which the typed input gate checks as it checks the model's. `deny` in `after tool` gives the match `failed(reason)` for an outcome the tool already returned. No new record kind, so the SQL kinds and the oracle are untouched.
- **Lowering.** A first version duplicated the match's arms into every path of the hook (continuation passing); `examples/hooks.hl`'s `before tool` then made `hl check` run past 300 s in the prover. The checker now lowers a decision to four hidden state fields, `_hook_decided`, `_hook_denied`, `_hook_reason` and `_hook_input`: the hook's code appears once per dispatch, statements after an `if` or `match` that may decide run under `if not _hook_decided`, and the arms appear at most three times (denied, withheld, ran). `hl check examples/hooks.hl` takes under a second. A `deny` or `allow` inside a loop, a dispatch inside a hook, a dispatch that is not a match subject while a dispatch hook exists, a parallel for with one, and a hook that runs nowhere are refused.
- **Lifecycle hooks.** `on session start` (guarded by `_session_started`), `on prompt submit(p: T)` (in each handler taking `p: T`), `on pre compact` and `on post compact` (around every `keep` or `clear` of a list of entries), `on stop` (before every `return`), `on subagent start(agent)` (in the ok arm of a spawn, direct or dispatched), `on subagent stop(agent, result)` (after every `join`) and `on session end(...) -> R`, a handler `session_end` that sets `_session_ended`, which every handler's `requires` then tests. These are nanocode's `SessionStart`, `UserPromptSubmit`, `PreCompact`, `PostCompact`, `Stop`, `SubagentStart`, `SubagentStop` and `SessionEnd` (`hooks.rs:49-151`). Names starting with `_` are now refused as references too, so no program reads the hidden fields.
- **Prover.** `assume` splits a conjunction that holds a part it cannot reason about (a `bool` field such as `not _session_ended`) and keeps the linear parts as facts; before, the whole `requires` was dropped. Existing outputs change only in prover steps (`examples/reviewer.hl`: 71,524 to 71,549).
- **Spawn.** `spawn name runs handler from "file"` declares an external tool whose parameters are the child handler's (`u64`, `bool`, `text`); `check::check_with` takes a loader and `hl` checks the child beside the parent. The runtime records the spawn's `ok` result as the child's key, `T.S` from the call's turn and sequence number, whatever the host answered (interpreter, generated Rust `spawn_output` with `program::spawns`, generated JavaScript `spawnOutput` with `spawn: true` on the tool literal), so a replay names the same child. `join(key)` is a `suspend` keyed by the child: the parent's run ends there and the child's completion resumes it with the JSON of the child's result. A spawned harness may not spawn, so a parent's worst case holds every session it can start.
- **Worst case.** A fifth measure, child sessions (direct spawn sites, and every dispatch when the harness declares a spawn tool), multiplied by loops; `hl check` prints it with each child's worst case and the family total when nonzero, so existing quoted output does not move. The prover counts spawn-capable runs on the plan, checks them against the IR's count and checks each child's outbound requests against the target's per-invocation limit.
- **Simulator.** The world is the host: an accepted spawn runs the child as its own session (`sim::run_child`) on the same target with a seed derived from the parent's, with its own faults, crashes, power losses and takeovers, and every invariant on its journal; a child violation fails the parent's seed. A join on a child's key is resumed with that child's result. `CHILD_SESSIONS_PER_SEED_MAX` = 16 (nanocode's `DEFAULT_MAX_RESIDENT_SUBAGENTS`) bounds children per seed, refusing more with a failure, and `CHILD_NAMED_PPM` = 500,000 makes the model name a started child in half its text inputs. The liveness bound on suspensions per turn now adds the turn's unmatched resumes, since a wrong key legitimately suspends again (`docs/snippets/spawn.hl`, two effects, failed seed 0 before).
- **Invariants and mutations.** `denied_unrun`: no tool request reaches the host between a deny site's run points (`deny_dispatches`, a runtime that runs the denied call anyway, caught at seed 0 on four targets and seed 2 on vercel). `child_scoped`: every recorded spawn key is its call's `T.S` and names a child the host started (`spawn_key_unscoped`, keyed by sequence number alone, caught at seed 0 on every target). The first `denied_unrun` compared the journal with every dispatch at the same id and failed unmutated seeds, where a run lost to a crash had dispatched a different call at that sequence number; it now watches the deny site's own run points, which a second Postgres owner's run (inside a hook) must not inherit.
- **Examples.** `examples/hooks.hl` ports `hooked_handler` (permission modes, `"ask"` through `suspend`, a PreToolUse hook that allows, updates or denies, a PostToolUse failure that withholds the outcome with nanocode's notice) and the lifecycle hooks through a `lifecycle_hook` tool. `examples/subagents.hl` spawns `examples/subagent_child.hl` with `spawn_agent`, joins the child `wait_agent` names, and keeps its roster in `on subagent start` and `on subagent stop`; it now runs 32 rounds of 8 calls (64 of 16 before), since every join ends a run and the strict sweep's greedy model made `hl sim --strict --seeds 0..6` take 631 s for one target, against 46 s now and 60 s for master's file. `wait_unjournaled` and `wait_rerun_skips` moved their plans to `docs/snippets/park.hl`, since subagents.hl no longer waits.

Losses, kept:

- Side questions answered from a snapshot are not written (GAPS.md's earlier 7 named them "if it fits"); nor subagent trees deeper than one level. Both are in ARCHITECTURE.md's Next.
- Text has no escapes, so a hook that rewrites JSON input takes its quote character from the input (`docs/snippets/hooks.hl`).
- `on stop` cannot block a stop: a `return` has no loop to continue, so `examples/hooks.hl` keeps nanocode's blocking Stop hook in its loop and uses `on stop` for the notification.
- A spawned child's session is the host's: the generated runtimes record the key and nothing more, and `hl-agent` does not start children.

Measurements (5-core Hand shared with two other agents, `nice`, `CHECK_JOBS=2`):

- `hl sim --seeds 0..2000` (check.sh): hooks.hl on every target, e.g. native-jsonl 19,028 runs, 11,212 crashes, 2,784 suspensions; subagents.hl native-jsonl 68,292 runs, 6,217 children, 1,401 joins resumed with a child's result, 31,935 crashes inside children; server-postgres 3,475 takeovers, 6,965 children, 18,293 child crashes; vercel 7,186 children, 30,536 child crashes.
- `docs/snippets/spawn.hl` seeds 0..200: native-jsonl 335 children, 271 joined, 353 child crashes; server-postgres 345 children, 218 child crashes.
- Differential runs match the interpreter on hooks.hl and subagents.hl: Rust seeds 0..300 on the three Rust targets (subagents native-jsonl 4,739 runs, 6,461 flushes), JavaScript seeds 0..100 on cloudflare and vercel (subagents vercel 3,930 steps, 254 resumes).
- `scripts/check.sh` on the merge (96df1dc) ran 906 s before failing in diff-plants: the `enum_unchecked` plant's anchored `sed` no longer matched the line `spawn_output` had wrapped; the checked output keeps its own line now.

Commands: `hl check examples/hooks.hl`, `hl check --target cloudflare docs/snippets/spawn.hl`, `hl sim --target T --seeds 0..200 [--mutation deny_dispatches|spawn_key_unscoped] docs/snippets/{hooks,spawn}.hl`, `hl sim --target native-jsonl --seeds 0..6 --strict examples/subagents.hl`, `RUST_DIFF_FILES=examples/subagents.hl scripts/rust-diff.sh`, `JS_DIFF_FILES=examples/hooks.hl scripts/js-diff.sh`, `scripts/mutations.sh --census`, `scripts/docs-check.sh`, `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh`.

## 2026-11-14: spawn trees deeper than one level, and side questions answered from the last finished turn

What this establishes (branch cc-tree, from master eae1320; side questions written on cc-tree-side and merged at c30aede):

- **Spawn trees.** A spawned harness may declare spawn tools of its own. A spawn whose child spawns must say how many sessions one call starts, the child's and every descendant's: `spawn name runs handler from "file" sessions at most c` (`ast::Spawn.sessions`, `ir::Spawn.sessions`; `sessions` is a new contextual word). Without it the checker refuses the spawn and names the clause; with it, a tree past `c` is refused (`examples/invalid/family_sessions.hl`: 3 sessions per call over `two_sessions` (2)). This is caller policy, as AGENTS.md allows: nanocode has no total either (`DEFAULT_MAX_SUBAGENTS` is `usize::MAX`, `crates/runtime/subagents/src/lib.rs:41`).
- **Cycles and depth.** `main.rs`'s loader keeps the chain of files it is checking, by canonical path, and refuses a file that is already on it ("the spawn tree returns to a harness it is inside", `docs/snippets/errors/spawn-cycle.hl`). It also refuses a chain deeper than `ir::SPAWN_DEPTH_MAX` = 16, as does the checker from a child's family depth. TigerStyle asks for an explicit bound on a walk over input. nanocode bounds no depth, since `durable.rs:2805` counts up to the agent count, but keeps `DEFAULT_MAX_RESIDENT_SUBAGENTS` = 16 resident per root session's tree (`lib.rs:45`, `runtime.rs:6392`). A chain deeper than 16 cannot be resident at once there.
- **Worst case.** `worst::family_with` builds the family from the bottom up. Each spawn tool's `Spawn::tree` is its child's own family, its totals and `1 + sessions`, or the child alone. The handler's family is its own measures plus the spawns on its longest path times the widest tree, and its `sessions` and `depth` as well. `hl check` prints each child line with its tree's totals and "N sessions with its descendants (sessions at most ...)". A tree deeper than one level also gets "sessions in its tree: N, D levels below it". One-level output is unchanged byte for byte (every example's and snippet's `hl check` output against master's binary).
- **Keys are scoped.** A session spawned under key `K` records its children as `K/T.S` (`ir::scoped_spawn_key`, `SPAWN_SCOPE_SEPARATOR` "/"). A root's scope is empty, so its keys stay `T.S` and no existing journal moves. The scope comes from the host:
  - the interpreter's `World::scope` (default empty);
  - generated Rust `World::scope`/`Host::scope`, forwarded by `JsonlWorld` and `SqlWorld`;
  - the turn request's optional `scope` field in generated JavaScript. Cloudflare's `POST /turn` and Vercel's request text carry it; an absent field is a root.
- **Prover.** `prove::children` now lowers every descendant's handler for the same target and recomputes the family from the plans. It takes records, outbound requests and sessions on each plan's longest path, times the spawns on the parent's plan. It asserts the result is at most what the IR counts. It checks each descendant's own outbound requests against the target's per-invocation limit and each spawn's tree against its `sessions at most`. A failure gives the path of spawns, such as `top.go > middle runs middle.work > heavy runs over_cloudflare.prompt (step below ...)`. Effects are not recomputed from plans: a cell's effects are not one op each. A tree deeper than one level gets a second proved line.
- **Simulator.**
  - A grandchild runs as a child does: `run_child` takes a `Placement` with target, mutation, seed, scope and the family's sessions left. Each level gets its seed derived from its parent's, its own faults and every invariant, and its violations propagate as "child session K: ...".
  - `CHILD_SESSIONS_PER_SEED_MAX` (16) now bounds the whole tree per seed, as nanocode's resident limit is per root session. The parent subtracts what each child's subtree started (`SimWorld.family_left`, deterministic and clone-safe). `Child` records its turn, tool and subtree sessions.
  - `children` counts every session of the tree. `child_crashes` counts each descendant crash once, where adding a grandchild's stats would otherwise have counted it twice. One-level programs' seeds and outputs are unchanged (subagents.hl, spawn.hl, hooks.hl and agent.hl, seeds 0..150, on all five targets, against master).
- **Invariants and mutations** (38 invariants):
  - `child_scoped` now requires `scope/T.S` at every level. `grandchild_unscoped`, a runtime that keys a child by `T.S` without its session's scope, is caught on all five targets.
  - New `family_bounded`: the sessions a turn's tree starts stay within its handler's family worst case, and each spawn's subtree within its `sessions at most`. `family_one_level`, a worst case that counts only the direct children (`worst::Levels::One`), is caught on all five targets.
- **hl sim --scope K** runs a harness as if spawned under `K`. The trace's seed event carries `scope`, the Rust and JavaScript differential drivers pass it on, and `RUST_DIFF_SCOPE`/`JS_DIFF_SCOPE` select it. `scripts/check.sh` runs `docs/snippets/tree_planner.hl` under `1.1` on every target. The differential plans take an optional scope field: `rust grandchild_unscoped` and `js grandchild_unscoped` (generated code that drops the scope) are caught by `backend_equal` on every Rust and JavaScript target.
- **Side questions.** `on side name(params) -> T [requires c] { ... }` declares a side handler (`Program::sides`, kept apart from `handlers`, so nothing that admits turns, lowers plans or picks a sim handler sees one).
  - The checker refuses, at the statement and through inlined fns: assignment, push, clear, keep, keep blobs and parallel for. It refuses idempotent, external, spawn and code tools, dispatch, listing reads, wait, suspend, join, now, random, `cancelled` and `take`.
  - It inlines no hook, and requires is evaluated on the snapshot. An ended session answers no side question, like nanocode's `AgentStopped`.
  - `hl check` prints "side handler NAME: worst case N effects, 0 journal records, M outbound requests". Cloudflare's per-invocation limit applies. finish_once and the arithmetic proofs cover sides; the durability plans do not.
- **Side snapshot and journaling.** A side question runs over the state after the latest finished turn (`state_before(last + 1)`), the state a running or parked turn started from. It is a scratch run: a fresh engine over a copy of that state, its flushes discarded, its model and tool requests sent with turn 0, which no turn has. It appends nothing anywhere, writes no checkpoint, takes no lease and moves no sequence number. Side answers are not journaled, as nanocode persists none; a crash loses the question.
- **Side paths per backend.**
  - Interpreter: `run::side::answer` between runs and while parked. Mid-turn, `World::side` is polled before every fresh effect.
  - Generated Rust: `JsonlJournal::side` and `sql_side`; the latter uses the new `sql_load`, LOAD without ACQUIRE.
  - Cloudflare: `POST /side`, also routed as `/sessions/ID/side`. It is not serialized behind a running turn and reads only the object's rows, never the turn's memory.
  - Vercel: `harness.side(lines, request, host)` and `harnessSide`, outside any step.
- **Side simulation.** Only programs with sides draw, so no other seed moves. The draws are `SIDE_AT_EFFECT_PPM` = 100,000 before fresh effects and `SIDE_BETWEEN_TURNS_PPM` and `SIDE_PARKED_PPM` = 500,000, from rngs keyed "side*". New invariant `side_isolated` requires two things:
  - the medium and the engine's buffered records are unchanged by each question;
  - the answer equals an oracle `side::run` over the durable journal's finished turns before the asking turn, with the same requests (seq, mode, context) and replies.

  `side_live_state` and `side_journaled` are caught on all five targets. The differential drivers carry side questions, and generated Rust and JavaScript answer as the interpreter does.
- **Docs.** LANGUAGE.md has "Spawn trees" at the end of Spawn and join, with `docs/snippets/tree.hl` → `tree_planner.hl` → `spawn_child.hl`, three levels. It has a new "Side questions" section with `docs/snippets/side.hl`, using nanocode's `SIDE_QUESTION_REMINDER` verbatim through `with`. The grammar and Contextual words gain `sessions` and `side`. ARCHITECTURE.md drops both items from Next.

Departures from nanocode and losses, kept:

- **Sessions bound.** `sessions at most` is a bound nanocode does not have. nanocode only evicts past 16 resident subagents.
- **Mid-turn snapshot.** Mid-turn, nanocode's side question sees the live transcript; harnesslang's sees the last finished turn. Mid-turn state rests on records that may not be durable, so a crash can roll it back. A Durable Object or a Workflow step cannot read another run's memory. The snapshot is the same on every target.
- **Postgres takeover.** An owner whose lease was taken answers from the turn it started until its next flush is fenced.
- **Vercel journal lines.** `harnessSide` needs the run's journal lines, which the host obtains itself.
- **Mid-turn polling.** On Cloudflare and Vercel, mid-turn polling is a simulation hook only. Real hosts ask through `POST /side` or `harnessSide`.
- **Cloudflare status rows.** A `POST /side` that interleaves with a finishing turn may read its uncommitted status row. The output gate holds the response until the commit, which rests on the existing output-gate fact.
- **hl-agent and claude_code.hl.** hl-agent neither starts children nor asks side questions, and `examples/claude_code.hl` gains no side handler, since beat-baseline owns that file.
- **Untested inputs.** Side handlers with non-text parameters, or calling a `finds` or blob tool, are not exercised by the simulator.

Measurements (5-core Hand shared with several agents, `nice`, `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=2`):

- `hl sim --seeds 0..2000 docs/snippets/tree.hl` (about 1 to 2 s per target):

  | Target | Runs | Crashes | Children | Joined | Child crashes | Other |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | native-jsonl | 35,618 | 16,997 | 6,702 | 5,505 | 9,253 | |
  | native-sqlite | 31,721 | | 7,524 | 6,486 | 6,443 | |
  | server-postgres | 31,683 | | 7,560 | 6,181 | 5,910 | 1,992 takeovers, 1,271 fenced |
  | cloudflare | 32,543 | | 7,524 | 6,486 | 7,265 | |
  | vercel | 38,097 | | 8,731 | 7,409 | 9,073 | |

- `hl sim --seeds 0..2000 docs/snippets/side.hl` (6 to 9 s per target):

  | Target | Runs | Crashes | Suspensions | Side questions | Other |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | native-jsonl | 30,318 | 15,450 | 9,836 | 9,258 | |
  | native-sqlite | 24,790 | | | 9,084 | |
  | server-postgres | 25,176 | | | 9,142 | 1,117 takeovers |
  | cloudflare | 26,576 | | | 9,331 | |
  | vercel | 25,890 | | | 9,136 | |

  `--strict --seeds 0..300` passes on all five. Before the oracle replayed only the turns before the asking turn, the strict sweep found the Postgres takeover case (seed 23).
- **First failing seeds** (seeds 0..200, `--twice`):
  - `grandchild_unscoped`: seed 1 on every target.
  - `family_one_level`: seed 0 or 1.
  - `side_live_state`: seed 0, seed 5 on cloudflare.
  - `side_journaled`: seed 0 everywhere.
- **Prover.** `hl check docs/snippets/tree.hl` proves on all five targets with 0 prover steps (no arithmetic): 3 sessions, 8 effects, 28 journal records and 5 outbound requests in the tree. `docs/snippets/side.hl`: the side handler takes 6 prover steps and the prompt handler 4,526. Every other example's `hl check` output is unchanged.
- **Differential runs.**
  - `tree.hl` and `spawn.hl`: Rust seeds 0..200 and JavaScript seeds 0..100.
  - `tree_planner.hl` under `--scope 1.1`: Rust 0..200 (server-postgres 16,280 events, 538 resumes) and JavaScript 0..100 (vercel 2,348 steps).
  - `side.hl`: Rust 0..200 (server-postgres 947 side questions, as many as the interpreter) and JavaScript 0..100 (cloudflare 465, vercel 449, equal to the simulator's).
- **check.sh.** A first `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh` on 07e89f9, the spawn trees before the merge, passed every stage it reached, through the mutations, js-mutations and prove stages. A runtime restart then killed it (exit 143).
- **check.sh after the merge.** `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh` on c53ffd8 exited 0 in 1,436 s. Wall times: strict 516 s, js-diff 1,183 s (26 jobs), sim 523 s (90 jobs), rust-diff 185 s, diff-plants 82 s, mutations 43 s, scoped-diff 10 s, agent 53 s.

Commands: `hl check --target T docs/snippets/tree.hl`, `hl check examples/invalid/family_sessions.hl`, `hl check docs/snippets/errors/spawn-{sessions,cycle}.hl`, `hl sim --target T --seeds 0..2000 docs/snippets/{tree,side}.hl`, `hl sim --target T --seeds 0..200 --scope 1.1 docs/snippets/tree_planner.hl`, `MUTATIONS='grandchild_unscoped family_one_level side_live_state side_journaled' scripts/mutations.sh`, `scripts/mutations.sh --diff {rust,js}:grandchild_unscoped:T`, `RUST_DIFF_FILES=docs/snippets/tree_planner.hl RUST_DIFF_SCOPE=1.1 scripts/rust-diff.sh`, `JS_DIFF_FILES=docs/snippets/side.hl scripts/js-diff.sh`, `scripts/docs-check.sh`, `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh`.

## 2026-11-14: on idle: the prompt cache's lifetime as a checked idle turn; claude_code.hl refreshes its cache as nanocode does

GAPS.md's gap 8 (the prompt cache's lifetime) is closed (branch cc-keep, from master eae1320).

What this establishes:

- **Idle handlers.** `on idle NAME() at FIELD bound N -> T { ... }` declares the one handler the host admits while the session idles, once the clock (the milliseconds `now()` reads) reaches the time the `u64` state field `FIELD` names; 0 arms nothing. It is an ordinary turn: its effects are journaled under it and replay returns them, so a refresh whose reply is recorded is never sent again (nanocode's `agent/keep.rs` keeps nothing durable). No new record kind: the SQL kinds and the oracle are untouched.
- **The count is checked code.** The checker (`check::idle`, an AST rewrite after `session_end`) adds one hidden field `_idle_runs`, sets it to 0 at the start of every other handler, adds `_idle_runs < N` to the idle handler's `requires` and `_idle_runs = _idle_runs + 1` and `FIELD = 0` to its start, and the invariant `_idle_bounded` (`_idle_runs <= N`), which the prover proves with the program's own. Setting the field to 0 disarms the due an idle turn ran for, so one due never runs twice unless the body assigns it again. A first version kept a second hidden field with the last due; it cost 0.12 M prover steps on claude_code.hl and was dropped.
- **Admission is the runtime's, the timer is advice.** `run_turn` (interpreter, generated Rust, generated JavaScript) refuses an idle request with `Ran::NotDue` (`busy` when the last turn is unfinished, `unarmed`, `early` when `World::clock` reads before the due), writing nothing and taking no turn number; an admitted one's `admitted` record holds `{"at": clock, "due": due}`, which a rerun does not compare. `idle_due(records)` gives a host the armed due (Rust `idle_due(start, records)`, `JsonlJournal::idle_due` and `next_turn`; JavaScript `runtime.idleDue`).
- **Hosts.** Cloudflare: after every finished turn the Durable Object sets its alarm to the due, and `alarm()` runs the idle turn when no turn is unfinished. Vercel: before each request the session reads the due in a step and races the next prompt's hook against a workflow `sleep()` until it (the binding now keys prompt hooks by prompt count, the same tokens for programs without an idle handler). `hl check` prints the idle handler's line with `N` times its worst case.
- **Simulator.** The world is the host (`src/sim/idle.rs`): after every prompt it reads the armed due; a prompt preempts it (`IDLE_PREEMPT_PPM` = 300,000, never after the last prompt), the timer fires early (`IDLE_EARLY_PPM` = 150,000, once per due) or late by up to `IDLE_LATE_MS_MAX` (ten minutes, twice nanocode's 5-minute `CACHE_LIFETIME`; `IDLE_LATE_PPM` = 100,000), and an idle turn's model call reads the cache and writes none in `CACHE_WARM_PPM` = 800,000; at most `REQUESTS_PER_DUE_MAX` (2) requests per due for each of `N` + 1 idle turns. The workload draws prompts from the handlers other than the idle one, and every idle draw happens only for a program with one, so no other program's seeds moved (docs-check reruns the quoted outputs). Idle turns take no scheduled events.
- **Invariant and mutation.** `idle_due`: every idle turn was admitted at a clock at or after its recorded due, for the due its state armed, at most `N` after each other turn, and no early refusal named a clock at or after its due. `idle_early` (the runtime skips the clock check) is caught on all five targets (docs/snippets/keep.hl seeds 0..200: first at seed 4 or 8). Differential plants `rust idle_early` and `js idle_early` are caught as `backend_equal` on every target. The drivers report `not_due` exits; the Cloudflare driver delivers idle requests through `alarm()`, and a prompt turn's retry by alarm only once its `admitted` record is durable (an idle alarm left over from the last turn would otherwise run the idle turn in its place).
- **examples/claude_code.hl** reads `now()` around every request and keeps the cache from them (`keep_cache`: due = start + `cache_lifetime_ms` - 2 x reach, nanocode's `lifetime - 2*reach - elapsed`), and `on idle refresh() at refresh_due bound cache_refreshes` stops when the clock passed the request plus the lifetime, refreshes with `messages(context) mode refresh`, stops on a failure or when the cache read nothing or wrote something, and keeps the cache again from its own readings. `cache_lifetime_ms` = 300,000 is nanocode's `CACHE_LIFETIME` (agent.rs:71) and `cache_refreshes` = 12 is `ClaudeModel::cache_refreshes` for the 5-minute cache (pricing.rs:73: write 1.25x over read 0.1x of input, `cache_write_5m / cache_read`, 12 for every model priced). `clock_ms_max` = 8,640,000,000,000,000 is ECMAScript's time value bound (ECMA-262, 21.4.1.1), which bounds the clock arithmetic for the prover.
- **agent/** (hl-agent): `--keep-warm N` (at most `cache_refreshes`) keeps the process alive after the prompt and runs up to N idle turns, sleeping until each due; mode `refresh` sends the request that produced the context's last reply (the entries before its last `assistant` entry) with `max_tokens` 0, unstreamed: the Messages API answers `max_tokens: 0` with an empty message, and refuses it streamed ("stream cannot be true when max_tokens is 0"), as nanocode's `refresh` also sends it through `create_prepared` (keep.rs:160-177).

Departures from nanocode, kept:

- Refreshes run only between turns, never during a long tool call inside a turn, where nanocode's keep task also fires.
- The bound resets at every other turn (`_idle_runs`); nanocode resets its count at every kept request. claude_code.hl's prompt turns reach the model unless they fail first, so the two agree except for a turn that fails before its request.
- A refresh keeps the cache from its own reach, where nanocode keeps the original request's; reach is measured to the whole reply, not to the response headers, so the due is earlier (safer). A request whose reply took half the lifetime or more is not kept, where nanocode refreshes at once.
- The one-hour cache (`HOUR_CACHE_LIFETIME`, 20 refreshes), fast mode (no refreshes) and the subagent rule of `refresh_due` are not written: hl-agent sends 5-minute ephemeral breakpoints only.
- The refresh request is recomputed by the host from the journaled context instead of kept in memory; it matches the cache while the system prompt and tools are unchanged, and the stop rule catches any change.
- Idle turns take turn numbers, so a Cloudflare or Vercel client sends a prompt as the session's next turn.

Measurements (5-core Hand shared with three other agents, `nice`, `CHECK_JOBS=2`):

- Real refreshes, claude-haiku-4-5, `hl-agent run --keep-warm 2` on a 48 KB file read: the last request wrote 16,882 cache tokens; the idle turns ran 297.1 s and 595.4 s after it (dues 296.1 s and 296.9 s after each start), each with 6 input, 0 output, 16,882 cache read and 0 cache write tokens, `{"refreshed":16882}`. The prompt answered "heliotrope". A first attempt below Haiku 4.5's minimum cacheable prompt (3,286 tokens) wrote no cache, so its refresh would have stopped as lapsed. A run killed while idle and started again on its journal found the due from the journal and slept the rest of it.
- `hl sim --seeds 0..2000` (check.sh): docs/snippets/keep.hl native-jsonl 8,637 turns, 21,951 runs, 12,749 crashes, 3,605 idle turns, 565 refused; native-sqlite and vercel 8,603 turns, 3,571 idle; server-postgres 1,416 takeovers, 3,598 idle; cloudflare 8,907 turns, 3,875 idle. examples/claude_code.hl native-jsonl 8,006 turns, 27,467 runs, 2,974 idle, 438 refused; native-sqlite 2,979 idle; server-postgres 1,661 takeovers, 3,001 idle; cloudflare 3,409 idle; vercel 2,991 idle.
- Differential runs match the interpreter: keep.hl Rust seeds 0..300 on the three Rust targets and JavaScript 0..100 on both (vercel 1,663 steps); claude_code.hl Rust 0..300, JavaScript 0..100 (cloudflare 1,369 runs, vercel 2,777 steps).
- `hl check examples/claude_code.hl`: prover steps for handler prompt 1,729,587 before, 3,509,607 after (88% of `PROOF_STEPS_PER_HANDLER_MAX`, 4,000,000); refresh 26,086; worst case 9,216 to 10,240 effects and 17,923 to 19,971 records (two `now()` per round); 2.8 s to 4.9 s wall. Variants measured on the way: four kept fields with invariants ran out of the budget, three plain fields 3.9 M, two fields without arithmetic 3.1 M.
- `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh`: 1,329.5 s (strict 224 s, sim 528 s wall and 1,050 s busy, rust-diff 234 s, js-diff 213 s, mutations 150 s, diff-plants 148 s), failing only in its last stage, agent/'s rustfmt check, which the next commit fixed; the run on the final commit passed.

Commands: `hl check docs/snippets/keep.hl`, `hl sim --target T --seeds 0..200 [--mutation idle_early] docs/snippets/keep.hl`, `scripts/mutations.sh --diff rust:idle_early:T`, `JS_DIFF_FILES=docs/snippets/keep.hl scripts/js-diff.sh`, `RUST_DIFF_FILES=docs/snippets/keep.hl scripts/rust-diff.sh`, `hl-agent run --instruction I --cwd D --model claude-haiku-4-5 --journal J --keep-warm 2`, `scripts/docs-check.sh`, `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh`.

## 2026-11-14: hl-agent starts real child sessions; claude_code.hl spawns, waits for and lists subagents

What this establishes (branch cc-agent, from master eae1320):

- **examples/claude_code.hl** declares nanocode's subagent tools (`crates/runtime/subagents/src/tools.rs` at c7679e3): `spawn spawn_agent runs delegated from "claude_code_child.hl"`, `wait_agent(agent_id: text)` and `list_agents(include_completed: bool = false)`, with `on resume` for the join. A round whose calls hold a `wait_agent` runs them one after another (`run_joining`): each `wait_agent` is `join` on the key read from its input, its answer cut like a direct tool result, and every other call dispatched as before; a round without one runs the unchanged `parallel` for (`run_parallel`), since a parallel arm may run no effect. Each requested call still pushes exactly one entry, so the context reserve and the worst case's effects, records and requests are unchanged (9,216 effects, 17,923 records); the new measure is 8,192 child sessions (512 rounds x 16 calls), 71,312,384 effects with them.
- **examples/claude_code_child.hl** is the subagent: `claude_code.hl` with its compaction, cuts, stop recovery and backoff, without the MCP listing, `ToolSearch` and the round limit argument, handler `delegated(role, task)` (the spawn tool's parameters), which opens with nanocode's `agent_prompt` (`model.rs:286-310`) and ends `submitted(output)` once a `submit_result` call in a round is accepted. An answer without one gets nanocode's `COMPLETION_INSTRUCTIONS_LEAD` ("Before finishing, call submit_result ...") and the loop goes on; a child spawns nothing, as the language requires.
- **The prover** counted spawn sites by indexing the handler's sites with the plan's slot, which past the handler's sites names a parallel batch's virtual site: `hl check` panicked (index out of bounds) on the first harness with both a spawn tool and a `parallel` for. It now maps the slot to its real site (`src/prove/mod.rs`, `lowered.real`), as the durability proof does.
- **hl-agent** emits both harnesses (`agent/build.rs`, `harness` and `child_harness`, the plant applied to each) and implements both `Host` traits with one macro over `Agent::run_tool` and `Agent::run_model`. A spawn (`agent/src/children.rs`) writes `{role, task}` to `<journal>/children/T.S/spawn.json` (fsynced, renamed, directory fsynced) and runs the child on a thread as its own durable session: journal `children/T.S/journal.jsonl`, its own state directory, usage ledger and stream files, the parent's system prompt, and the child's tool set (tools.json plus `submit_result`; the parent's adds `spawn_agent`, `wait_agent` and `list_agents`, `agent/prompts/subagent-tools.json`). At a join the runtime ends the run (`Ran::Suspended`); `run_turn` runs the turn again, its replay reaches the open suspend and `Host::resume` joins the child's thread, or, when none runs in this process, runs the child from its journal (recovery reports its open external calls `unknown`), and answers with the JSON of the child's finished result, which the runtime appends and flushes. A child whose run fails at the host (journal corrupt, stop) is not answered: the turn stays suspended and hl-agent exits with an error. Reruns are bounded by `JOINS_PER_TURN_MAX` (the harness's `rounds_max x calls_per_round_max`), threads running at once by `RUNNING_MAX` = 16 (nanocode's `DEFAULT_MAX_RESIDENT_SUBAGENTS`, `lib.rs:45`; past it a spawn fails with a message the model reads), and the listing by the same harness bound. At the end of the turn hl-agent waits for children it started whose results were never read. `usage.json` adds every child ledger's requests, tokens and tool calls to the parent's and counts `children`; wall time is the parent's.
- **scripts/agent-drill.sh** (mock): the parent spawns after step 2, runs step 3 while the child runs, then waits; the child (`$out/child.json`, served by `mock-messages` to requests offering `submit_result`) runs two Bash steps, a Read and `submit_result`; `"$spawned"` in a scripted input is the key the last `spawn_agent` result gave. The checks run per journal (parent and every `children/*/`): every external dispatch, `spawn_agent` included, once; every cut stream answered; no `.sse` left. In mock mode it also requires one child journal under the parent's, with a `status` record, and the parent's join result to be the child's `{"submitted": ...}` exactly once; it counts kills whose last log line was the child's (`hl-agent[T.S]:`).

Departures from nanocode, kept:

- Parameters harnesslang cannot type or hl-agent cannot honour are not offered: `spawn_agent` takes `role` and `task` only (no `harness`, `model`, `thinking`, `reasoning_mode` or `output_contract`); `wait_agent` takes one `agent_id` text, the key `T.S`, rather than a list of integer ids and `timeout_ms`, and returns the child's result rather than a snapshot of every requested agent; `list_agents` takes `include_completed` only; `submit_result`'s `output` is text, so there is no schema validation and the receipt ends the child at once rather than asking for a final message. Descriptions are nanocode's, cut where they name what is not offered.
- The child's prompt drops nanocode's agent ID, coordination paragraph and budget (no `send_agent_message`, no budgets) and adds the role.
- `run_joining` cuts every result at the direct-tool limit, without `run_parallel`'s 12,000-token cut for `exec` and `wait`, which hl-agent does not offer.
- `wait_agent` on a finished child answers its result again instead of nanocode's "nothing left to wait for" error; an ID that is not `T.S` or names no spawned child is answered `{"failed": ...}`.
- A child interrupted by a kill restarts only when the parent's replay reaches its join, not at startup, and a child the parent never waits for is run to its end before hl-agent exits rather than continuing the root later.
- Offering three more tools costs about 1,500 prompt tokens per request (the nanoeval rows below).

Measurements (5-core Hand shared with other agents, `nice`, release builds, claude-haiku-4-5):

- `hl check examples/claude_code.hl`: proved on all five targets, 2,604,571 prover steps (1,729,587 for master's file), 9.1 s under load (3.8 s for master's file); `examples/claude_code_child.hl` 1,560,948 steps, 4.2 s.
- `hl sim --seeds 0..2000 examples/claude_code.hl`: native-jsonl 27,997 runs, 19,851 crashes, 1,581 children, 123 joined, 4,531 child crashes (85 s); native-sqlite 1,744 children, 134 joined; server-postgres 1,660 takeovers, 1,880 children, 148 joined; cloudflare 1,859 children, 153 joined; vercel 2,223 children, 140 joined, 7,749 child crashes. `examples/claude_code_child.hl`: passes on every target: native-jsonl 18,300 runs, 13,268 crashes, 8,609 unknown (110 s); native-sqlite 16,039 runs; server-postgres 1,159 takeovers, 693 fenced; cloudflare 18,113 runs; vercel 19,105 runs, 2,841 unknown. `--strict --seeds 0..6` on native-jsonl: 2 s for the parent, 1 s for the child, as for master's file.
- `scripts/agent-drill.sh` (mock, seed 1): 12 kills (9 in model calls, 3 in tools; 2 while the child was the last to log), 10 external dispatches for 10 calls in two journals (parent 8 including the spawn, child 2), none repeated, 6 recovered unknown, 6 streams cut and requested again, one child journal at `journal/children/1.14/`, finished, and its `{"submitted":"the child recorded 2 steps"}` journaled once as the parent's join result; 18 requests in all. Seeds 2 and 3: 11 and 12 kills (3 and 2 in the child), both child Bash calls recovered `unknown` and not run again. `AGENT_DRILL_PLANT=batch_flush_after_dispatch` is still caught (`1/6` dispatched 7 times).
- A real delegation (`output/real`, instruction: spawn a subagent to count the words in notes.txt with the shell while the parent writes lines.txt, then wait and write words.txt): exit 0 in 9.9 s, 6 requests (parent 4, child 2), 6 tool calls, 10,955 input, 644 output, 8,510 cache-read and 4,549 cache-write tokens. Parent journal 25 records (12,465 bytes): admitted; per round an `mcp` listing call and result and a model record; the spawn's call and `{"ok":"1.3"}` batched with a Read; a Write; the join as `call suspend {"key":"1.3"}` and `result {"ok":"{\"submitted\":\"9\"}"}`; a Write; status and checkpoint. Child journal `children/1.3/journal.jsonl` 9 records (4,593 bytes): admitted, model, Bash `wc -w notes.txt` call and result, model, `submit_result {"output":"9"}` call and result, status, checkpoint. The parent wrote lines.txt while the child ran. The answer was right (9 words); the line count was not (6 for a 5-line file: the Read tool shows the empty line after the last newline as line 6, an existing behaviour of `agent/src/tools.rs`).
- nanoeval 556e56b local tasks, each in a scratch copy of its environment then its `tests/test.sh`: extract-todos reward 1 (5.8 s, 3 requests, 2 tool calls, 7,857 input, 4,185 cache-write, 286 output tokens), uppercase-message 1 (5.3 s, 3, 2, 7,792, 4,150, 277), write-greeting 1 (2.8 s, 2, 1, 7,812, 0, 182): the same requests and tool calls as before; prompt tokens 12,042 against 10,515 for extract-todos.

Commands: `hl check examples/claude_code.hl`, `hl sim --target T --seeds 0..2000 examples/claude_code.hl` and `examples/claude_code_child.hl`, `hl sim --target native-jsonl --seeds 0..6 --strict F`, `AGENT_DRILL_SEED=S scripts/agent-drill.sh`, `AGENT_DRILL_PLANT=batch_flush_after_dispatch scripts/agent-drill.sh`, `hl-agent run --instruction instruction.md --cwd ws --model claude-haiku-4-5 --journal journal`, `hl-agent run --instruction tasks/T/instruction.md --cwd SCRATCH --model claude-haiku-4-5 --journal J` then `tests/test.sh`, `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh`.

## 2026-11-14: children-cache: idle turns, spawn trees, side questions and hl-agent's child sessions together; GAPS.md rewritten

Branch children-cache merges the three lanes above, cc-tree (spawn trees and side questions, bbc701e), cc-keep (`on idle` and the prompt cache's lifetime, 167157e) and cc-agent (hl-agent's real child sessions, 39c004f), from master eae1320. What the merges establish beyond the three entries:

- **Shared lists.** `Mutation::ALL` holds all 67 mutations (cc-tree's four, then `idle_early`) and `Invariant::ALL` 39 (`family_bounded`, `side_isolated`, `idle_due`), appended in that order; scripts/mutations.sh plans and diff plans are the union, check.sh sweeps and diffs tree.hl, side.hl and keep.hl.
- **Simulator.** One `request_turn` drives every turn, prompted or idle; the side question after a turn (`SIDE_BETWEEN_TURNS_PPM`) is asked inside it, after the turn's run, and the parked question in `drive_turn`'s suspended arm. Programs without side handlers draw nothing for them, so idle programs' seeds are unchanged, and no example has both.
- **Prover.** cc-agent found that `children()` indexed a handler's sites with a plan slot, which for a parallel batch is a virtual site past the end, so `hl check` panicked on a harness with a spawn tool and a `for ... parallel`. cc-tree's rewrite (`spawns_on`, `planned_tree`) had the same indexing; it now maps every slot through the plan's `real` table, at every level of the tree.
- **claude_code.hl** carries both lanes: `on resume`, the subagent tools and `run_joining`, and `on idle refresh()` with `keep_cache`. Together they ran past the prover's budget: handler prompt took 3,085,686 steps with neither cc-keep's clock readings nor `keep_cache`, 3,743,400 with the two `now()` readings, and failed at 4,000,000 with `keep_cache` after every reply. `keep_cache` now runs only where the turn ends with the model's answer (`"end"` and `"tool_use"`): the due matters only once the session idles, which follows a finished turn, so keeping after every reply only to overwrite it was work the prover paid for. Departure: a turn that ends failed or exhausted keeps no cache, where nanocode keeps every request. With it in both arms the handler takes 4,078,944 steps (3,910,693 in one arm), so `PROOF_STEPS_PER_HANDLER_MAX` doubles to 8,000,000, a policy value (LANGUAGE.md's bounds table): `hl check examples/claude_code.hl` proves on all five targets in 11.5 s on the shared Hand (4.9 s for cc-keep's version, 9.1 s for cc-agent's). No other example's steps moved.
- **agent/**: cc-keep's refresh request and `--keep-warm` sit beside cc-agent's `host_for!` hosts; a refresh is sent unstreamed by mode, and the child harness, which has no idle handler, panics on `NotDue` as on `Suspended`. After its prompt, hl-agent runs its idle turns, then waits for children never joined.
- **docs/GAPS.md** listed only gap 8, which `on idle` closes, so it is rewritten from a fresh read of nanocode c7679e3's Claude and Codex backends: eight gaps ranked by what they block (effects preempted by cancel and steer, parallel lanes with their own effects, code cells that outlive their call, background jobs, rewriting history entries, host-resolved unknown outcomes, forks and rewinds, request settings as state), each with nanocode's file and line references, the missing construct and the smallest addition.

Losses, kept: hl-agent's children are threads, so a kill -9 kills parent and child together; the drill counts the kills that land while the child runs (3 of 12 at seed 1), and a child process killed alone is not drilled. The rest are in the three entries above.

Measurements (5-core Hand shared with other agents, `nice`):

- `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh` on cd51e33 (the code of this branch's head, which adds only this file and GAPS.md, neither read by a check): ok in 1,284 s (sim 620 s wall / 1,238 s busy, js-diff 994 s wall, rust-diff 211 s, strict 179 s, diff-plants 106 s, mutations 65 s for 66 plans, examples 54 s, agent 34 s).
- `scripts/agent-drill.sh` (mock, seed 1) on the merge: 12 kills (9 in a model call, 3 in a tool, 3 while the child ran), 10 external dispatches of 10 distinct across the parent's and the child's journals, 2 child dispatches recovered unknown and never rerun, 1 child joined, 1 blob journaled, 6 cut streams requested again.
- Real task, claude-haiku-4-5, the merged hl-agent (delegate a word count to a subagent while the parent writes lines.txt): exit 0 in 10.0 s, 6 requests, 6 tool calls, 10,954 input, 684 output, 8,571 cache-read and 4,573 cache-write tokens, 1 child; lines.txt 5 and words.txt 9, both right; the parent's journal 41 records, the child's (children/1.5/journal.jsonl) 9.
- `hl sim --seeds 0..50` on the merge: keep.hl, side.hl and tree.hl ok on native-jsonl, cloudflare and vercel (keep.hl native-jsonl 89 idle turns, side.hl 238 side questions, tree.hl 156 children); claude_code.hl seeds 0..2000 in check.sh, e.g. native-sqlite 1,714 children and 1,355 idle turns.

Commands: `hl check examples/claude_code.hl`, `hl check --target native-jsonl F` on variants of claude_code.hl in output/, `hl sim --target T --seeds 0..50 docs/snippets/{keep,side,tree}.hl`, `scripts/agent-drill.sh`, `hl-agent run --instruction I --cwd W --model claude-haiku-4-5 --journal J`, `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh`.

## 2026-11-14: hl-agent against nanocode again: the Bash fix, a system prompt that asks for few tool calls, and one interleaved run of both agents

What this establishes (branch beat-baseline, from master 7fa419b; merged real-agent 0ad1c37 and master at 6872fd1, 22a99bf and 553d5f0):

- **Why hl-agent cost more and took longer** in the eval entry above, from the meter's records of that run (`output/eval/main` of the eval lane, 60 attempts per agent): per request the two agents read about the same prompt (hl-agent 25,666 cache-read tokens per request, nanocode 23,320), and hl-agent's fixed prefix is smaller (system 3,747 characters and four tools, about 2,800 tokens cached, against nanocode's 8,578 with the `exec` description). Parameters were the same (streaming aside: model, `max_tokens` 128,000, adaptive thinking with summarized display, effort high; nanocode also sends `context_management` `clear_thinking_20251015` with `keep: all`, which clears nothing, and a top-level `cache_control` where hl-agent marks the system, the last tool and the last block). The difference was the number of requests and what each kept: nanocode's Code Mode prompt tells Claude to orchestrate related calls in one `exec` cell and to "return only the results needed for the next reasoning step", so a cell runs several steps and prints a few lines; hl-agent, offered Bash, Read, Edit and Write directly, made one call per request (nginx-request-logging 13.7 requests against 6.0, openssl-selfsigned-cert 9.7 against 4.0) and read whole files (build-cython-ext: `invariants.py` 62,365 characters, `spacecurve.py` 50,797, kept in every later request; that task alone was $0.038 of the $0.028 per-attempt gap). Wall time was dominated by the Bash hang (kv-store-grpc and pypi-server at the 900 s timeout, 85 s per attempt over the suite) and by non-streamed requests the Hand's egress dropped. Read's limits are nanocode's own (`MAX_READ_BYTES` 262,144, `MAX_READ_TOKENS` 25,000 in `crates/backends/claude/tools/src/tools/text_files.rs`), so they were not the cause.
- **Fixes.** (1) real-agent's 0ad1c37 (Bash waits for the shell, output to files) merged; the conflict was the removed `PIPE_CHUNK_BYTES` beside master's removed `RESULT_TOKENS_MAX`, both dropped. (2) Streaming came with master. (3) `agent/prompts/claude-code-system.json` gains a "# Tool calls" block, the direct-tool counterpart of nanocode's Code Mode guidance: steps whose order is known go in one Bash command, independent calls in one response, read only the part of a file needed (search first), return only the output the next decision needs, start servers in the background and check them in the same command, separate calls only for a judgment. The system prompt is the host's, so this is host work; `examples/claude_code.hl` did not change.
- **Code Mode for hl-agent was not taken.** Offering `code exec { tools Bash, Read, Edit, Write ... }` in claude_code.hl with nanocode-like limits is refused on Cloudflare: `hl check` reports 131,584 outbound requests (131,072 nested calls in cells: 512 rounds x 16 calls x 16 cell calls) against the 10,000 per invocation, while check.sh checks every example on every target. hl-agent would also need a djs-backed `CellEngine` with nanocode crates as dependencies, which agent/ does not have. Left open.

Pilots (hl-agent only, 1 attempt, root with the prompt block, $2.63): pilot 1 on six tasks: 6/6, 7.5 requests and $0.091 per attempt (the same tasks in the eval entry: hl-agent 9.3 and $0.133, nanocode 5.4 and $0.115); pilot 2 on build-cython-ext, kv-store-grpc, pypi-server, polyglot-c-py, vulnerable-secret, cobol-modernization: 5 of 6 passed (polyglot-c-py failed on its answer), build-cython-ext $0.845 against the eval entry's $1.839 (hl-agent) and $1.076 (nanocode).

**The run.** `python3 evals/run.py --suite evals/suite.txt --tasks output/eval/tasks --agent-root output/eval/root-merged --out output/eval/final --agents nanocode,hl-agent --attempts 3 --model claude-sonnet-4-6 --parallel 3 --limits none --meter-port 18792 --relay-port 13142`, then `python3 evals/report.py output/eval/final`. Rows go attempt by task by agent, so the two agents of a task and attempt run side by side in the same pool. nanocode c7679e3 (sha256 of the unpatched binary 2cd1984e…), hl-agent built at 22a99bf. Hosted Hand, Linux 6.18.56 x86_64, 5 cores, 14 GB, shared with two other agents, rustc 1.100.0, `nice`; `scripts/check.sh` with `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice -n 19` ran during the first 15 minutes of it. Prices as in the eval entry.

| Agent | Attempts | Passed | Failed | Errored | Pass rate (95% Wilson) | Cost | Cost/attempt | Wall/attempt | Requests/attempt | Prompt tokens/attempt | Output tokens/attempt |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |

| hl-agent | 60 | 52 | 8 | 0 | 86.7% (75.8-93.1%) | $11.37 | $0.190 | 200 s | 10.3 | 160,701 | 6,820 |

Paired by task and attempt (60 pairs), hl-agent minus nanocode, mean with its 95% normal interval (report.py's new section): cost -$0.0644 (-$0.1462 to $0.0175), wall -52.1 s (-109.8 to 5.6 s), requests 0.53 (-2.32 to 3.39), prompt tokens -119,737 (-245,310 to 5,836). hl-agent was cheaper in 39 of 60 pairs (median -$0.0088) and faster in 30 (median 0 s). Leaving out the 12 pairs where either side had a request the upstream connection dropped (nanocode 8 rows, hl-agent 9): 48 pairs, cost mean -$0.0148 (median -$0.0080, cheaper in 32), wall mean -32.9 s (median +0.5 s, faster in 22); totals nanocode $7.87 and 6,228 s, hl-agent $7.16 and 4,647 s.

So: hl-agent matched nanocode's pass rate and was cheaper and faster on average in this run, but neither difference is outside its 95% interval, and the per-attempt median wall time is a tie; the mean wall gain comes from the long tail (nanocode's kv-store-grpc 2 and sqlite-db-truncate 2 and regex-log 2 ran to the timeout after dropped connections). nanocode cost more here than in the eval entry ($0.254 against $0.202), mostly largest-eigenval ($1.154 per attempt, 38 requests) and dropped-connection retries.

Losses, read from each row's verifier log and meter records:

- **openssl-selfsigned-cert (hl-agent 1/3, nanocode 3/3).** Attempt 1: `check_cert.py` imports `cryptography`, which the image lacks, and exits 1 ("Required library missing"); attempt 3: the script falls back to its own parser and prints no YYYY-MM-DD date, which the test requires. Both are the model's script, not run by the agent the way the verifier runs it; the new prompt's "check the result in that same command" did not stop it. Not a harness fault, but it may be the prompt trading a verification call for fewer requests; 3 attempts cannot tell.
- **cancel-async-tasks (hl-agent 0/3, nanocode 1/3).** Both agents fail it nearly always (0/3 each in the eval entry); one attempt.

Matching it is the next host fix; it was found after the run and is not in the measured binary.

Losses and limits, kept:

- The run measured hl-agent built at 22a99bf. master then merged subagents (553d5f0): the head offers `spawn_agent`, `wait_agent` and `list_agents` beside the four tools, which every request carries (a 3-task smoke at 553d5f0: 3/3, $0.021 per attempt against $0.009 to $0.012 at 22a99bf, the difference mostly the new prefix's cache writes). The suite was not rerun at the head.
- A first full run at ca4f33a was stopped after 30 rows when master moved and is not counted (`output/eval/final-premerge`, $2.75).
- Docker cannot apply cgroup limits on this Hand (`--limits none`), and the egress drops some long requests for both agents, as in the eval entry.
- Spend: $26.61 for the measured run, $2.75 for the stopped one, $2.66 for the pilots and $0.09 for smokes, about $32 in all.
