## 2026-11-14: in-flight: interruptible effects, background jobs and recovery hooks (gaps 1, 4 and 6)

Branch in-flight, from master 17f5d21, closes gaps 1, 4 and 6 of docs/GAPS.md with three declarations (LANGUAGE.md, Effects in flight), in the checker, the interpreter, the simulator, the prover, the generated Rust and JavaScript, `examples/claude_code.hl` and hl-agent.

What it establishes:

- **Interruptible effects.** `interruptible by cancel[, steer]` after a tool's effect class or a model declaration. An effect that would start while an interrupting event is pending (a cancel, or a steer `take steers` has not taken) does not run; one during which such an event is drained at its own sequence number is abandoned: an external call is reported `unknown` with a `recovery` record (the record Vercel's in-step recovery already writes), a none or idempotent call records `failed` with nanocode's `StartedToolCall::REASON` text, the model records `{"interrupted":[]}`, a fifth `attempt` variant, so an interruptible model needs a window. No new record kind; replay reads the outcome from the result records. On Vercel an interruptible site flushes before it runs and drains right after it, so the drain precedes its result record; the JavaScript does it with a step that returns a `ran` output and a workflow drain (`STEPS_PER_EFFECT_MAX` 2 -> 3).
- **Jobs.** `jobs at most N` on an external text tool and `on job(notice: text) bound M`. A host answer `{"job": text}` is journaled as is and read as `ok(text)`; the job is open under `T.S` until a `done` notice closes it. Notices are a third inbound event (`Inbound::Job`), counted in `records_max`, read with `take jobs`; `close_inbox` leaves them for the next turn. A notice for a job whose start result is not yet recorded is answered `full` and kept. `hold(c)`/`hold(e) at most c` is a wait (`SiteKind::Hold`, durability `FlushBeforeRun`, counted with the waits) that returns at once with no job open, else when a notice or cancel arrives or at the deadline, and always drains after. Every run of a turn over open jobs asks `World::jobs` which still run and records nanocode's "killed, outcome unknown" notice (`lost:K`) for the rest at the next drain. The jsonl Rust runtime reads a journal whole when the program takes job events, since a tail from a checkpoint would lose earlier turns' jobs.
- **Recovery hooks.** `effect external recoverable`. Recovery asks `World::recover` for each open call of such a tool (at load, and on Vercel at a retried step): a reply becomes the call's result, `not_dispatched` leaves it open to run under the same sequence number, `unknown` is as before. The `recovery` record gains `replied` and `not_dispatched` lists, present only when non-empty, so every other journal is byte-identical. On Postgres a `not_dispatched` answer claims the call for the asking owner's epoch and the simulated host refuses it from an older owner; without the claim seed 38 dispatched a Write twice after a takeover (external_once).
- **Prover.** Every interruptible effect gets three arms (finishes, abandoned, never starts) and a recoverable reopened call the host's three answers, with `Entry::Attested` clearing its reopened state. Recovery records now cost 0 in the plan's record count, as `hl check` counts them beside the worst case ("plus one recovery record per restart"): the path-insensitive count over a parallel for's two repeats otherwise counted a retried step's recovery and result on top of the call record (63 against 45 records on vercel for docs/snippets/in_flight.hl). `recover_trusts_lost` is a lowering mutation the prover catches as external_once on every target.
- **Simulator.** `src/sim/jobs.rs`: a jobs tool's call becomes a job with `JOB_START_PPM` = 400000 when it has room, stalls with `JOB_STALL_PPM` = 250000, finishes within `JOB_RUN_MS_MAX` (ten clock steps) and dies in a crash with `JOB_DIES_PPM` = 500000; recovery is answered truthfully or `unknown` (`RECOVER_UNKNOWN_PPM` = 300000). New invariants `interrupt_honored`, `job_once`, `job_reported` (42 in all), new mutations `interrupt_ignored`, `interrupt_reruns`, `recover_trusts_lost`, `job_lost_dropped`, `job_lost_all` (72 in all), each planned in scripts/mutations.sh on docs/snippets/in_flight.hl; `job_lost_dropped` runs seeds 0..1000 since a died job a later run must report is rarer. Holds reuse `wait_until` and `wall_limit`. The sequential twin is skipped when jobs ran, as it is when events were sent.
- **Differential plants** (merged from lanes in-flight-rust 8a8f750 and in-flight-js 68c9fea): rust `interrupt_ignored`, `recover_skipped`, `job_lost_dropped`; js `interrupt_ignored`, `recover_reply_ignored`, `job_unopened`; each caught as backend_equal.
- **claude_code.hl.** Bash is `external recoverable jobs at most bash_jobs_max` (8), Edit and Write `external recoverable`, `on job(notice: text) bound job_notices_max` (8). When the model answers, `answered()` holds for up to `job_hold_ms` = 600000 (nanocode's `DEFAULT_BACKGROUND_TIMEOUT_MS`), takes the notices and goes on with another round when there are any. Taking notices at every round instead cost 7,257,013 prover steps against 4,078,944 for master's file; holding only at the answer costs 4,329,878. The file takes no cancel, so it declares no interruptible effect.
- **hl-agent.** A Bash call past its timeout keeps running as a job when the tool has room ("moved to the background (ID: T.S). You will be notified when it completes.") and the turn hold waits for it; `run_in_background` jobs end with the turn, nanocode's `ENDS_WITH_TURN` binding, and every job is stopped when the turn ends. Job notices reach the runtime through `Host::inbox`. Recovery: Bash writes a fsynced receipt before it spawns (before its ledger line) and its result after; with the result receipt it replies, without the start receipt it answers not dispatched, else unknown. Write replies when the file holds the content; Edit replies when the file holds `new_string` and not `old_string`, answers not dispatched in the opposite case, else unknown.

Departures, kept: waits, cells and code cells are not interruptible; no steer withdrawal; no turn is admitted because a job finished; hl-agent sends no stall notices (GAPS.md 9).

Measurements (5-core Hand shared with other agents, `nice`, release builds):

- `hl sim --target T --seeds 0..2000 docs/snippets/in_flight.hl`: native-jsonl 17,764 runs, 12,732 crashes, 378 interrupted, recovery answered 1,165 replies, 2,441 not dispatched, 1,575 unknown, 528 jobs, 141 notices, 284 died; native-sqlite 348 interrupted, 513 jobs; server-postgres 1,057 takeovers, 332 interrupted; cloudflare 456 interrupted, 511 jobs; vercel 486 interrupted, 538 jobs.
- `hl check examples/claude_code.hl`: proved on all five targets, 4,329,878 prover steps (4,078,944 for master's file), 19,971 journal records per run, plus at most 8 event records per turn.
- Merging master adbf1f5 (Bash output to files, 0ad1c37): a Bash call moved to the background keeps its output in its own pair of files (`bash-N.stdout`, `bash-N.stderr` in the state directory, N a per-process sequence), read when the job ends; a job whose files cannot be read reports that it lost its output in its notice instead of a completed state.
- With output in files a foreground Bash call such as `python3 -m http.server 8765 &` returns at once and leaves the server in its process group, which master then never stops. hl-agent records such a group (still alive, by `kill -0`, when its shell exits) and kills it when the turn ends, beside the turn's jobs: the real server task below, rerun after the merge, made exactly that call (`run_in_background` false), got hello, and left no server.
- `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh` after merging master adbf1f5: ok in 957 s (js-diff 765 s wall, sim 519 s wall / 1,035 s busy, strict 193 s, rust-diff 86 s, examples 75 s, mutations 46 s for 71 plans, agent 38 s).
- `scripts/agent-drill.sh` (mock): the slow step (`sleep 3`, timeout 100 ms) becomes job 1.15, the drill kills hl-agent while it runs, and the next run records `lost:1.15`; seeds 1, 4, 5, 6, 7 pass with 4 to 8 recovery replies per run (seed 1: `{'reply': 5, 'unknown': 2}`), each external call dispatched once. The drill now skips a child whose spawn its parent recovered `unknown` (an orphan that is never joined; master's drill fails seed 5 on that child's cut stream).
- A real background server (`output/real-job`: start `python3 -m http.server` in the background, curl it, write fetched.txt): exit 0 in 9.9 s, 4 requests, 3 tool calls, 7,912 input and 414 output tokens, the server stopped at the turn's end; before, the server call would have waited out Bash's 120 s timeout.
- nanoeval 556e56b local tasks, each in a scratch copy of its environment then its `tests/test.sh`: extract-todos reward 1 (5.2 s, 3 requests, 2 tool calls, 7,856 input, 4,182 cache-write, 355 output tokens), uppercase-message 1 (5.7 s, 3, 2, 7,783, 4,143, 289), write-greeting 1 (3.5 s, 2, 1, 7,793, 0, 136).

Commands: `hl sim --target T --seeds 0..2000 docs/snippets/in_flight.hl`, `MUTATIONS=M scripts/mutations.sh`, `hl prove --target T --mutation recover_trusts_lost docs/snippets/in_flight.hl`, `hl check examples/claude_code.hl`, `AGENT_DRILL_SEED=S scripts/agent-drill.sh`, `hl-agent run --instruction tasks/T/instruction.md --cwd SCRATCH --model claude-haiku-4-5 --journal J` then `tests/test.sh`, `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh`.

## 2026-11-14: gaps-2-5-8: the agent drill's seed-5 failure, model settings, rewrite and parallel lanes

Branch gaps-2-5-8, from master 37df067. It establishes:

- **The agent drill at seed 5 was a host fault, and it is timing-dependent.** The drill's kill delays come from the seed but land wherever the run happens to be, so a seed fails only on some runs: on master, `AGENT_DRILL_SEED=5` passed 29 of 30 repeated runs and seeds 0..40 passed once each. The failing run (run 22) killed hl-agent after the Write of done.txt had its call record flushed but before it ran. Recovery asked hl-agent about the open Write; `recover_write` answered `unknown` whenever the file did not already hold the content, so the harness never ran the Write again (as it must not, for an unknown external call), and done.txt was never written. Write now takes the same fsynced start receipt as Bash before it runs: the content in place is a reply, no start receipt is `not_dispatched` (the call runs again under the same sequence number), a start receipt without the content is `unknown`. A first version routed every receipted tool through the Bash runner, which turned the Write into a failed Bash call; Bash alone runs as Bash now. With the fix seed 5 passes 30 of 30 and seeds 0..40 pass: 381 kills (master 359), recovery answered 164 replies, 134 unknown and 2 not dispatched (master 172, 112, 0), 21 seeds with the spawn recovered unknown, 543 s for the 41 seeds.
- **Model settings (gap 8).** `model m ... settings(effort: effort = "high", fast: bool = false)` declares typed request settings, each a bool or a text enum with a literal default; `m(context) with {effort: e}` gives a call's values from any expression, so a harness keeps them as session state. Every request of such a model carries every setting, the `model` record journals them as `"settings"` (absent for a model without settings, so every other journal is byte-identical), and a replay that computes other settings diverges, as a mode does. New invariant `settings_sent` (43 in all), mutation `settings_dropped`, differential plants rust and js `settings_dropped`. `examples/claude_code.hl` declares `settings(effort: effort_level = "high")`, a `set_effort` handler stores the effort between turns, and each prompt turn pins it (`pinned = effort`) and sends the pinned value with its requests and its prompt-cache refreshes; hl-agent puts the request's effort into `output_config` (its constant `EFFORT` stays the child harness's, which has no settings).
- **rewrite (gap 5).** `rewrite field { pattern => entry | drop, ... }` maps a state list of a sum in place; unnamed variants stay, arms run no effect (refused by the checker). It writes no record; the prover keeps the length when no arm drops and otherwise bounds it by the old length; arm expressions' cuts count among the program's cuts. Mutation `rewrite_live_only` (rewrites only past the journal, not when a rerun replays the turn) is caught by `checkpoint_compacted` on all five targets; plants rust and js `rewrite_kept_lost`. A first snippet made one repair call after the rewrite and no external effect followed it, so a crash never fell between the rewrite and a durable record and the mutation survived 300 seeds on every target; the snippet now repairs inside its round loop. `examples/claude_code.hl` repairs its history once per turn when the provider rejects a request over appended tool changes (an error naming `tool_addition`, `tool_removal` or `tool_reference`, nanocode's `refused_tool_changes`): it drops the listing notices and asks again.
- **Parallel lanes (gap 2, in part).** A parallel for's arms may run effects of their own and an `after tool` hook: they run after the batch, in list order, and their effects take the sequence numbers after the batch's. The checker refuses a return, a dispatch, an event read or a nested batch in an arm, and still refuses a parallel for in a harness with a `before tool` hook. A hooked dispatch's lowering puts the hook's pure bindings before the dispatch; the checker moves them into the lane and dispatches the item itself. The sequential twin (`sequential_equal`) is skipped for programs whose lanes run effects, since the plain loop interleaves those effects with its calls; mutation `lane_seq_shared` (lane effects numbered from the batch's first sequence number) is caught by `liveness`. GAPS.md gap 2 now lists what remains: per-call `before tool` hooks, concurrent lanes and program-chosen grouping (`parallel when`). claude_code.hl has no tool hooks, so it does not use lanes.

Departures, kept: lanes run one after another, not concurrently; `sequential_equal` does not cover batches with effectful lanes; a rewrite arm sees the list being rewritten as empty in every backend (the interpreter, the Rust and the JavaScript take the list before mapping it).

Measurements (5-core Hand shared with two other agents, `nice`):

- `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh` on d4cf8d5: ok in 1,338 s (js-diff 882 s wall, sim 470 s wall / 936 s busy, strict 258 s, rust-diff 190 s, diff-plants 177 s for 84 plants, agent 92 s, examples 86 s, mutations 49 s for 74 plans).
- `hl check examples/claude_code.hl`: proved on all five targets, handler prompt 4,445,350 prover steps (4,329,878 on master; effort pinning alone left it unchanged, the history repair added 115,472).
- `hl sim --seeds 0..200` on all five targets: settings.hl, rewrite.hl (native-jsonl 504 turns, 1,736 runs, 1,232 crashes) and lanes.hl (native-jsonl 1,568 runs, 1,064 crashes, 2,085 dispatches) ok; rust-diff and js-diff of rewrite.hl and lanes.hl over seeds 0..300 match the interpreter.
- A real task with claude-haiku-4-5 (write hello.txt): completed in 2.6 s, 2 requests, 1 tool call, 3,969 input and 97 output tokens; both model records journal `"settings":{"effort":"high"}`.

Commands: `AGENT_DRILL_SEED=5 scripts/agent-drill.sh` 30 times and `AGENT_DRILL_SEED=S scripts/agent-drill.sh` for S in 0..40, on master and on the branch, from a separate worktree; `hl sim --target T --seeds 0..200 [--twice --mutation M] docs/snippets/{settings,rewrite,lanes}.hl`; `RUST_DIFF_FILES=F scripts/rust-diff.sh`, `JS_DIFF_FILES=F scripts/js-diff.sh`, `scripts/mutations.sh --diff B:P:T`; `hl check examples/claude_code.hl`; `hl-agent run --instruction I --cwd W --model claude-haiku-4-5 --journal J`; `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh`.

## 2026-11-14: gaps-3-7-9: code cells across calls and stopped by a cancel, fork and rewind, hl-agent --fork-from and stall notices (gaps 3, 7 and part of 9)

Branch gaps-3-7-9, from master 37df067, merged from sub-lanes gaps-7-fork (ec244e9), gaps-3-cells (139a326) and gaps-3-stop (b2cb34e), then master b45e1d8. One design (fork as a session started from an entry checkpoint, a cell as a coroutine of its sites) was written first and the sub-lanes built it in the checker, interpreter, simulator, prover, generated Rust and generated JavaScript.

What it establishes:

- **Cells across calls (gap 3).** `waits NAME` on `code` adds nanocode's wait tool, `(cell_id: text, terminate: bool = false)`, reached only through dispatch (a direct call is refused, so `wait(c)` in a handler stays the sleep; the name `wait` is allowed). A cell is a coroutine of its sites: an exec or wait site whose live cell would sleep past `clock + yield_ms` on its earliest timer records `ok("Script running with cell ID N")` (`{"running": ...}` in the journal) and leaves the cell unfinished; a wait site replays the cell from its journal under the origin's `T/S/code-K` ids and runs it on; `terminate` writes the finished update and `ok("Script terminated")`; an unknown id `failed("exec cell ID not found")` (nanocode's texts, code_mode/mod.rs:971, 1140, 2329). Every return and trap writes a finished update for each cell still running before the status, as Codex terminates a turn's cells. Nothing about the yield is journaled but the site's result, so a crash before it reruns the site, which decides again. New invariant `cell_settled_at_turn_end` (and `cell_finishes_once` counts finished updates per cell across sites), new prover property of the same name with the lowering mutation `cell_leaks`, caught by `hl sim` and `hl prove` on all five targets. The engine folds the cell records it appends, so a wait in the same run sees them; the session folds accept a nested call under a Done origin site whose cell is unfinished while the turn's last site is open.
- **A cancel stops a cell (gap 9, first item).** `interruptible by cancel` inside a `code` block (cancel only; it needs `on cancel`). The turn takes its events after each external nested call returns, after each none call dispatched at delivery and after each timer wait; a recorded cancel stops the cell: no nested call starts after it, the cell gets its finished update and the site records nanocode's STOPPED, or STOPPED_UNKNOWN when the cancel arrived during an external nested call, which a recovery record lists unknown (agent.rs:3401-3402). A fresh site with a cancel pending runs nothing. `interrupt_honored` counts stopped results; mutation `cell_cancel_ignored`; the prover gives every interruptible cell site a stop arm.
- **Fork and rewind (gap 7).** `fork NAME at latest|turn` is an external tool keyed by its scoped `T.S`, like spawn; the runtime fills its journaled input `{discarded, origin, state, turn}` from the durable journal (`state_before(N + 1)`, as a side question's snapshot) passed through `on forked(origin: text, discarded: u64)`, which may assign, push, clear and keep but runs no effect and has no return. A turn that is neither 0 nor finished is recorded `failed("no finished turn N to fork from")` without contacting the host. The child's journal opens with `{"host":{"key":"forked","value":{"discarded":D,"origin":O,"turn":N}}}`, nanocode-turnlog's `Host` record so the oracle decodes it, and the entry checkpoint at revision 0, which every backend now loads (generated Rust and JavaScript refused it before). A rewind is a fork at an earlier turn, as nanocode's rewind writes a new session (claude agent/durable.rs:1640-1675). `fork_entry` is exposed by the interpreter, the generated Rust (`fork_entry(start, records, at, origin)`) and the generated JavaScript (`forkEntry`, Vercel `harness.fork`), so a host forks too and `on forked` needs no fork tool. New invariant `fork_state_equal`; mutations `fork_live_state` and `fork_hook_skipped`; a quarter of the seeds of a harness with a fork tool or `on forked` start from a forked entry, so every backend's load of one is diffed. A fork counts as one child session; a harness with blobs cannot declare a fork tool, and a parallel for is refused beside one.
- **hl-agent.** `--fork-from <journal> [--at T]` writes the parent's `fork_entry` records as the new journal's first records (a rerun finds them and goes on) and runs its instruction as turn 1; `--turn N` runs an instruction as a later turn of one journal, which gives a parent turns to rewind; a forked state that holds images is refused, since their bytes stay in the parent's journal. `examples/claude_code.hl`'s `on forked` disarms the cache refresh and, with `discarded > 0`, pushes nanocode's rewind notice. Stall notices (gap 9, last item): a held background job whose stdout and stderr stay the same size for `STALL_SILENCE` (45 s, nanocode's claude_bash.rs) and whose last line looks like an interactive prompt (nanocode's `prompting`) gets one non-done notice, "appears to be waiting for interactive input", with its last output and nanocode's advice.

Departures, kept:

- A yielded cell does not run between sites (only its timers move on), and STOPPED carries no partial output, since the engine reports output only at the end.
- Detached cells are not written: a trap stops its cells. A detached cell's later records would follow its turn's status, which every fold here and nanocode's turnlog Fold refuse, its header sits before the turn's checkpoint where every load starts, and recovery lists open calls only for unfinished turns (GAPS.md 3 has the smallest addition).
- A rerun after a crash may report STOPPED where the first attempt would have said STOPPED_UNKNOWN (recovery still reports the call unknown); on Vercel a cancel taken at the flush before an external nested call is handled after the call returns.
- Forks snapshot a finished turn, not nanocode's running-turn checkpoint; `on forked` replaces nanocode's keep of admitted tool ids and recovery notices; Cloudflare exposes no fork endpoint; the host record is stored with SQL kind `checkpoint` so the schema is unchanged; `hl emit` output changes for every program (shared runtime text), though no other journal or seed moves.
- In the simulator a program with `waits` advances the clock by up to `yield_ms` per model call (think time), since a yield does not sleep.

Measurements (5-core Hand shared with other agents, `nice`, release builds, claude-haiku-4-5):

- `hl sim --seeds 0..1000 --twice docs/snippets/code_wait.hl`: ok on all five targets, 3 to 4 s each; yields native-jsonl 162, native-sqlite 167, server-postgres 181, cloudflare 164, vercel 176. `cell_leaks` first failing seeds: native-jsonl 29, native-sqlite 18, server-postgres 10, cloudflare 28, vercel 10. `hl prove docs/snippets/code_wait.hl` about 170 ms, 3,329 prover steps; with `--mutation cell_leaks` it fails `cell_settled_at_turn_end` on every target.
- `hl sim --seeds 0..1000 --twice docs/snippets/code_stop.hl`: ok on all five targets; interrupted native-jsonl 81, native-sqlite 92, server-postgres 86, cloudflare 108, vercel 80. `cell_cancel_ignored` first failing seeds: 12, 31, 31, 23, 12.
- `hl sim --seeds 0..200 docs/snippets/fork.hl`: native-jsonl 978 turns, 315 forks; native-sqlite 273 forks; server-postgres 187 takeovers, 281 forks; cloudflare 286; vercel 369; `--twice 0..100` ok. `fork_live_state` and `fork_hook_skipped` fail `fork_state_equal` at seed 0 (native-jsonl) or 2. Hook proof 96 prover steps.
- Differential runs: rust-diff 0..2000 (code_wait, code_agent, code_tasks), 0..1000 (code_stop), 0..500 (fork) on the three Rust targets and js-diff 0..2000, 0..1000 and 0..300 on cloudflare and vercel match the interpreter. Plants caught by `backend_equal`: rust and js `cell_leaks`, `cell_cancel_ignored`, `fork_hook_skipped`, rust `fork_entry_ignored`. The Vercel runs found two interpreter bugs fixed on the way (a site-start preempt on a re-entered Open site, a stop recovery that flushed and so ended a step) and a latent Rust JSONL tail read that took the next cell id from the last cell line instead of the last header.
- Programs that use none of it: `hl sim` summaries (seeds 0..100 to 0..300, three or more targets), `hl check` and `hl prove` byte-identical for code_tasks, code_agent, agent, in_flight, tree, spawn and side against 37df067 and 3f21e2c.
- `hl check examples/claude_code.hl` after merging master: prompt handler 4,445,350 prover steps (unchanged by this branch: 4,329,878 before and after the hook on 37df067's file), hook forked 1,868.
- Real fork and rewind (`output/real-fork`, one workspace): parent turn 1 wrote notes.txt `codeword=heliotrope`, turn 2 (`--turn 2`) changed it to `marigold` (journal 42 records, 12,156 bytes; 4 requests, 2 tool calls). `--fork-from parent` (forked `{"discarded":0,...,"turn":2}`) wrote `marigold` to answer.txt: 2 requests, 1 tool call, 2.9 s, 7 input, 117 output, 8,816 cache-read and 213 cache-write tokens, the forked context reading the parent's prompt cache. `--fork-from parent/journal.jsonl --at 1` (forked `{"discarded":1,...,"turn":1}`): the request held turn 1's exchange and the rewind notice, and the model read notes.txt before answering (2 requests, 1 tool call, 3.6 s); its wording misread the state twice ("no codeword in my history", "your first message gave me marigold") although the request it was sent holds heliotrope.
- Real stall notice (`output/real-stall`): Bash with timeout 2000 ms on `printf 'Overwrite existing config? (y/n) '; sleep 300` moved to job 1.5; the stall notice was journaled about 45 s later and answered in a round of its own; the hold went on until the job ended: exit 0, 303.5 s, 4 requests, 1 tool call, 4,066 input, 207 output, 8,578 cache-read, 4,488 cache-write tokens.
- `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh` on 3f21e2c (gaps 3 and 7 merged, before the cancel and master): ok in 1,442 s.

Commands: `hl sim --target T --seeds 0..1000 --twice docs/snippets/{code_wait,code_stop}.hl`, `hl sim --target T --seeds 0..200 docs/snippets/fork.hl`, `MUTATIONS='cell_leaks cell_cancel_ignored fork_live_state fork_hook_skipped' scripts/mutations.sh`, `hl prove --target T --mutation cell_leaks docs/snippets/code_wait.hl`, `scripts/mutations.sh --diff {rust,js}:{cell_leaks,cell_cancel_ignored,fork_hook_skipped}:T`, `RUST_DIFF_FILES=F scripts/rust-diff.sh`, `JS_DIFF_FILES=F scripts/js-diff.sh`, `hl check examples/claude_code.hl`, `hl-agent run --instruction I --cwd ws --model claude-haiku-4-5 --journal J [--turn N] [--fork-from P [--at T]]`, `scripts/docs-check.sh`, `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh`.

## 2026-11-14: hl-agent on 40 Terminal-Bench tasks against nanocode and stock Claude Code; --effort, the kill fix rerun, and what the drops cost

What this establishes (branch eval-head, from master ca53d55):

- **hl-agent --effort high|medium|low** runs claude_code.hl's `set_effort` handler as its own durable turn before the prompt turn (turn N, then the prompt at N+1), so the effort is journaled like any other setting; a rerun on the same journal finds the setting turn already finished. No new invariant: it calls an existing handler. `evals/run.py --hl-agent-arg` passes such flags through.
- **System prompt, "# Finishing" block** (`agent/prompts/claude-code-system.json`): write each deliverable file as soon as a working version exists, then check the result the way it will be used (the exact command and paths the task names, a fresh shell, install a missing tool to check). Read from failures where hl-agent built the right thing but the check ran differently: sqlite-with-gcov exported PATH in its session only (the verifier found no `sqlite3`), polyglot-c-py never ran the Python half because the image has no python3 (nanocode installed it in 3 of 3 attempts, hl-agent in 1 of 3), and timeouts that left no output file (raman-fitting `results.json`, chess-best-move `move.txt`, db-wal-recovery `recovered.json`, pytorch-model-recovery `model.pt`).
- **evals** run stock Claude Code as a third arm (`claude-code`, 2.1.325, `claude -p --dangerously-skip-permissions --output-format stream-json`, `IS_SANDBOX=1`, the same meter and containers). Its native binary is a Bun single-file executable whose patchelf'd copy segfaults (exit 139), so it runs unpatched on the image's own glibc, or the musl build where `/lib/ld-musl-x86_64.so.1` exists (the nanoeval Alpine tasks); it ran in all 40 images. `setup.sh` installs both with `CLAUDE_CODE_VERSION`. The meter counts `/v1/messages?beta=true`, which Claude Code sends. `report.py` takes `DIR=LABEL` to name an arm and pairs every arm with the first.
- **The kill question.** Of the 37 Terminal-Bench images, 12 have a `kill` executable (adaptive-rejection-sampler, chess-best-move, configure-git-webserver, db-wal-recovery, extract-elf, git-leak-recovery, git-multibranch, polyglot-c-py, prove-plus-comm, query-optimize, regex-log, sqlite-with-gcov); the other 25 do not. The pre-fix binary enforced Bash timeouts only on those 12 (and there killed servers left running at turn end); on the rest a hung command held the attempt to the agent timeout, as pytorch-model-recovery attempt 2 of the second suite did (4 requests, then 740 s in one Bash call). With the fix, the 10-task rerun (`output/eval/fix`, 2 attempts) passed pytorch-model-recovery 2/2 (pre-fix 1/2), kv-store-grpc, pypi-server and headless-terminal 2/2, tune-mjcf 1/2 (pre-fix 0/2), gcode-to-text 1/2; cancel-async-tasks, polyglot-c-py, largest-eigenval and raman-fitting 0/2.
- **adaptive-rejection-sampler** cannot be scored on this Hand at effort high: both agents' first request thinks for about 590 s (34,135 and 38,930 thinking characters) and the stream is dropped (`IncompleteRead`), every time, in both reruns. Its hl-agent rows are errors ("no model request succeeded"), not failures.
- **Dropped streams.** Over the main and second suites 4,136 s of hl-agent's 22,966 s of agent time (18%) went to requests the egress dropped mid-stream (thinking deltas were arriving, so not idle timeouts); 66 of 2,453 requests. Both agents retry at once; the loss is the work in flight.

Effort pilot (hl-agent with the Finishing block, 40 tasks x 1, run side by side): high 30/38 scored (78.9%), $0.226 per attempt, 305 s; medium 31/39 (79.5%), $0.285, 277 s. Medium sent more requests (15.2 against 12.5) and cost more; paired, 4 tasks went to medium and 3 to high (sign test p = 1.000). high stays the default.

**The head-to-head** (`output/eval/head` for nanocode and hl-agent interleaved in one pool, `output/eval/cc` for Claude Code on the same 40 tasks, 1 attempt each, the same hour). nanocode c7679e3, hl-agent built at d6c75ed (the Finishing block, effort high), Claude Code 2.1.325, claude-sonnet-4-6, prices as in the eval entry. The Hand ran at load 20 to 33 on 5 cores during these runs (other lanes' builds and four eval runners), so wall times and the 900 s timeouts are not comparable with earlier entries.

| Agent | Attempts | Passed | Failed | Errored | Pass rate (95% Wilson) | Cost | Cost/attempt | Wall/attempt | Requests/attempt | Prompt tokens/attempt | Output tokens/attempt |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| nanocode | 40 | 27 | 13 | 0 | 67.5% (52.0-79.9%) | $11.48 | $0.287 | 333 s | 12.5 | 280,729 | 9,710 |
| claude-code | 40 | 29 | 9 | 2 | 76.3% (60.8-87.0%) | $11.63 | $0.291 | 258 s | 11.4 | 348,114 | 8,716 |
| hl-agent | 40 | 28 | 11 | 1 | 71.8% (56.2-83.5%) | $11.03 | $0.276 | 293 s | 13.4 | 250,324 | 9,595 |

Paired by task: hl-agent against nanocode 4 tasks to 3 (p = 1.000), cost -$0.011 (-$0.109 to $0.086), wall -40.5 s (-94.4 to 13.3); Claude Code against nanocode 5 to 2 (p = 0.453); hl-agent against Claude Code 3 to 5 (p = 0.727). No difference is outside its interval at 40 attempts. Over all of this lane's runs with nanocode in the same pool (the main and second suites, 100 attempts each, pre-fix binary), hl-agent 78.6% at $0.239 against nanocode 73.0% at $0.272, 11 tasks to 7 (p = 0.481).

Scorecard:

| Metric | Baseline | Current | Target |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| hl-agent pass rate, 40 tasks | 78.6% (100 attempts, pre-fix, nanocode 73.0%) | 71.8% (40 attempts; nanocode 67.5%, Claude Code 76.3%) | above every reference by 10 points at half their cost |
| hl-agent cost per attempt | $0.239 (nanocode $0.272) | $0.276 (nanocode $0.287, Claude Code $0.291) | at most $0.145 |
| Sign test against the best reference | none | p = 0.727 against Claude Code (3 to 5) | p < 0.05 |

The target is not met: hl-agent ties nanocode and trails Claude Code by 2 tasks of 40, inside the noise. Bets not run, for the spend cap: Claude Code's own prompt and tool set inside claude_code.hl (it reached the same pass rate with fewer requests), a verifier-in-the-loop turn before the final answer, and runs on a larger machine with 2 attempts per arm so a 10-point difference is detectable.

Losses and limits, kept:

- One attempt per task per arm; at this size only a difference of about 8 discordant tasks to 1 would reach p < 0.05.
- Claude Code runs on the image's glibc, the other two on the host's through the bind-mounted loader; Claude Code also sends its own system prompt (27,807 characters), tools and `max_tokens` 32,000.
- Spend in this entry: the kill-fix rerun $6.56, the effort pilot $9.45 (high) and $11.57 (medium), the head-to-head $22.60 and Claude Code $11.77, smokes and the adaptive-rejection-sampler rerun under $0.30: about $62. Dropped streams are billed upstream but the meter cannot count them.

Commands: `python3 evals/run.py --suite output/eval/suite-all.txt --tasks output/eval/tasks-all --agent-root R --agents nanocode,hl-agent --attempts 1 --model claude-sonnet-4-6 --parallel 4 --limits none --out output/eval/head` (and `--agents claude-code --out output/eval/cc`, `--agents hl-agent --hl-agent-arg=--effort --hl-agent-arg=medium`), `python3 evals/report.py output/eval/head output/eval/cc`, `python3 evals/report.py output/eval/p-high=high output/eval/p-medium=medium`, `AGENT_DRILL_SEED=1 scripts/agent-drill.sh`, `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh`.

## 2026-11-14: deploy: hl deploy, status, logs, rollback, secrets and destroy without wrangler or the Vercel CLI

Branch deploy, from master ca53d55. It establishes:

- **Deploying is part of hl.** `hl deploy --target T (FILE | --bundle DIR --name NAME)`, `hl status`, `hl logs [--follow] [--events N] [--minutes M]`, `hl rollback [--to VERSION]`, `hl secrets set KEY|list|delete KEY` (set reads stdin) and `hl destroy --yes` call the Cloudflare and Vercel REST APIs with `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`/`CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` or `VERCEL_TOKEN`/`VERCEL_TEAM_ID`, and install versioned copies for the native targets. `src/deploy/` stays std only: a JSON codec, SHA-1 (checked against its catalogued value), a multipart encoder, a WebSocket client (plain TCP, or `openssl s_client` for wss) and curl as the transport, its configuration on stdin so the token never reaches argv, bodies in mode 600 files in a mode 700 scratch directory that `main` removes. `hl deploy --endpoints` lists the 27 operations it calls, each by method, path and operation ID in a pinned spec: Cloudflare api-schemas 8b1a451 (`openapi.json`, sha256 214602bc…) and vercel/sdk fe01b47 (`vercel-spec.json`, sha256 f96d1077…).
- **Cloudflare.** One module upload (`worker.mjs`, compatibility date 2026-11-01 as the workerd oracle measures) with the `HARNESS` Durable Object binding to `HarnessObject`, the `LOADER` Worker Loader binding, `--var` values as `plain_text` bindings and `keep_bindings: ["secret_text", "secret_key"]` (as wrangler sends, so redeploys keep secrets set with `hl secrets`). The first upload carries migration `hl-v1` with `new_sqlite_classes`; the current tag is read from the script list, later uploads carry none, and a script at a tag hl did not write is refused. Status reads deployments, versions and secrets; rollback deploys an earlier version at 100% (the Durable Object's storage is not rolled back, and secrets follow the version); logs query Workers observability (`view: events`, filtered on `$metadata.service`, an ad-hoc `queryId`), `--follow` starts a tail and reads it over WebSocket with protocol `trace-v1`, then deletes it; destroy deletes with `force=true`.
- **Vercel.** The emitted harness, its workflow binding (the `WORKFLOW_BINDING` text of the emitted module, now also `emit::js::workflow_binding()`), a Next.js route that starts a workflow run or resumes a hook, `next.config.mjs` with `withWorkflow` and a `package.json` pinning next 16.4.6, react 19.3.1 and workflow 5.5.0 are uploaded by SHA-1 (`x-vercel-digest`), the project is created if missing, `--var` values become plain production variables, the deployment is created for production and polled until READY within `build_seconds_max` (2,700 s, Vercel's build limit) every `HL_DEPLOY_POLL_MS` (default 2,000 ms). Rollback is instant rollback; secrets are encrypted variables (deployments after the change read them); logs read build events, `--follow` streams runtime logs.
- **Native targets.** `.hl/deploy/T/NAME/versions/N` holds each emitted harness and its digest, `current` is replaced atomically (write, fsync, rename), secrets live in a mode 600 `secrets.env`, `history.log` records installs, rollbacks and secret changes, and `hl logs` prints it. Nothing is started: the host links the installed harness, as `agent/` does.
- **Ready for prebuilt modules.** `--bundle DIR` ships every file under DIR (sorted, at most `bundle_files_max` 15,000 files and `bundle_bytes_max` 100 MB, Vercel's limits), the main module named by `DIR/hl-bundle.json` (`{"main": ...}`), `.mjs`/`.js` as `application/javascript+module` and `.wasm` as `application/wasm` parts on Cloudflare; a module plus a fixed host ships this way. The bundle digest (sha256 over each file's SHA-1, size and path) is what status, the fake and the drill compare.
- **A fake of both APIs that checks every request against the pinned specs.** `oracle/deploy/fake.mjs` (node, no dependencies) routes by the specs' own paths, validates path, query and required header parameters, JSON bodies and the upload's multipart metadata against the operation's schema (undeclared keys, enums, patterns, lengths; required fields except read-only ones; read-only fields refused in requests), and validates its own answers the same way (write-only fields refused). It keeps the providers' state: versions, deployments, migration tags (a repeated class migration or a stale `old_tag` is refused), bindings and `keep_bindings`, secrets as new versions, uploaded blobs by SHA-1, a deployment's QUEUED, BUILDING, READY steps, and checks that every uploaded module parses (`node --check`) and that its relative imports are uploaded. The fake found three things on the way: Cloudflare's create-deployment body marks `id`, `source` and `created_on` required but read-only (now honoured as read-only), secret answers must not carry the write-only `text`, and an empty tail delete answers no `result`.
- **The drill and its plants.** `scripts/deploy-drill.sh` fetches the specs at their commits (hash-checked, cached in `output/deploy/specs`), checks every `--endpoints` line against them, then runs deploy, secrets set, a second deploy of another program, a secrets list, status, logs, `logs --follow --events 2`, rollback (the serving digest must be the first deploy's), secrets delete, three more deploys on the clouds (a new one, the same again, which must be skipped, and one with a changed variable, which must not) and destroy on cloudflare, vercel and native-jsonl, and fails on any spec violation or any listed operation it never called. Six deploy plants (`--plant`), planned in `scripts/mutations.sh` with a census against `hl deploy`'s usage and queued by check.sh: `migration_every_deploy` (cloudflare: "Migration tag precondition failed"), `secrets_dropped` (cloudflare, native-jsonl: the secret is gone after the redeploy), `digest_wrong` (vercel: the upload's digest does not match), `rollback_current` (cloudflare and native-jsonl: the serving digest is not the first deploy's; vercel: 409), `environment_type_unknown` (vercel: only the spec validator catches it), `fingerprint_partial` (below). `scripts/deploy-real.sh` runs the same flow on the real APIs when the tokens are set and destroys what it made; this Hand has none, so it skipped.
- **Fast deploys (raised target).** After the six verbs passed on both platforms against the fakes, the target became a cold deploy under 3 s end to end with one round trip per step. Each deploy now computes a fingerprint (Cloudflare: the bundle digest, the bindings, the compatibility date and the main module; Vercel: the bundle digest and the `--var` values) and writes it into the deployment (`workers/message: hl deploy <fingerprint>`, Vercel `meta.hlFingerprint`). Cloudflare reads the script list, the latest deployment and the account subdomain in one parallel round, skips the upload when the serving deployment carries the fingerprint, and after an upload enables workers.dev (first deploy only) and reads the new version in one more parallel round. Vercel reads the project, skips everything when production carries the fingerprint, uploads every file in parallel (at most `requests_in_flight_max` 6, HTTP/1.1 clients' per-host connection count) only for a new project, and otherwise creates the deployment first and uploads only the files Vercel reports as `missing_files` (content addressed by SHA-1, as the Vercel CLI does). New plant `fingerprint_partial` (the fingerprint leaves out variables) is caught on both platforms by the drill's new steps: a repeated deploy must print `unchanged`, and one with a changed `--var` must not.

Scorecard (`scripts/deploy-drill.sh --bench`: deploy minimal.hl cold, again unchanged, then agent.hl changed, against the fakes with 100 ms added to every answer to stand for an API round trip; 5-core Hand at load average 23 to 27):

| metric | baseline | current | target |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| deploy verbs passing the drill (deploy, status, logs, rollback, secrets, destroy) | 0/6 per platform | 6/6 on cloudflare, vercel and native-jsonl | 6/6 on both |
| cloudflare cold deploy | 1,674 ms, 5 round trips | 1,292 ms, 3 round trips (6 requests) | under 3 s, one round trip per step |
| cloudflare unchanged redeploy | 1,917 ms, 5 round trips | 370 ms, 1 round trip | |
| cloudflare changed redeploy | 2,362 ms, 5 round trips | 1,371 ms, 3 round trips | |
| vercel cold deploy (to READY in the fake) | 3,999 ms, 10 round trips | 2,519 ms, 6 round trips (10 requests) | under 3 s |
| vercel unchanged redeploy | 5,473 ms, 9 round trips | 243 ms, 1 round trip | |
| vercel changed redeploy | 5,036 ms, 9 round trips | 3,044 ms, 6 round trips (7 requests, 3 of them polls) | |
| deploy plants caught | 0 | 6 plants, 10 plan rows | every plant caught |

Big bets: content-addressed skips (done, the order-of-magnitude win: unchanged deploys went from 5 and 9 round trips to 1); parallel rounds (done); a fixed host uploaded once per account so a program deploy uploads only its module: on Vercel the content addressing already uploads only changed files, so a fixed host plus a per-program module needs no more client work; Workers modules have no per-file content addressing (only static assets do), so on Cloudflare it needs the host as its own Worker reached through a service binding, not built. A real Vercel deploy stays bounded by Vercel's own build (next build of the workflow app), not by round trips.

Real deploys: `scripts/deploy-real.sh` takes `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` and `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`, or, when only the account is set and `wrangler` is on PATH, the token `wrangler auth token` prints for the logged-in account (so a machine logged in with wrangler runs it without minting a token), and `VERCEL_TOKEN`; it destroys what it made. Not run here: no tokens on this Hand.

- **Docs.** README.md's Deploy section quotes `scripts/deploy-drill.sh --demo` (a fixed bundle in `docs/snippets/deploy` deployed, a secret set, status, a followed tail, rollback and destroy against the fake, deterministic ids and clock), which docs-check reruns; ARCHITECTURE.md has a Deploy section.

Departures, kept:

- Unverified against the real providers on this Hand: no tokens. In particular the Vercel project layout (route, `withWorkflow`, package versions) has never built on Vercel, the observability query's response shape and ad-hoc `queryId` follow the spec and Cloudflare's docs only, wss tails through `openssl s_client` are untested (the fake serves ws), and the fake's 409 on a rollback to the serving Vercel deployment is the fake's rule. `scripts/deploy-real.sh` is the check for all of it.
- The emitted Worker and Vercel routes have no authentication of their own; put Cloudflare Access or Vercel deployment protection in front of them.
- `iso_time` uses the coefficients of Hinnant's civil-from-days algorithm as bare numbers; the time units are named.
- `hl logs` on Vercel without `--follow` shows build events, not runtime logs, which the API only streams.
- `HL_DEPLOY_NOW_MS` and `HL_DEPLOY_POLL_MS` exist for the drill's determinism and speed.

Measurements (5-core Hand shared with other agents):

- `scripts/deploy-drill.sh`: ok, 32 hl commands over three targets, 27 of 27 operations called, no violations, 16 s wall (most of it node reading the 27 MB and 12 MB specs per helper call); each deploy plant caught in 3.0 to 7.8 s.
- Bundles: `examples/agent.hl` on cloudflare one 348,531-byte module; on vercel 5 files, 236,080 bytes; on native-jsonl one 266,817-byte harness.rs.

Commands: `scripts/deploy-drill.sh`, `scripts/deploy-drill.sh --demo`, `scripts/deploy-drill.sh --bench` (`DEPLOY_DRILL_HL` picks the binary; the baseline ran 13de204's), `DEPLOY_DRILL_PLANT=P DEPLOY_DRILL_TARGETS=T scripts/deploy-drill.sh`, `scripts/mutations.sh --census`, `scripts/mutations.sh --deploy P:T`, `scripts/deploy-real.sh`, `hl deploy --endpoints`, `scripts/docs-check.sh`, `nice scripts/check.sh`.

## 2026-11-14: openai-host: an OpenAI Responses host for codex_agent.hl, and an interrupted model reply that every backend read as rejected

Branch openai-host, from eval-head 972a3f2. It establishes:

- **hl-agent hosts codex_agent.hl against the Responses API.** `agent/build.rs` emits `examples/codex_agent.hl` as a third module; `hl-agent run --harness codex` (the default for a model whose name does not start with `claude`) runs it with `OPENAI_API_KEY` and `OPENAI_BASE_URL`. `agent/src/transport.rs` is the curl and plain-HTTP transport both providers now share, with a stop check every 50 ms (`STOP_POLL`); `agent/src/openai.rs` builds the request, assembles the SSE stream (`response.output_item.done` items in output order, then `response.completed`, `response.incomplete` or `response.failed`; an `error` event or a stream that closes before its terminal event is transient) and maps it onto the harness's `attempt`; `agent/src/codex.rs` holds the tools and `agent/src/codex_host.rs` the `Host`.
- **Stateless requests, decided.** Every request carries the whole input with `store: false` and `include: ["reasoning.encrypted_content"]`; the reply's text field keeps the output items (reasoning with its encrypted content, messages, function and custom tool calls), which the host sends back verbatim, without the item ids a stateless request may not name. `previous_response_id` was rejected: the journal is the session's source of truth, and a re-request after a crash, a compaction, a fork or a rewind must not depend on server-side responses that expired, were never stored (zero data retention) or belong to another branch of the session. Stock Codex makes the same choice over HTTPS (`core/src/client.rs`: `store: false`) and uses `previous_response_id` only on its WebSocket transport, as nanocode's Codex backend does. The cost of resending is met by prompt caching: `prompt_cache_key` is a per-session key kept in the journal's `state/cache_key`, and the instructions, tools and the AGENTS.md and `<environment_context>` preamble (kept in `state/preamble.json`) form a fixed prefix.
- **Errors and retries.** HTTP 429 and the codes `rate_limit_exceeded`, `slow_down`, `server_is_overloaded` and `policy_capacity_unavailable` are throttled, 408, 409 and 5xx transient, both with the server's `retry-after-ms` or `retry-after`, else a "try again in Ns" hint in the message (`codex-api/src/sse/responses_error.rs`); quota, policy and invalid-prompt codes are rejected; `context_length_exceeded` is an empty reply that stopped with `context_exceeded`, which the harness compacts once. codex_agent.hl retries five times (`DEFAULT_STREAM_MAX_RETRIES`), from 200 ms doubling (`async-utils/src/backoff.rs`), honouring the server's delay up to 60 s (`MAX_CONNECTION_RETRY_DELAY`).
- **Usage and cost.** The ledger records uncached input, cached input, output and reasoning tokens per request and, for a model the price table knows, `cost_nano_usd` from nanocode's standard and long-context rates (`crates/core/types-core/src/pricing/estimate.rs`: Sol, Terra, Luna and Astra, long context past 272,000 input tokens); `usage.json` adds `reasoning_tokens`, `cost_usd` and `priced_requests` when they are known, so Claude's file is unchanged.
- **Tools.** `exec_command` and `write_stdin` follow Codex's unified exec (`core/src/unified_exec/mod.rs`): yields clamped to 250..30,000 ms, empty polls to 5,000..300,000 ms, at most 64 sessions, 10,000 output tokens by default with Codex's middle truncation marker and header lines (`core/src/tools/context.rs`); a `tty: true` session runs under `script` when the platform has it, so the program sees a terminal and stdin stays writable. `apply_patch` is the freeform Lark tool with Codex's parser and its four-pass line matching (`apply-patch/src/parser.rs`, `seek_sequence.rs`, `file_update.rs`). `exec_command` and `apply_patch` are `external recoverable`: a fsynced start receipt before they run and a result receipt after, and an `apply_patch` whose added and deleted files are already in place is answered with its success text.
- **Steer and cancel.** `hl-agent steer --journal J --text T` and `hl-agent cancel --journal J` drop an event file into `J/control`, which `Host::inbox` delivers and `Host::answer` removes; a cancel stops the model's stream and kills a running command's process group. codex_agent.hl takes steers at every round, as Codex drains pending input before each sampling request, and answers a cancel with Codex's `<turn_aborted>` guidance.
- **codex_agent.hl** follows codex-rs 81b995a (sources in GAPS.md 10): the reply is `message(text, text, bool) | calls(list<call, calls_per_round_max>, text, text)`, the model has a 272,000-token window, a reasoning-effort setting and is interruptible by cancel, compaction keeps the user's messages through a `rewrite`, and the MCP listing is kept. Proved on all five targets.
- **An interrupted model reply was read as rejected on every backend.** The runtime journals an interrupted model as `{"interrupted":[]}`, but the interpreter, the generated Rust and the generated JavaScript decoded a zero-field variant only from a bare name, so the program's `interrupted` arm never ran: the turn took `rejected("model reply: variant 'interrupted' has 0 field(s)")`. The three backends agreed, so no differential run saw it, and `interrupt_honored` checks only the records; the fake API's cancel drill found it. All three decoders now accept an empty field list. New invariant `interrupt_decoded` (46 in all): every model reply journaled as interrupted decodes, through the engine's own decoder, as the interrupted variant. New mutation `interrupted_undecoded` (80 in all), planned on `docs/snippets/in_flight.hl` and failing `interrupt_decoded` at seed 5 on native-jsonl and vercel; differential plants rust and js `interrupted_undecoded`, each caught as `backend_equal`. Journals are unchanged.
- **codex_agent.hl's new shape moves one plan.** With a listing read every round on a turn that now also retries and compacts, `listed_class_dropped` on native-sqlite, server-postgres and vercel is first caught at seed 1 by `unknown_reported` (the rerun dispatches the external listed call whose record was never durable) before `external_once` sees the second dispatch; its plan now names both.
- **The fake Responses API** (`agent/src/bin/mock-responses`) serves scripted steps as the real stream does (`response.created`, `output_item.added`, text, argument and custom input deltas, `output_item.done`, `response.completed` with usage) and refuses, with the API's own messages, what the real API refuses: no bearer key, `store` not false, no encrypted reasoning included, an item id a stateless request cannot name, encrypted reasoning it did not issue, a call sent without its reasoning item, a call without its output or an output without its call, a custom call answered as a function call. Cached tokens follow the prompt cache's rules (the common prefix with the previous request of the same `prompt_cache_key`, at least 1,024 tokens, in steps of 128). Faults per step, each once: `throttle` (429 with a "try again in 1.2s" hint), `overloaded` (503 with `retry-after-ms`), `cut` (the connection closes mid-stream) and `failed` (`response.failed` mid-stream).

Measurements (5-core Hand shared with other agents, `nice`, release builds):

- `scripts/codex-drill.sh` (seed 1, in `scripts/check.sh`): 12 kill -9s (9 during a model stream, 3 during a tool), every throttle, overload, cut and failed stream retried, 3 cut streams requested again to a journaled reply, recovery answered 3 replies and 3 unknown, 9 external calls each dispatched once, every effect at most once, done.txt written, 15 requests, 70,016 cached against 1,076 uncached input tokens, $0.001008 at Luna's rates, 7.7 s of agent time; the control turns: a steer sent while a command ran reached the next request and the turn completed, a cancel stopped a 30 s command in 130 ms and killed its process group, a cancel stopped a 20 s model stream in 179 ms, both turns ending `cancelled_turn`. With `batch_flush_after_dispatch` planted, seeds 1, 2 and 3 fail with external calls dispatched 2 to 10 times. Before the decoder fix the model cancel ended the turn `failed` with the decode error above.
- `hl check examples/codex_agent.hl`: proved on all five targets, 507,528 prover steps, worst case 19,459 journal records and 9,728 outbound requests per turn (Cloudflare's limit is 10,000).
- `hl sim --twice --seeds 0..500 examples/codex_agent.hl`: native-jsonl 3,505 runs, 2,260 crashes, 76 interrupted; native-sqlite 2,858 runs; server-postgres 185 takeovers; cloudflare 100 interrupted; vercel 104 interrupted. `rust-diff` of `docs/snippets/in_flight.hl` over seeds 0..2000 matches the interpreter after the fix. The listing mutations planned on codex_agent.hl (`listing_unchecked`, `input_unchecked`, `listed_class_dropped`) are still caught.
- `scripts/agent-drill.sh` (Claude, seed 1) passes on the shared transport: 9 kills, 5 cut streams requested again.

Departures, kept: no real run yet (no OpenAI key on the Hand; evals/RESULTS.md says what to run); the gaps in GAPS.md 10.

Scorecard (hill-climb metric: codex_agent.hl's pass rate on the OpenAI host against stock `codex exec` on the same model and suite):

| metric | baseline | current | target |
|---|---|---|---|
| pass rate against stock codex, same model | not runnable (no host; no key) | not runnable (host done; no key on the Hand) | above stock codex at lower cost per task |
| fake-API drill: kill -9s survived without a repeated external call | none (no host) | 12 of 12, plant caught on seeds 1, 2, 3 | every seed of 0..40 |
| faults recovered (throttle, overload, cut stream, failed stream) | none | 4 of 4 | 4 of 4 plus a 5xx storm past five retries ending failed |
| cancel latency, tool and model stream | none | 130 ms and 179 ms | under 100 ms (STOP_POLL is 50 ms) |
| cached share of input tokens in the drill | none | 98.5% (70,016 of 71,092) | the same against the real API |

Big bets, ranked: one harness core shared by the Claude and OpenAI hosts, so a gap fix (compaction, steers, cancels, retries) lands in both; a code-mode variant for the catalog's code_mode_only models (GAPS.md 10, item 1); concurrent external calls in a parallel batch (gap 2), which Codex has and which bounds wall time on multi-call replies.

Commands: `scripts/codex-drill.sh`, `CODEX_DRILL_SEED=S CODEX_DRILL_PLANT=batch_flush_after_dispatch scripts/codex-drill.sh`, `AGENT_DRILL_SEED=1 scripts/agent-drill.sh`, `hl check examples/codex_agent.hl`, `hl sim --target T --seeds 0..500 --twice examples/codex_agent.hl`, `hl sim --target T --seeds 0..300 --twice --mutation interrupted_undecoded docs/snippets/in_flight.hl`, `scripts/mutations.sh --diff rust:interrupted_undecoded:native-jsonl`, `scripts/mutations.sh --diff js:interrupted_undecoded:vercel`, `RUST_DIFF_FILES=docs/snippets/in_flight.hl scripts/rust-diff.sh`, `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh`.

## 2026-11-15: stock Codex as a reference agent in evals, the meter's OpenAI route and the codex wiring drill

Branch eval-codex, from eval-head e378df9, master ce76f48 merged. It establishes:

- **A `codex` arm.** `evals/run.py --agents codex` runs `codex exec --dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox --skip-git-repo-check --json -m MODEL -C WORKDIR -c openai_base_url="<run>/openai/v1" INSTRUCTION` in the task's own container, with `HOME` in the row's journal, `CODEX_API_KEY=metered-by-host` (the variable `codex exec` reads; the meter puts the real key on) and `OPENAI_BASE_URL` set as well. Codex 0.169.0 reads the base URL only from `openai_base_url` in its configuration (codex-rs `core/src/config/mod.rs`), not from the environment, so the flag is what routes it. The same verifier scores it. The binary is the official static musl build, `codex-x86_64-unknown-linux-musl.tar.gz` from release rust-v0.169.0 (sha256 9af4d751…, 294 MB unpacked), so one file runs on glibc and musl images alike; `CODEX=1 evals/setup.sh` downloads it once, checks the hash and copies it into the agent root as `bin/codex`.
- **The meter relays OpenAI too.** `/run/<id>/openai/<path>` goes to api.openai.com with `authorization: Bearer $OPENAI_API_KEY`; every other path still goes to api.anthropic.com with `x-api-key`. `NANOEVAL_OPENAI_UPSTREAM` and `NANOEVAL_ANTHROPIC_UPSTREAM` replace an origin (an `http://` origin skips the proxy), which is how the drill puts a fake behind it. It starts with either key and answers 503 on a route whose key it lacks. Responses streams are read from `response.output_item.done` and `response.completed`, `incomplete` or `failed`; usage maps to the same record fields as Anthropic's: `input_tokens` is input minus `input_tokens_details.cached_tokens`, `cache_read_tokens` the cached tokens, no cache writes (OpenAI bills none), `reasoning_tokens` beside them; function, custom, local shell and web search calls count as tool calls. `run.py` counts `/messages`, `/responses` and `/responses/compact` as model requests, and asks for the key each chosen agent needs rather than always the Anthropic one.
- **WebSocket is refused, and Codex falls back.** Codex 0.169.0 opens the Responses API over WebSocket first (a GET with `upgrade: websocket` on `/v1/responses`). The meter answers 426, which Codex takes as the signal to switch the session to HTTPS streaming at once (codex-rs `core/src/client.rs`, `StatusCode::UPGRADE_REQUIRED`); measured: one refused upgrade per session and one error line on stderr. Request content is the same; each HTTPS request carries the whole input instead of a delta after `previous_response_id`, which the prompt cache absorbs. Relaying the WebSocket would mean decoding (and possibly inflating) frames to meter them; not done.
- **The other two OpenAI arms reach the meter the same way.** nanocode gets `NANOCODE_OPENAI_BASE_URL=<run>/openai/v1` (its Codex backend, HTTPS transport, lists models from `/v1/models` first), and `hl-agent` gets `OPENAI_BASE_URL=<run>/openai` for its Codex host. With a Claude model nothing changes for either.
- **`evals/codex-drill.sh` is the wiring check.** For each agent in `AGENTS` (default `codex`) and each nanoeval task it starts `evals/fake_openai.py` (a Responses API fake: the first request gets one shell call running the task's `solution/solve.sh` through whichever of `exec_command`, `shell_command` or `shell` the request offers, the next a final message; fixed usage of 1,000 input with 600 cached and 50 output tokens), runs `run.py` with only `OPENAI_API_KEY=fake-upstream-key` set, and `evals/codex_drill_check.py` requires: outcome passed by the task's verifier, exactly 2 requests at the fake carrying the meter's key and no `metered-by-host` anywhere upstream, no WebSocket upgrade reaching it, and the meter's totals equal to what the fake billed (2 requests, 1 tool call, 800 input, 1,200 cache-read, 100 output tokens). `report.py` prices gpt-5.5 (https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/models/gpt-5.5, prompts under 272K tokens: $5 input, $0.50 cached, $30 output per million) and counts a codex row's inner tool calls from the `item.completed` command, file change, MCP and web search events of its `--json` stream.

Measurements (2026-11-15, hosted Hand, 5 cores, load 13 to 41 from other work, `--limits none` since Docker here cannot apply cgroup limits):

- `AGENTS=codex,nanocode,hl-agent sh evals/codex-drill.sh`: codex 3/3 and nanocode (c7679e3) 3/3 rows pass every check; a codex row takes 2.6 to 2.9 s of agent wall time. hl-agent's Codex host (built from the openai-host branch on 2026-11-14) passes write-greeting and fails extract-todos and uppercase-message: their alpine images have no bash, and every `exec_command` answers "Failed to create unified exec process: No such file or directory (os error 2)", where Codex itself runs `/bin/sh -lc`. That is a host bug the drill found, not wiring, and the openai-host branch's to fix.
- Planted faults, each in a copy of `evals/`, each caught on all 3 codex rows: the meter not putting its key on (upstream authorization null), the meter not subtracting cached tokens (input_tokens 2,000, expected 800), and the meter relaying WebSocket upgrades instead of refusing them (7 upgrades reach the fake, 9 metered requests instead of 2).
- `python3 evals/report.py DIR=codex DIR=nanocode` on the write-greeting drill rows: both 1/1, $0.008 an attempt at the fake's usage, sign test p = 1.000.
- Not measured: a real model. No OpenAI API key is on this Hand, and the owner's machine (where a Codex subscription login in `~/.codex/auth.json` might be) was not attached to this work, so neither real run happened. A subscription login talks to the ChatGPT backend (`chatgpt.com/backend-api/codex`), not api.openai.com; the meter needs a third route forwarding that token and its account header before such a run can be metered.

Scorecard:
