## 2026-11-14: mpp: Machine Payments Protocol payments as journaled, proved, simulated effects

What it establishes. A harness pays for HTTP 402 answers with MPP: `wallet NAME spend at most C charge at most M payments at most K` and `paid by NAME` on a tool or a `tools from listing` (LANGUAGE.md, Payments). The plan, refined after reading nanocode d83b08f (`crates/integrations/mpp`: flow.rs's 402 loop, provider.rs's `PaymentProvider` and `PendingPayment`, protocol.rs's challenge, credential and receipt, mcp.rs's -32042, `bin/nanocode-sim/src/mpp.rs`) and nanocode-ts d5b21f1 (`src/machines/Payments.ts`, `Payment.ts`, `Wallet.ts`): in nanocode the 402 loop lives inside the HTTP client and no payment reaches the turn log, so a crash after a payment and before the call's result re-runs an idempotent call and can pay again. Here a payment is a journaled effect of its own. Decisions:

- The checker desugars every paid call (and every `dispatch` of a harness with a paid tool or listing) into existing IR: the first call; up to `K` rounds of a call of the wallet, a synthesized `external recoverable` tool `NAME(required: text, max: u64) -> u64`, and a retry with `payment` set to the 402's challenge text; the outcome rebuilt by one new pure expression, `Expr::Outcome`, from hidden state fields (`_paid_tag`, `_paid_text`, `_paid_value_T`, or the first call's own result when no round ran). The program's match arms appear once. One new hidden text builtin, `_with_payment`, sets the field in a dispatched call's input. So the prover, the oracle's class check, every backend and the differential runs see ordinary tool calls: the Rust and JavaScript backends needed only `Expr::Outcome`, `_with_payment`, the wallet's refusal in `tool_input` and the wallet's u64 reply at recovery.
- Recovery is structural: the wallet call is external and recoverable, so durable_before_external flushes its call record before money moves, recovery asks the wallet (`replied` with the units, `not_dispatched`, `unknown`) and external_once forbids a second dispatch on every target, including Vercel's retried steps and Postgres takeovers. An unknown payment reserves the whole room. This is the reserve/settle shape with the payment's call id as the idempotency key.
- The cap is proved, not trusted: the room is `min(M, C - spent)`, a wallet amount above the room is refused, `_spent` is checkpointed state and the checker adds `_spend_within_cap` (`_spent <= C`), which the arithmetic prover proves at every return and trap. `hl prove --mutation drop_pay_guard` removes the amount check and the proof fails on payments.hl, paid_lanes.hl and mpp_agent.hl on every target, while programs without a wallet still prove.
- Paid calls run in parallel lanes: the batch makes the first call and the lane runs the rounds after it (`ir::validate` admits a lane dispatch only when its input is `_with_payment`).
- The model cannot pay: the wallet's name refused as input that does not fit (`input of NAME: NAME is the harness's wallet; only a paid call's payment reaches it`) in the interpreter, the generated Rust and JavaScript. A model-supplied `payment` is passed through; a host attaches only a credential its wallet made for exactly that challenge text, so a model can at most re-present a spent credential.
- Session challenges are paid like charges (the voucher per 402); channels, settlement, receipts and paid model calls are GAPS.md's MPP list, ranked.
- No real funds: the owner's rule is that no MACH, Tempo gateway wallet or mainnet funds are spent, and no Tempo testnet path needs no one's input, so the end-to-end runs are the simulator, the differential runs and a kill -9 drill whose host plays a 402 server and a wallet.

Departures, kept: a recovered wallet reply is checked as u64 (every other recovered reply stays text, as before); hl-agent has no wallet (signing a Tempo payment needs secp256k1 and Keccak, which zero-dependency hl-agent would carry itself) and claude_code.hl and codex_agent.hl stay unpaid: nanocode pays inside the HTTP client of the model and of MCP, which here needs paid model calls (GAPS.md MPP 1) and a wallet host first; `hl emit` output changes for every program (a `WALLET` constant in the generated Rust and two runtime functions), no journal of a program without a wallet moves.

Scorecard (MPP parity items a program can write and run against the simulator; baseline 0): charge yes, session voucher yes, MCP listing yes, HTTP fetch tool yes, parallel lanes yes, cap proved yes, no re-pay across crashes, takeovers and retried steps yes (simulator, exhaustive crash enumeration, kill -9 drill), fork (`_spent` is checkpointed state, so a fork carries it) yes; paid model calls no, settlement/receipts no, channels no: 9 of 12. Raised target: 12 of 12 with settlement journaling the receipt once, and a family-wide cap.

Measurements (5-core Hand shared with other agents, `nice`, release build 9 min):

- `hl sim --seeds 0..1000 examples/mpp_agent.hl`: ok on all five targets (native-jsonl 8,991 crashes, server-postgres 635 takeovers and 402 fenced writes); `docs/snippets/payments.hl` 0..500: native-jsonl 908 payment-required answers, 478 payments, 11,683,227 units, 522 presentations; `--twice 0..200` ok on native-jsonl and cloudflare; `docs/snippets/paid_lanes.hl` 0..300 ok on all five.
- Mutations, every target, seeds 0..300: `cap_unchecked` caught (spend_within_cap at seed 11, or payment_presented), `spent_unaccounted` (spend_accounted, seed 0 or 1), `credential_dropped` (payment_presented, seed 0 or 1). Differential plants: rust and js `payment_unset`, js `paid_outcome_original`.
- `hl sim --exhaustive --depth 2 --seeds 0..30 docs/snippets/payments.hl`: native-jsonl 31,360 crash points (93,203 torn tails) and vercel 32,434, no violation.
- Kill -9 drill of the generated native-jsonl Rust for `examples/mpp_agent.hl` (`DRILL_FILE=examples/mpp_agent.hl DRILL_SESSIONS=150 DRILL_SEED=1000 scripts/rust-crash-drill.sh`): 150 sessions, 543 kill -9s over 693 runs, 1,016 payments in the ledger, none dispatched twice; 8 sessions of seeds 1..8: 65 kills.
- `RUST_DIFF_SEEDS=0..200` and `JS_DIFF_SEEDS=0..100` on mpp_agent, payments and paid_lanes: generated Rust (three targets) and JavaScript (cloudflare, vercel) match the interpreter.
- `hl check examples/mpp_agent.hl`: worst case 465 effects, 917 records (each round counted), 1,002,516 prover steps (debug build).

Commands: `hl sim --target T --seeds A..B [--twice] [--mutation M] F`, `hl sim --exhaustive --depth 2 --target T --seeds 0..30 docs/snippets/payments.hl`, `hl prove --target T --mutation drop_pay_guard F`, `DRILL_FILE=examples/mpp_agent.hl scripts/rust-crash-drill.sh`, `RUST_DIFF_FILES=F scripts/rust-diff.sh`, `JS_DIFF_FILES=F scripts/js-diff.sh`, `scripts/docs-check.sh`, `scripts/check.sh`.

## 2026-11-15: deploy-next: route auth, hl monitor, speculative first deploys, one curl run per round

Branch deploy-next, from master ce76f48. It establishes:

- **Routes are authenticated.** `hl deploy FILE` on cloudflare ships `hl-auth.mjs` as the main module: it re-exports `worker.mjs` (so `HarnessObject` stays exported from the main module) and answers 401 unless `Authorization: Bearer` matches the `HL_AUTH_TOKEN` secret binding, 503 when the secret is missing, before the harness's `fetch`. The Vercel route runs the same check against `process.env.HL_AUTH_TOKEN`. The comparison loops over the expected token's bytes only. The token is `auth_token_bytes` (32, 256 bits, read from `/dev/urandom`) as hex, created in the same request that creates the script (a `secret_text` binding in the first upload; `keep_bindings` keeps it) or the project (`environmentVariables`, type `encrypted`), and written to `OUT/auth-token` mode 600. A `--bundle` deploy ships as it is. New plant `auth_unchecked` (the check always passes) is caught on both platforms: the drill imports the shipped `hl-auth.mjs` and the Vercel route against stubs and expects 401, 401, 200, 503 for no token, a wrong one, the token in `OUT/auth-token` and no secret, and checks that the fake's provisioned secret hashes to the token in `OUT/auth-token`.
- **`hl monitor`** prints the serving version, the events of the last `--minutes`, how many failed (Cloudflare: level `error` or a `$workers.outcome` other than ok; Vercel: level `error` or status 500 and up) with the rate, and the latest `monitor_recent_shown` (10, a screen) turns, in one round on Cloudflare (subdomain, deployments and the telemetry query together). Vercel reads runtime logs for at most `monitor_follow_seconds` (5 s, caller policy: the endpoint only streams); curl's timeout exit (28) ends that read normally. On the native targets it prints status and the history. The drill checks `3 events, 1 errors (33.3% failed)` after a failed invocation on both clouds, and the README demo quotes it.
- **First deploys skip the lookups.** With no `deploy.json` in the output directory, Cloudflare uploads at once with migration `hl-v1` alongside the account subdomain read, then enables workers.dev and reads the new version in one more round: 2 rounds, was 3. A refused upload (the script exists at a tag) says so and falls back to the lookup path, which only an existing hl-v1 script reaches; a script without a tag is uploaded with the migration on either path, as before. Vercel creates the project with its variables and the token while uploading the files in the same round (5 files and the project in the 6 requests in flight), creates the deployment, follows its build events (`follow=1`) to their end, and reads it once: 4 rounds, was 6. A project that exists (409) falls back to the lookup path; a follow that fails or passes `build_events_followed_max` (2,000, the log events limit) says so and polls.
- **One curl run per round.** `Client::send_all` writes every request of a round into one curl config (`--parallel --parallel-immediate`, `next` between transfers, `%{urlnum} %{http_code}` per transfer), so a round starts one process instead of one per request; without `--parallel-immediate` curl waited to multiplex and ran the fake's HTTP/1.1 transfers one after another (4 rounds for 4 requests, measured). Threads are gone from `src/deploy/`. On HTTPS this also shares a process, its TLS setup and its DNS cache within a round; whether it multiplexes on the real APIs is untested.
- **The fake parses modules in process.** It checked every uploaded module with a spawned `node --check`, about 170 ms each at load 30, which the bench counted as deploy time; it now builds a `vm.SourceTextModule` (3 ms for the 348 KB module, same syntax errors; the drill starts node with `--experimental-vm-modules`). It also answers `follow=1` build events as a stream that ends once the build does, takes `environmentVariables` on project creation, exposes the hash of the provisioned `HL_AUTH_TOKEN` and takes `?level=` on invoke. The drill's clock moved to 00:30 so the telemetry window holds the drill's events (logs found 0 before).
- **The bench** takes `DEPLOY_BENCH_RUNS` (best of N, each run its own names), reports hl's own CPU time (bash `time`) and the load average.

Scorecard (`DEPLOY_BENCH_RUNS=5 scripts/deploy-drill.sh --bench`, 100 ms added to every answer; 5-core Hand shared with about 15 agents):

| metric | baseline | current | target |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| cloudflare cold deploy | 1,292 ms (load 23 to 27), 1,507 ms rerun at load 28; 3 rounds, 6 requests | 653 ms best of 5 at load 38; 2 rounds, 4 requests; hl cpu 38 ms | under 600 ms |
| vercel cold deploy (to READY) | 2,519 ms (load 23 to 27), 3,001 ms rerun at load 28; 6 rounds, 10 requests | 914 ms best of 5 at load 41; 4 rounds, 9 requests; hl cpu 59 ms | under 600 ms |
| cloudflare unchanged / changed | 370 ms, 1 round / 1,371 ms, 3 rounds | 384 ms, 1 round / 1,007 ms, 3 rounds | |
| vercel unchanged / changed | 243 ms, 1 round / 3,044 ms, 6 rounds | 366 ms, 1 round / 1,202 ms, 4 rounds (blobs already uploaded) | |
| cold round trips (cloudflare, vercel) | 3, 6 | 2, 4 | 2, 4 |
| routes needing a token | 0 of 2 platforms | 2 of 2 | 2 of 2 |
| deploy plants caught | 6 plants, 10 plan rows | 7 plants, 12 plan rows | every plant |

At zero latency a cold deploy still took 600 to 900 ms of wall time at load 30 to 40 while hl used 40 to 60 ms of CPU and the fake 10 to 70 ms: the rest is waiting for a core on an oversubscribed Hand (a bare `curl` took 95 ms there). The round count says what an unloaded machine reaches: 2 x 100 ms plus about 100 ms for Cloudflare, 4 x 100 ms plus about 120 ms for Vercel. The target is met in round trips and hl CPU, not in wall time on this Hand.

Not done: a fixed host uploaded once per Cloudflare account. The emitted `worker.mjs` is runtime and program in one module (348 KB for `minimal.hl`, almost all of it runtime), and splitting it means `src/emit/` writing the program as a module the host loads (through the `LOADER` Worker Loader binding it already has) or a host Worker behind a service binding that owns `HarnessObject`; neither is in `src/deploy/`. With that split the program upload becomes a few KB and the host is skipped by its fingerprint like any unchanged deploy.

Departures, kept:

- The speculative paths and `follow=1` are unverified against the real APIs (no tokens on this Hand): in particular whether Cloudflare refuses a first-deploy upload to an existing hl script in a way the fallback reads, and whether Vercel's event stream ends with the build. Both fall back loudly. `scripts/deploy-real.sh` is the check.
- `hl-auth.mjs` spreads the harness's default export and replaces `fetch`; other handlers keep their own `this`. It is checked under node against a stub, not workerd.
- A project or script hl did not create gets no token; its routes answer 503 until `hl secrets set HL_AUTH_TOKEN`.

Measurements: `scripts/deploy-drill.sh` ok on cloudflare, vercel and native-jsonl (13, 13 and 9 commands, 27 of 27 operations called, no violations); `scripts/mutations.sh --deploy auth_unchecked:cloudflare` and `:vercel` caught ("an unauthenticated request was served").

Commands: `DEPLOY_BENCH_RUNS=5 scripts/deploy-drill.sh --bench`, `DEPLOY_BENCH_LATENCY_MS=0 scripts/deploy-drill.sh --bench`, `scripts/deploy-drill.sh`, `scripts/deploy-drill.sh --demo`, `scripts/mutations.sh --deploy auth_unchecked:cloudflare`, `scripts/mutations.sh --census`, `scripts/docs-check.sh`, `nice scripts/check.sh`.

## 2026-11-15: fuzz-swarm: a generator that reaches the newer language, a rotated swarm and a regression corpus

Branch fuzz-swarm, from master ce76f48. The program generator (`hl gen`, `src/generate/`) only wrote the core language, so the differential runs of generated code never met the features added since. It now also writes `wait(c)` and `wait(e) at most c`, `truncate_middle`, `len` of a text, `contains`, `before`, `after` and `trim`, `clear` and `keep ... last`, `rewrite` with drop arms, tool parameter defaults, `on session start` and `on stop`, `before tool` and `after tool` hooks with `deny` and `allow`, and walks a reply's calls with a `match dispatch`, plain or `parallel`. Each feature has a named percentage in its module; wait durations come from Vercel's Function limit (`VERCEL_FUNCTION_DURATION_SECONDS_MAX`), so every program emits for every target. In 301 programs (seeds 0..301): rewrite 191, keep 213, clear 208, contains 102, truncate_middle 79, session start 79, stop 70, wait 60, after tool 56, before tool 40, parallel 21, bounded wait 12, a default in 300.

- **`scripts/fuzz-swarm.sh A..B`** generates the programs, runs `scripts/fuzz-one.sh` on each with `FUZZ_JOBS` at once, then `hl sim --exhaustive --depth 1` on every target (crash points), and prints programs, failures and programs per hour; failing programs keep their run directory. By default it rotates the Rust target by program number (one of native-jsonl, native-sqlite, server-postgres per program, all three across the population) and runs both JavaScript targets on every program (`FUZZ_ROTATE=0` runs all five). A Rust compile took 1.8 s of CPU but 6 to 40 s of wall time on this shared Hand, three per program, and was the bottleneck.
- **`scripts/fuzz-regress.sh`**, a check.sh stage: every program in `scripts/fuzz-regressions/` through `fuzz-one.sh` on all five targets, seeds 0..20; `hl gen --seeds 0..400` (every generated program parses and checks); `hl fuzz-prove --seeds 0..400 --sim-seeds 5`. About 35 s.

Bugs found and fixed, one commit each:

- **Generated Rust did not compile for `keep L last E` when `E` reads `L`** (`keep items last len(items) - 1`): `keep_last(&mut state.items, ... state.items.len() ...)` borrows the list mutably and immutably at once (E0502). The emitter now binds the count, and `keep ... after`'s first entry, before borrowing the list. Found by the swarm in 5 of its first 23 programs (all on native-sqlite and native-jsonl rotations); regression `scripts/fuzz-regressions/keep_reads_list.hl`, which covers both forms. A compile error needs no simulator mutation: removing the binding makes fuzz-regress fail at rustc.
- **The generator wrote statements after a `match dispatch` whose arms all return** (`statement is unreachable after a return`, a panic in `hl gen` at seed 1272). A walk's match now ends its block when every arm returns.

No divergence between the interpreter and generated Rust or JavaScript, and no simulator invariant failure, was found in the rest of the runs below.

Measurements (5-core Hand, load 30 to 57 from other agents throughout):

- Baseline, old generator, all five backends per program, `FUZZ_JOBS=2 FUZZ_ROTATE=0`: 15 programs in 15.8 min, 57 programs per hour, 0 failures.
- Rotated, `FUZZ_JOBS=3`, seeds 2000..: 84 programs in 22.3 min, 226 per hour; 5 failures, all the keep bug.
- Rotated, newest generator, `FUZZ_JOBS=2`, seeds 3000..: 33 programs in 12.8 min, 155 per hour, 0 failures.
- Interpreter only (`hl sim --seeds 0..20 --twice` and `--exhaustive --depth 1 --seeds 0..2` on all five targets): 300 programs of the newest generator in 478 s, about 2,250 programs per hour, 0 failures; 100 programs with 31 seeds in 130 s.
- `hl fuzz-prove --seeds 0..3000 --sim-seeds 10`: 3,000 programs, 1,514 proved, 150,000 runs, 375,000 turns, 37,015 checkpointed states checked, no unsound proof, 304 s.

| metric | baseline | current | target |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| differential programs per hour, all backends covered | 57 | 226 (jobs 3), 155 (jobs 2) | 500 |
| language features the generator writes beyond the core | 0 | 17 | every checked feature |
| bugs found and fixed | 0 | 2 (1 compiler, 1 generator) | |
| interpreter and crash-point programs per hour | not measured | 2,250 | |

Not done: `the eval machine` was not attached to this thread, so every shard ran on the shared hosted Hand. The generator still leaves out spawn and join, suspend, idle turns, side questions, fork, code cells, blobs, listings, model modes and settings, jobs and interruptible effects, `fn`, and the compaction hooks; those reach the differential runs only through the examples and snippets.

Commands: `hl gen --seeds A..B --out DIR`, `FUZZ_JOBS=N scripts/fuzz-swarm.sh A..B`, `FUZZ_ROTATE=0 FUZZ_JOBS=2 scripts/fuzz-swarm.sh 0..40`, `scripts/fuzz-regress.sh`, `hl fuzz-prove --seeds 0..3000 --sim-seeds 10`, `nice scripts/check.sh`.

## 2026-11-14: climb: hill climbing as a construct, a prover that proves it, and a real autoresearch run

Branch climb, from master ca53d55. It establishes:

- **The climb construct.** `climb NAME over FIELD from E ledger L end V cycles N [candidates N] trials N [cost B propose P trial T] [wall W] [failures N] metric ... [gate e] accept RULE { propose(base) { } measure(subject) { } }` (LANGUAGE.md, Hill climbing). The checker lowers it into ordinary statements over hidden `_climb_` state fields, so the interpreter, both generated backends, the simulator and the prover need nothing climb-specific; `yield candidate/score/cost` are checked per block. Every evaluated candidate is a `cycle` row in the ledger (14 fields, the last the candidate's gate failures), so a resumed run goes on from the ledger.
- **Five replay invariants and six mutations.** `climb_kept_valid` (a kept row ran all trials, failed no gate and beats its baseline under the rule, recomputed from the row), `climb_state_follows`, `climb_ledger_complete`, `climb_cost_balanced` (each row's running total is its run's costs and never passes the budget). Mutations `climb_accept_any`, `climb_revert_skipped`, `climb_ledger_drops_losses`, `climb_budget_unchecked`, `climb_cost_double_counted` and `climb_gate_ignored` are caught on all five targets in seeds 0..200 (`climb_gate_ignored` first fails at seeds 12, 12, 101, 124, 12; it needed `docs/snippets/climb_gate.hl`, since in `climb.hl` a gated candidate that would otherwise be kept never occurred in 300 seeds). The gate failure count is recorded whatever the climb then does, so a lowering that ignores the gate still writes the evidence the oracle reads.
- **The prover summarizes nested loops in one pass per level.** `examples/autoresearch.hl` and both climb snippets ran out of the 8M-step budget. A loop was analysed by an inductive pass per invariant round, a summarizing pass and the reporting pass, and an inner loop repeated all three inside each outer pass, so cost grew as about 3 to the nesting depth. Profiling showed joins at `if` and `match` took 99.9% of the steps. Five changes, all sound (each either drops facts or skips work whose result is discarded): (1) the inductive round that keeps every invariant is reused as the loop's summary; (2) under a quiet pass (inductive or summarizing) a loop skips its reporting pass, whose state was always discarded, and an obligation pushes its goal without trying to prove it, as it already did after a failed proof; (3) a join of branches that changed no quantity returns the state before the branch; (4) a join keeps facts both sides hold syntactically without an implication check, and hulls a joined variable only against variables it shares a fact with on some side, and each joined pair once; (5) a loop's per-iteration delta bound of magnitude U64_MAX is dropped as uninformative: such facts carried coefficients of 2^64 into Fourier-Motzkin and overflowed i128, which emptied a join's facts. With unlimited steps and only (1) to (4), `climb_noise.hl` still failed 10 obligations through that overflow; with (5) it proves.
- **Cost bookkeeping without hidden invariants.** The first lowering declared three invariants (each spent counter at most its cap) and charged a yielded cost as `if y <= max - spent`. Invariants make the prover run inductive rounds on every loop that touches the fields and check them at every trap point; dropping the `cost` clause took `examples/autoresearch.hl` from 1,917,052 to 311,783 steps. Now a charge is `if y > max { breach } else { if spent > max - y { breach } else { spent = spent + y } }` and the per-cycle check is `if C > B { stop } else { if spent > B - C { stop } else { ... } }` with `C` the cycle's cost at its caps, so every subtraction is of a constant by something proved smaller and no invariant is needed; `climb_budget_unchecked` is now caught by `climb_cost_balanced`'s budget check. The yielded cost became a `let` local and `propose_cost` folded into `propose_spent` (two fewer hidden fields per climb). The prover's join also stopped hulling a joined variable against the anchors of other quantities' loops (a loop's summary needs only its own quantity's anchor).
- **hl-climb, a real autoresearch host.** `agent/` builds `hl-climb` around `examples/autoresearch.hl`: `hl-climb run --goal FILE --cwd REPO --bench CMD --test CMD --model ID --journal DIR`. Claude edits the working tree with Bash, Read and Edit; its final answer is the commit message; `bench` checks a revision out in a separate worktree and parses the last line as microseconds; `failing` is 0 when the test command passes, gated to zero.

Departures, kept:

- Candidates of a cycle are evaluated one after another, not in parallel: a measure block benchmarks on one machine, and concurrent benchmarks perturb each other, and a parallel for's lanes cannot dispatch. Parallel candidates would need isolated measurement, not a language change.
- The join's related-pairs filter loses relations between variables that share no fact on either side; every program in examples/ and docs/snippets still proves with it.
- The climb oracle treats the ledger rows of the session's entry state as given and checks the rows the session appends: `--any-state` starts `examples/autoresearch.hl` from a ledger of arbitrary rows (evaluation 482011494 at seed 2), which no history produced, and the checker keeps the ledger append-only, so the entry rows stay a prefix.
- The climb's counters are state fields, since harnesslang has no mutable locals; the prover therefore carries them across turns, which is what the cost invariants are for.

Measurements (5-core Hand shared with other agents, `nice`, release builds):

- Prover steps per handler, `hl prove --target native-jsonl`, master ca53d55 then this branch: examples/claude_code.hl prompt 4,445,350 to 261,607 (17x), refresh 26,086 to 4,782; claude_code_child.hl delegated 1,560,948 to 143,122; docs/snippets/recovery.hl 347,033 to 43,389; reviewer.hl review_change 71,549 to 13,780; subagents.hl prompt 75,690 to 14,341; claude_agent.hl prompt 70,339 to 11,337; agent.hl prompt 4,202 to 1,632. All 49 programs of examples/ and docs/snippets/ prove, none that proved before fails. Climb programs, over budget (8M) before: examples/autoresearch.hl 1,917,052 after the prover changes, 1,218,062 with the invariant-free cost bookkeeping, 939,308 without the two fields, 824,587 with the anchor filter; docs/snippets/climb.hl 3,040,356, then 1,386,870; climb_noise.hl 963,914, then 664,601; climb_gate.hl 228,759; a minimal climb 828,134 to 133,341. `hl check examples/claude_code.hl` wall time 20.9 s to 17.8 s (the rest is outside the arithmetic prover).
- `rust-diff` seeds 0..60 and `js-diff` seeds 0..40 of climb_gate.hl and autoresearch.hl match the interpreter on all five targets.
- Real run, `output/climb-host/make-target.sh` (a trial-division prime counter, `count_primes(15000)`, 5 repetitions, median microseconds), claude-sonnet-4-6, cycles 6, trials 5, cost 120: exit 0 in 1,532 s, 81 requests, 135 tool calls, 51 input, 58,428 output, 280,741 cache-read and 68,741 cache-write tokens; the budget ended it after 5 cycles. Kept: a bytearray sieve (harness trial means 2,057,471 to 61 µs under `nice` and load), an odd-only sieve (64 to 33), `sum(sieve)` to `sieve.count(1)` (31 to 15); one cycle offered no candidate. Standalone afterwards: the baseline 345,014 to 514,688 µs, revision 4bb9f16 15 to 16 µs with the tests passing, about 23,000x.
- The fifth kept candidate added `lru_cache` to `count_primes`, which turns the benchmark's repeated identical calls into cache hits and reads 0 µs; its commit message says so. The climb kept it correctly under its rule: the benchmark measured what it was told to. A benchmark that runs each repetition in a fresh process or on varied inputs, or a gate on a cold-cache measurement, is what keeps a climb from gaming it; the language cannot tell.

Commands: `hl prove --target native-jsonl F` over examples/*.hl and docs/snippets/*.hl with master's and this branch's binaries; `hl check F`; `hl sim --target T --seeds 0..300 --strict [--mutation M] docs/snippets/{climb,climb_noise,climb_gate}.hl`; `MUTATIONS='climb_accept_any climb_revert_skipped climb_ledger_drops_losses climb_budget_unchecked climb_cost_double_counted climb_gate_ignored' scripts/mutations.sh`; `RUST_DIFF_FILES=... RUST_DIFF_SEEDS=0..60 scripts/rust-diff.sh`; `JS_DIFF_FILES=... JS_DIFF_SEEDS=0..40 scripts/js-diff.sh`; `hl-climb run --goal goal.txt --cwd real-repo --bench "python3 bench.py" --test "python3 test.py" --model claude-sonnet-4-6 --journal real-journal`; `scripts/docs-check.sh`; `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh`.

Scorecard:

| Metric | Baseline | Current | Target |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `examples/autoresearch.hl` prover steps | over 8,000,000 (fails) | 824,587 (proves on all targets) | under 1,000,000 met; raised to under 100,000, not met |

Big bets: (1) one quiet pass per loop and joins that skip shared facts and unrelated pairs (an order of magnitude on every multi-loop handler: 16.5x on claude_code.hl); (2) cost bookkeeping that needs no invariants (autoresearch 1.92M to 1.22M); (3) not tried: summarizing a whole climb as a closed-form bound per cycle, and parallel candidate lanes, which need isolated measurement and lanes that may dispatch.

## 2026-11-15: prover-loops: the arithmetic prover asks each question once, of only the facts it can touch

Branch prover-loops, from master ce76f48, with the prover part of branch climb (one quiet pass per loop, no proofs or main pass under quiet, joins that skip shared facts and unrelated pairs, no hulls against other quantities' anchors) cherry-picked first so the two merge. Every change is in `src/prove/arith` and `src/prove/linear.rs`; what `hl prove` and `hl check` print is byte-identical to master on every examples/*.hl and docs/snippets/*.hl file except the step counts.

Where the steps went, measured by tagging every Fourier-Motzkin step with its caller (scratch instrumentation, not committed): on master, `examples/claude_code.hl`'s prompt handler spent 3.47 M of its 4.45 M steps measuring hull bounds in joins and 0.86 M checking which facts of one side of a join the other side implies. Three changes:

- **A range is one projection.** `linear::range` pins a fresh variable to the measure and eliminates everything else once, reading the upper bound from the rows where the variable's coefficient is positive and the lower from the rest; before, `lower` was `upper` of the negated measure, a second full elimination. Joins, loop summaries, slopes and opaque operands take ranges.
- **A question sees only the facts connected to it.** `linear::connected` keeps the facts reachable from the goal's variables through shared variables (and constant rows). Dropping facts only weakens the system, so every bound or implication derived from the kept facts holds for the full state: sound always, and exact whenever the dropped facts are satisfiable on their own, since they share no variable with the rest. Ranges always ask the connected facts. A join's implication checks ask them alone (`implies_feasible`): both sides were checked feasible before the join, so the answer is exact. Every other implication (`implies_split`) asks the connected facts first and the full state when that fails, so a state made infeasible by facts elsewhere still proves its obligations as before. The product rule's single-sided `extent` still asks the full state, since there an infeasible state must read as a zero bound.
- **An if's branch reuses assume's answer.** `assume_settled` returns whether the alternative it kept was found feasible (or none was); `branch` checks feasibility again only when that is unknown (several alternatives, a condition the prover does not read, a budget that ran out, or a match arm).

Scorecard (`hl prove` over the 46 files of examples/ and docs/snippets/, steps summed over handlers):

| metric | baseline (master ce76f48) | climb's prover part alone | current | target |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| total prover steps | 6,878,201 | 572,300 | 231,494 | 687,820 (10x), raised to 68,782 (100x) after it was met |
| largest file (examples/claude_code.hl) | 4,473,516 | 267,059 | 91,801 | |
| examples/claude_code_child.hl | 1,560,948 | 137,836 | 54,493 | |
| docs/snippets/recovery.hl | 347,033 | 43,215 | 16,844 | |
| files whose proof output changed beyond step counts | | 0 | 0 | 0 |

The 10x target is met (29.7x total, 48.7x on the largest file); the raised 100x target is not. claude_code.hl's prompt handler now spends about a third of its steps in joins' hulls (28 k), a fifth in projecting each side of a join onto the kept variables (19.5 k), and the rest in join implications, feasibility checks before branches and assumptions, and obligations.

Wall time did not follow: `hl prove examples/claude_code.hl` took 27.7 s with master's binary and 25.8 s with this one, back to back on the shared Hand at load 46. The arithmetic prover is no longer what `hl prove` waits for; the next lever for wall time is outside the step count (the per-target durability and plan proofs), which this branch did not measure.

Not done: a convex-hull join that reuses the projected sides for all of a joined variable's measures (each measure still eliminates its side again), and feasibility that carries a known-feasible flag through states (it would touch every `State` constructor, which climb's branch also edits).

Commands: `output/steps.sh BIN` (scratch: runs `BIN prove` on each file and sums the `(N prover steps)` it prints), a per-file diff of `hl prove` output with step counts removed between master's binary and this one, `scripts/prove-mutations.sh` (every mutation caught), `scripts/docs-check.sh` (LANGUAGE.md's quoted step counts updated from rerun output: agent.hl 4,202 to 1,002, model_reply.hl 17,036 to 2,085, fork.hl's hook 96 to 40), `nice scripts/check.sh`.

## 2026-11-15: int-core: deploy-next, fuzz-swarm, mpp and mpp-host, climb and prover-loops on one branch

Branch int-core, from master ce76f48, merges deploy-next, fuzz-swarm, mpp-host (which contains mpp), climb (climb-exp is the same tree as climb's 560dad6) and prover-loops. prover-loops-climbonly's uncommitted change, no hulls against other quantities' anchors, is prover-loops' 3b2176d, so nothing of it was added. Conflicts were additive: payments and hill climbing each added IR fields, mutations, invariants, contextual words and a LANGUAGE.md section, and hl-agent's build emits both the paying and the climbing harness. In the prover, prover-loops' side was kept: it is climb's prover part plus its own changes.

Two faults appeared only once the branches met, each fixed in its own commit:

- **`linear::connected` panicked on climb's snippet** ("every reached variable is visited once"). Its loop allowed one visit per variable that a fact holds, but a goal can name a variable no fact holds; such a variable is visited too. The bound is now the fact variables plus the goal's variables, the most the frontier can ever hold.
- **hl-climb did not compile**: mpp-host added `Ran::Units`, a wallet's reply, which climb's host did not match. It answers a number, as hl-agent does.

The agent drill failed once at load 40 with no fault in hl: a kill landed on the `spawn_agent` call before the child's journal existed, recovery answered it unknown, and the drill accepted an unknown spawn only when an orphaned child journal was left behind. It now also treats a `spawn_agent` call the parent recovered as unknown as an unknown spawn and skips the child checks, as it already did for orphans; the next run (`AGENT_DRILL_SEED=1 scripts/agent-drill.sh`) passed with 12 kills, one child and one join.

Master moved to 90f1605 (the evals, Codex host and gap-mining lanes) and was merged in: hl-agent's build emits the paying, climbing and Codex harnesses together, `hl-agent` takes run, pay, steer and cancel, the Codex host answers a wallet's units as a number, and the pay path and hl-climb no longer stop leftover process groups at the end of a turn, since master's hl-agent lets them outlive it as nanocode does (`Workspace::stop_left` is gone). Mutations 89, invariants 53.

## 2026-11-15: eval-runner: run.py survives a row that raises and resumes; suite 1 three attempts against stock Claude Code

Branch eval-runner, from master 90f1605. It establishes:

- **A row that raises no longer ends the run** (`evals/run.py`). r5 (40 tasks x 2, three agents) died after 71 rows: `meter_totals` hit a `JSONDecodeError` in one row's `requests.jsonl`, `future.result()` re-raised it in `main`, and the pool shut down. `main` now catches any exception from a row's future and writes that row's `result.json` as `errored` with `crashed: true` and the exception's type and text in `note`; the other rows go on.
- **A rerun into the same `--out` resumes.** `run_row` skips a row whose `result.json` parses and has an outcome of passed, failed or errored and is not `crashed`; a crashed, partial or unreadable result reruns from scratch. Copying finished rows of another run into `rows/` therefore reuses them.
- **The meter fix from eval-context** (`evals/meter.py`, from 5c8a0a7): Claude replies now carry `output_tokens_details`, which sent `usage_of` down the OpenAI branch and recorded 0 cache read and write tokens on every Claude row. New rows meter correctly; rows already recorded are re-costed from `responses.jsonl`, which keeps the raw usage.
- **Suite 1 at three attempts, hl-agent, nanocode and Claude Code are level.** hl-agent 52/60 (86.7%, 75.8-93.1%) at $0.224 an attempt, nanocode 53/60 (88.3%) at $0.227, Claude Code 52/59 (88.1%) at $0.201, one Claude Code row errored. Sign tests: hl-agent against nanocode 1 to 2 (p = 1.000), against Claude Code 2 to 3 (p = 1.000); Claude Code against nanocode 4 to 4. hl-agent makes 3.3 more requests an attempt than nanocode (1.0 to 5.6) at the same cost. The table is in evals/RESULTS.md.
- **The spend watchdog read the broken meter.** It stopped the run at $25 of metered spend for the 121 new rows and read $12.78 at the end; re-costed from `responses.jsonl` the new rows cost $27.27 (the 59 copied r5 rows $11.81), so the cap was exceeded by $2.27 without tripping.

Measurements: 180 rows, 59 from r5 attempts 1 and 2 and 121 new, 06:00 to 07:47 UTC on the shared 5-core Hand with eight other lanes (1-minute load average 14 to 26), parallel 4, `nice`. No row crashed in this run.

Commands: `python3 evals/run.py --suite evals/suite.txt --tasks T --agent-root R --agents nanocode,hl-agent,claude-code --attempts 3 --model claude-sonnet-4-6 --parallel 4 --limits none --ca-bundle /etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt --meter-port 18830 --relay-port 13230 --out output/eval/s1x3` under `nice`, with the r5 suite 1 rows of attempts 1 and 2 copied into `output/eval/s1x3/rows` first; a copy of the run with each row's meter token fields recomputed from `responses.jsonl` through `usage_of` into `output/eval/s1x3r`; `python3 evals/report.py output/eval/s1x3r`.
## 2026-11-15: eval-cost: where hl-agent's dollars go, effort low by default, and a meter that dropped Anthropic cache tokens

Branch eval-cost, from master 90f1605. It establishes:

- **Where the cost goes.** From the meter's records of the eval-head head-to-head (40 hl-agent attempts, effort high, $0.277 an attempt): output tokens $0.144 (52%), cache reads $0.071 (26%), cache writes $0.062 (22%), uncached input $0.000. 9,595 output tokens an attempt, about three quarters of them thinking; 235,089 cache-read and 16,616 cache-written tokens over 13.5 requests. Thinking stays in the context for the rest of a tool loop (regex-log's first reply thought 17,797 tokens and the next request's prompt grew by 17,835), so it is also written once and read on every later request. Over 200 attempts (head, main, second, fix, p-high): failed attempts cost $0.41 against $0.21 for passed ones (41% of spend); requests of more than 5,000 output tokens are 61 of 2,500 but 33% of all output tokens.
- **Bets measured from the records and not taken.** Clearing earlier thinking (`clear_thinking_20251015` keeping the last turn) would save $0.016 of reads an attempt and cost $0.017 of rewrites, since each clear moves the cache prefix. Cache misses are rare: 250 tokens an attempt are written beyond the context's growth (11 events in 200 attempts). Tool results are 40,063 characters of an attempt's final context, but 81% of that is results over 8,000 characters, almost all base64 PNGs from Read, which are billed by pixels; Bash results over 8,000 characters are 5,400 characters an attempt. The fixed prefix (system prompt and tools, about 4,000 tokens) is read for $0.0012 a request. None of these reaches half the cost; thinking does.
- **Effort low by default** (`examples/claude_code.hl`: `effort_level` lists `"low"` first, so the state field starts there, and the settings default is `"low"`; `agent/src/anthropic.rs`: `EFFORT`, which child sessions use, is `"low"`). `hl-agent --effort high|medium` still selects the others as a journaled turn. On 10 Terminal-Bench tasks x 3 (regex-log, sqlite-db-truncate, vulnerable-secret, extract-elf, fix-code-vulnerability, headless-terminal, multi-source-data-merger, build-pmars, prove-plus-comm, pytorch-model-recovery), against the same tasks' effort-high rows of the eval-head lane's three-attempt run (r3, built at e378df9, the same harness):

| Arm | Attempts | Passed | Cost/attempt | Output | Reads | Writes | Requests | Output tokens | Read tokens | Write tokens |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| high (r3) | 30 | 30 | $0.245 | $0.151 | $0.037 | $0.057 | 11.2 | 10,069 | 124,632 | 15,092 |
| medium (p-medium, 1 attempt) | 10 | 10 | $0.143 | $0.084 | $0.026 | $0.033 | 10.4 | 5,610 | 87,086 | 8,717 |
| low (this lane) | 30 | 29 | $0.090 | $0.044 | $0.019 | $0.025 | 8.8 | 2,961 | 62,193 | 6,657 |

  Low cuts thinking and with it every term: fewer output tokens, less context to write once and read on each request, and fewer requests (the model does not deliberate between tool calls it would make anyway). The failed low attempt is pytorch-model-recovery attempt 2, which timed out at 900 s at a load average of 12 to 14.
- **Regression sample** (the new default build, no `--effort` flag, every request carried `effort: low`): fix-git, git-leak-recovery, kv-store-grpc, log-summary-date-ranges, nginx-request-logging, openssl-selfsigned-cert (1 attempt each) and pypi-server, hf-model-inference (2 each), all passing at high before: 10 of 10, $0.045 an attempt. Harder tasks, 2 attempts each: cobol-modernization 2 of 2 (high 3 of 3), count-dataset-tokens 0 of 2 (high 1 of 3), git-multibranch 0 of 1 (high 2 of 3), polyglot-c-py 0 of 2 (high 3 of 4); pytorch-model-cli errored when the disk filled. On those three tasks low passed 0 of 5 where high passed 6 of 10: low may cost pass rate where a task needs deliberate checking (polyglot-c-py ended after 4 requests without running the Python half; git-multibranch's push check failed as before; count-dataset-tokens gave 79566 as before), which 5 attempts cannot settle. The full-suite rerun at the new default is what decides it; `--effort medium` is the fallback the table supports at $0.143.
- **The meter dropped every Anthropic cache token since the API added `output_tokens_details`** (`evals/meter.py` `usage_of`; master's eval-runner entry, 0734074, fixed the same condition the same way while this lane ran, and the merge keeps one copy). The OpenAI branch was chosen whenever `output_tokens_details` appeared, and Anthropic replies now carry `output_tokens_details: {thinking_tokens}`, so rows written from about 04:20 UTC (the int-evals r5 run among them) record 0 cache reads and writes and understate cost by roughly half. The Anthropic shape is now recognised by its cache fields, and `thinking_tokens` is recorded as `reasoning_tokens` (also summed into each row's meter). `evals/remeter.py RUN_DIR...` rebuilds every row's usage and `result.json` meter from its `responses.jsonl`, which kept the raw usage; `meter.py` and `run.py` run `main()` only as scripts so it can import them. Checked on an r5 row: 0 cache reads before, 13,193 after, the same as the raw usage.

Against the eval-runner entry's fresh suite-1 baseline (20 tasks x 3 at effort high, the fixed meter: hl-agent $0.224 an attempt and 11.7 requests, nanocode $0.227 and 8.4, Claude Code $0.201 and 9.1), effort low is the lever that moves hl-agent below every reference; its own request count on the 10 tasks fell from 11.2 to 8.8.

Spend (metered, claude-sonnet-4-6): the low arm $2.69 (30 attempts), the regression and harder-task runs $0.40 and $1.47, a run whose meter port collided with another lane's (every row 404, then stopped) $0.18; $4.74 in all.

## 2026-11-15: eval-rerun: suite 1 at three attempts with hl-agent at effort low and medium; low stays the default

Branch eval-rerun, from master 3679d15. It establishes:

- **Effort low is the default on suite 1, not only on the 10 cost tasks.** hl-agent at its new default passed 57/60 (95.0%, 86.3-98.3%) at $0.082 an attempt, 64 s and 8.6 requests; at `--effort medium` 53/60 (88.3%, 77.8-94.2%) at $0.172, 132 s and 10.9 requests. Against the eval-runner entry's references on the same tasks and attempts: nanocode 53/60 at $0.227, Claude Code 52/59 at $0.201. Sign tests: low against nanocode 6 to 2 (p = 0.289), against Claude Code 6 to 2 (p = 0.289), against medium 5 to 1 (p = 0.219); medium against nanocode 3 to 3 and against Claude Code 3 to 3 (p = 1.000). No pass-rate difference is significant at 60 attempts; the cost differences are: low is $0.144 an attempt below nanocode (95% interval $0.082 to $0.206) and $0.090 below medium ($0.047 to $0.133). Medium buys no pass rate here: its extra thinking lengthens the tool loop (2.3 more requests an attempt) and the context every later request reads (84k more prompt tokens an attempt), and two of its attempts on largest-eigenval ran into the 900 s timeout, where low had none.
- **The eval-cost lane's worry about hard tasks does not hold on this suite.** There low passed polyglot-c-py 0 of 2; here low passed it 2 of 3 and medium 2 of 3 (nanocode 3 of 3). cancel-async-tasks is the task most sensitive to effort: low 2 of 3, medium 0 of 3, nanocode 1 of 3, Claude Code 0 of 3. The tables are in evals/RESULTS.md.
- **`evals/run.py` runs a variant of an agent as its own arm.** `AGENTS` gains `hl-agent-medium`, which runs the `hl-agent` binary (`AGENT_BINARIES`) with `--effort medium` (`AGENT_ARGS`), so one run carries both efforts with row ids that do not collide. Rows are ordered task-major, tasks by agent timeout longest first, so the slow tasks start first and attempts of one task run side by side.
- **`evals/report.py` compares every pair of arms** (it compared only nanocode against each other arm) and lists each pair's discordant rows by task and attempt; an `hl-agent-*` variant takes its tool calls from `usage.json` as `hl-agent` does.

Measurements: 120 new rows (hl-agent and hl-agent-medium, 20 tasks x 3), 08:24 to about 09:25 UTC on the shared 5-core Hand with eight other lanes (1-minute load average 17 to 25), parallel 4, `nice`; no row errored or crashed. The 120 nanocode and Claude Code rows are copied from the eval-runner lane's `output/eval/s1x3r`. Spend (metered, claude-sonnet-4-6, the fixed meter): $15.30 for the 120 new rows ($4.94 low, $10.33 medium), under a watchdog that would stop the run at $30.

Commands: `cargo build --release` at the root and in `agent/`, the binary patched with setup.sh's patchelf into an agent root with root-r4's lib; `nice -n 10 python3 evals/run.py --suite evals/suite.txt --tasks /home/hand/wt/eval-head/output/eval/tasks-all --agent-root output/eval/root --agents hl-agent,hl-agent-medium --attempts 3 --model claude-sonnet-4-6 --parallel 4 --limits none --ca-bundle /etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt --meter-port 19110 --relay-port 14110 --out output/eval/s1r`, with the s1x3r nanocode and Claude Code rows copied into `output/eval/s1r/rows` first; `python3 evals/report.py output/eval/s1r`.

## 2026-11-15: eval-confirm runs: suite 60 x 3 on Sonnet, then suites 1+2 x 2 on Haiku, under a disk and spend watchdog

Branch eval-confirm, from 3c6d6b3. It establishes:

- **Every suite-3 task built here; none was replaced.** All 20 images are prebuilt on Docker Hub (0.03 to 1.27 GB compressed, custom-memory-heap-crash 6 GB unpacked) and pulled without error; none asks for more than 4 CPUs or 8192 MB (caffe-cifar-10 asks for exactly 4 and 8192), so the replacement rule never fired.
- **`evals/run.py` holds new rows while a pause file exists** (`PAUSE_FILE`, polled every `PAUSE_POLL_SEC`): `run_row` waits before starting a row when `<out>/pause` is present, so a watchdog can lower the effective parallelism to 0 without killing rows in flight. The watchdog (scratch, output/eval/watch.py) writes the file when `df /home/hand` reaches 94% and removes it at 91%, deletes each finished row`s journal/home tree (journal/hl too at 88% and above), removes only this lane`s exited containers and, under disk pressure, only suite-3 images this lane pulled after all their rows finished; it also writes the pause at $116 of real spend and kills this lane`s driver at $120. Real spend is each new row`s responses.jsonl through `meter.usage_of` and `report.cost`, as evals/remeter.py computes it; the 180 copied suite-1 rows are not counted.
- **Order and parallelism.** The run started at `--parallel 3` over suite 60 (task-major, longest agent timeout first, so build-pov-ray at 12000 s and the 3600 s suite-3 tasks led). At 12:20 UTC, on the lead`s request, it was restarted at `--parallel 5` with suites 1+2 first (their images were cached; every suite-1 row is copied, so this runs suite 2), then suite 60 for the rest of suite 3, then the Haiku pass. The three caffe-cifar-10 rows in flight at the restart were killed; their $1.67 of spend is counted in the cap and the rows reran.
- **Upstream drops error rows, not failures.** Claude Code rows whose every request ended in a reset connection from the upstream (`ConnectionResetError` in the meter, status 0) retried until the 900 s timeout and were scored errored ("no model request succeeded"); report.py leaves them out of pass rates and lists them.
- **Results** are in evals/RESULTS.md under "eval-confirm", regenerated from snapshots of finished rows (remetered) at every 150 new rows and at the end: per suite and overall Wilson intervals, real cost per attempt, paired sign tests for every pair of agents and the discordant rows (per-task losses) of each pair.

## 2026-11-14: wasm: the compiler writes WebAssembly modules, every program lowers, one fixed host per platform

Branch wasm, from master ca53d55, merged from sub-lanes wasm-interp, wasm-events, wasm-blobs, wasm-cells2, wasm-hosts, wasm-jobs (with wasm-spawn) and wasm-speed. It establishes:

- **`hl emit` writes a WebAssembly module by default.** `--format wat` prints its text and `--format source` the generated Rust or JavaScript, which hl-agent's build, the Rust and JavaScript differential runs and the source drills still use. The compiler writes the bytes itself (`src/emit/wasm/assemble.rs`, `encode.rs`): a shared runtime in `src/emit/wasm/runtime/*.wat` (22 files) plus the program's data tables (`data.rs`) and one function per handler (`lower.rs`). The assembler keeps only the functions, globals and data an export reaches. The CRC-32 table is a data segment the compiler writes from `run::crc::TABLE`, not a loop at every run.
- **Every program lowers.** All 46 programs in examples/ and docs/snippets/ (model modes, settings, declared replies and windows; rewrite; parallel for with lanes, as a batch request the host completes in its own order; fork tools, `on forked` and forked entries; events, cancel, steer, take, wait, hold, suspend, deny and side questions; idle turns; text builtins, blobs, listings and search tools; code cells with waits, cancels and nested recovery; interruptible effects, background jobs, recoverable tools and spawn tools) match the interpreter on all five targets: `scripts/wasm-diff.sh` compares every hook, flush (byte for byte), tool, model and world request and every exit with `hl sim --trace`. `scripts/check.sh` runs it for every program on every target, on the node host (`scripts/wasm-sim.mjs`) and on the native host (`hl wasm-sim`, a std-only WebAssembly interpreter in `src/wasm/`); its census fails when a program that lowers had no wasm run.
- **One generic import.** Besides the durability points (flush, hook, attempt, acknowledge) and effects (tool, model, now, random), a module reaches the world through one `hl.world` import taking a JSON request (batch, events, waits, listings, jobs, recover, sides, cells). The hook-kind table is 0 tool, 1 dispatch, 2 model, 3 now, 4 random, 5 listing, 6 cell, 7 wait, 8 hold, 9 suspend, 10 deny.
- **Fixed platform hosts.** `hosts/cloudflare.mjs` (a Durable Object) and `hosts/vercel.mjs` run any program's module, using JSPI (`WebAssembly.Suspending`/`promising`, on by default in workerd 2026-11-07 and node 24.22) for their async imports. `CLOUDFLARE_FORMAT=wasm scripts/cloudflare-drill.sh` runs the examples through the fixed host in real workerd in trace and kill modes and catches all seven drill mutations; `WASM_DIFF_HOST=platform` diffs the fixed hosts against the interpreter, and `scripts/host-mutations.sh` catches three Vercel host mutations. The fixed Vercel host refuses programs that take events, whose steps the generated JavaScript splits.
- **Planted divergences.** `PLANTS` in `src/emit/wasm/mod.rs` (36) are text substitutions in the runtime; each has a `wasm` plan in `scripts/mutations.sh` and is caught by `backend_equal` in seeds 0..100 on every target it lists, with an exemption and its reason elsewhere. On the way the lane found a runtime bug its own diff could not see: `$u64_multiply` divided by a zero left operand (`i32.and` evaluates both sides), so `0 * x` crashed the module.

Departures, kept: the source emitters stay, since hl-agent is built from the generated Rust and the source drills (crash, SQL, cloudflare source mode, js-mutations) test them; a fixed Vercel host for programs with events needs the step split the generated JavaScript does; tool requests from a module carry no `returns` or `nested` fields, which only a real platform host would use.

Scorecard (metric, baseline at a9b0fd0, now, target):

- Programs that lower and match on all five targets: 6 of 46, 46 of 46, 46 of 46 (raised: delete the source emitters once hl-agent runs a module).
- Module bytes (cloudflare, examples): about 21 to 23 KB for the six programs then, now 63.7 KB (minimal) to 90.8 KB (claude_code) with every feature and the host exports; the generated JavaScript is 347.5 to 402.0 KB and the Rust 264.5 to 356.5 KB; target 16 KB (bet: stub the runtime of features a program does not use; dead-code elimination alone took minimal from 44.1 to 39.9 KB before the host exports).
- Native host run time against node's (native-jsonl, 30 seeds, min of 3 runs at load average about 24): about 8 to 9 times at the start of wasm-interp; now minimal 557 against 168 ms (3.3 times), agent 1,677 against 329 ms (5.1 times); target 1.5 times. Wins kept: a register IR in `src/wasm/` (locals and stack positions are frame slots, `local.get` and constants are read in place, `local.set` retargets the op that made the value, compares fuse into jumps; 16-byte ops) and inlining of straight-line leaf functions of at most `INLINE_BYTES_MAX` (64) bytes, with constant folding.
- Ops per run on the native host (native-jsonl, 30 seeds): minimal 451,911 at the start, 432,525 after the CRC-32 table became a data segment, 343,785 with the register IR and inlining; agent 1,060,602 to 833,184; images 8,881,086 to 4,014,190 with the register IR, then 974,210 once `$base64_sextet` reads a 256-byte table the compiler writes instead of scanning the alphabet (9.1 times fewer in all; images run time native 1,667 ms against node 168 ms). The profile now puts `$crc32` (12 ops per byte), JSON parsing and `$alloc` first.

Measurements (5-core Hand shared with several agents at load average 10 to 35, so timings are noisy):

- Node host, 100 seeds, native-jsonl: minimal 1,040 runs in 909.5 ms, instantiate 346 µs per run; agent 1,220 runs in 2,221 ms; claude_code 913 runs in 7,036 ms. Cloudflare: minimal 963 runs in 558 ms.
- workerd (oracle/cloudflare/bench.mjs, 60 turns, two rounds): deploy bytes minimal 347,547 source against 88,788 for host plus module; steady turn medians 17.1/16.1 ms source against 11.5/23.9 ms wasm (minimal), bound by storage sync.
- `scripts/mutations.sh --diff` for every wasm plan: 119 plan-target pairs caught before the cells and jobs merges, then each new plant on its targets.

Commands: `hl emit --target T [--format wasm|wat|source] [--plant P] F`; `WASM_DIFF_FILES=F WASM_DIFF_TARGETS=T WASM_DIFF_SEEDS=0..300 [WASM_DIFF_HOST=native|platform] [WASM_DIFF_SCOPE=1.1] [WASM_DIFF_PLANT=P] scripts/wasm-diff.sh`; `hl wasm-sim --target T --module M --trace F`; `scripts/mutations.sh --diff wasm:P:T`; `CLOUDFLARE_FORMAT=wasm scripts/cloudflare-drill.sh`; `scripts/host-mutations.sh`; `CHECK_JOBS=3 nice scripts/check.sh`.

## 2026-11-15: wasm-size: the assembler shakes each module for its program

Branch wasm-size, from wasm 329cd50. It establishes:

- **A shaking pass before encoding** (`src/emit/wasm/shake.rs`, called from `assemble::assemble`). Immutable globals and zero-argument functions whose body is one constant become constants; a load from a `words` table at a known address becomes the word there (the program's data tables are read only: no runtime store targets one); a load at a computed index into a table becomes the set of that table's words.
- **Value sets across calls.** Each parameter of a function no export reaches directly, and each function's result, gets the set of table addresses (and zero) that every call passes or every return gives, found by an optimistic fixpoint over the folded code (sets start empty and only grow; a value that is not an address or zero makes the set unknown, so the fixpoint is bounded by the number of named data forms). Inside a function, a local assigned once keeps its value set, a top-level `local.set` gives the exact set to the statements after it, a `loop` forgets what its body assigns, and an `if (eqz x) (then ... return)` guard removes zero from `x` afterwards.
- **Folding decided branches.** A condition whose value set is one value picks its arm (an impure condition is kept as a `drop`); a `br_if` on a constant becomes `br` or nothing; code after a `return`, `br`, `unreachable` or a block that never falls through is dropped up to the end of its block. The existing reachability pass then drops every function, global and data form no export reaches.
- **The plant `fold_arms_flipped`** (an entry with an empty substitution in `PLANTS`; `emit_planted` passes `Arms::Flipped`) takes the other arm of every decided branch. `scripts/mutations.sh --diff wasm:fold_arms_flipped:native-jsonl` and `:vercel` catch it by `backend_equal` in seeds 0..100. The census awk in `scripts/mutations.sh` now also reads one-line `PLANTS` entries.

Scorecard (metric, baseline at 329cd50, now, target):

- Bytes of all 46 modules (cloudflare): 3,042,362; 2,158,373 (−29%); target 1,000,000.
- minimal (cloudflare): 63,897; 41,438 (−35%; code 29,526, data 10,648); target 16,000.
- claude_code (cloudflare): 90,937; 74,322 (−18%); target 40,000.
- Smallest module: 62,775 (early_return); 41,119 (modes).
- native-jsonl, all 46: 2,169,861.

What is left in minimal and why: the journal fold reaches the cell, job, event and fork record decoders through `$apply` and `$record_from`, whose dispatch is on the record's tag read from the journal, not from a program table, so a foreign record stays decodable; 417 data forms (10.3 KB, mostly error texts and the 1 KB CRC table) ride with the code that names them. Next bets: gate the record decoders on the program's tables (the interpreter refuses such records the same way, which needs a differential plant per record kind), and share error-text prefixes.

Measurements (5-core Hand at load average 15 to 30): sizes from `hl emit --target cloudflare F` for every program in examples/ and docs/snippets/; `WASM_DIFF_SEEDS=0..10 scripts/wasm-diff.sh` on minimal, agent, catalog and claude_code (native-jsonl) after every step; 27 program-target pairs of a 0..10 sweep on native-jsonl, cloudflare and vercel matched before the load stopped it; `scripts/check.sh` on the merged head.

Commands: `hl emit --target T F`; `scripts/mutations.sh --diff wasm:fold_arms_flipped:T`; `WASM_DIFF_FILES=F WASM_DIFF_TARGETS=T WASM_DIFF_SEEDS=0..10 scripts/wasm-diff.sh`.

## 2026-11-15: wasm integration: hosts, shaking and the specialized native interpreter on one branch

Branch int-wasm, from wasm f568b79 (which already held master ce76f48 and the sub-lanes wasm-interp, wasm-events, wasm-blobs, wasm-cells2, wasm-jobs, wasm-spawn and wasm-speed), merging wasm-hosts, wasm-size and wasm-speed2. It establishes:

- **wasm-hosts:** `wasm::native` is the fixed native host (one journal file, keep truncates, flush appends and syncs, a JSON world); hl-agent runs the claude_code module through it instead of generated Rust; `load` lives in the library; `hl bundle` writes what `hl deploy` uploads, `hl emit --format mjs` the JavaScript host form; the native crash, workerd kill and agent drills run modules. A change left uncommitted in the wasm-hosts worktree, a `recovery_unreported` plant aimed at a `$record_recovery_attested` function no runtime file defines, was left out.
- **wasm-size:** the shaking pass (its own entry above).
- **Each host diffs in its own directory.** `scripts/wasm-diff.sh` writes under `output/wasm-diff/<plant>/<host>/`: `scripts/check.sh` runs the node (seeds 0..100) and native (0..30) diffs of one program and target at once, and they shared `trace.jsonl` and `harness.wasm`, so the first full check of this branch failed `examples/agent.hl` on native-sqlite at seed 56, a seed only the node run's trace held; the program matches on both hosts alone and side by side.
- **wasm-speed2:** the native interpreter runs a specialized instruction set (`src/wasm/specialize.rs`) with indexed loads, an i64 constant pool, address-plus-constant loads, byte-class JSON string scans and fused loops for byte scans and CRC-32.

Scorecard (metric, wasm and wasm-size heads before this merge, now):

- Programs whose module matches the interpreter on all five targets in `scripts/check.sh`: 46 of 46; 46 of 46.
- Module bytes, cloudflare: minimal 63,897 (wasm) and 41,438 (wasm-size), 41,790; claude_code 90,937 and 74,322, 74,674; claude_agent 50,929; all 46 programs 2,158,373 (wasm-size), 2,174,565. The wasm-hosts exports add about 350 bytes per module.
- Native host ops per run (native-jsonl, seeds 0..30): minimal 343,417 to 271,683 (−21%); agent 832,320 to 660,005 (−21%); claude_agent 1,524,672 to 1,207,903 (−21%).
- Native host run time against node's (native-jsonl, seeds 0..30, min of 2 runs, load average 17 to 27): minimal 1,312 against 368 ms (3.6 times) to 969 against 292 ms (3.3 times); agent 3,533 against 849 ms (4.2 times) to 1,499 against 899 ms (1.7 times); claude_agent 5,141 against 1,045 ms (4.9 times) to 2,938 against 1,054 ms (2.8 times); target 1.5 times.

Measurements: the baseline ran wasm-size's binary (built at its head, whose `src/wasm/` is wasm's) and the current one this branch's, back to back on the 5-core Hand shared with other agents; `WASM_DIFF_HOST=native WASM_DIFF_SEEDS=0..10 scripts/wasm-diff.sh` matched minimal, agent, claude_code and claude_agent on native-jsonl after the speed2 merge; `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh` on the merged head.

Commands: `hl emit --target T F | wc -c`; `WASM_DIFF_HOST=node|native WASM_DIFF_FILES=F WASM_DIFF_TARGETS=native-jsonl WASM_DIFF_SEEDS=0..30 scripts/wasm-diff.sh`; `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh`.

Merging master brought the interpreter, Rust and JS runtimes' rule that a variant with no fields also decodes from an empty array (`{"interrupted":[]}`), which the wasm runtime's `value_from_json` refused, so `examples/codex_agent.hl` failed the module diff on 4 of 5 targets (first on native-jsonl seed 5, a cancelled turn recorded as a failed compaction). `value.wat` now takes that shape too, and the wasm plant `interrupted_undecoded` removes it again: `scripts/mutations.sh --diff wasm:interrupted_undecoded:T` catches it on all five targets in seeds 0..100, and codex_agent matches the interpreter on all five targets with both hosts in seeds 0..100.

Two wasm plants had drifted from the runtime they plant into, and no full check had reached the diff-plants stage to show it. `recovery_unreported` still searched for the `$record_recovery_nested` append that the host merge replaced with `$record_recovery_attested`, so it matched nothing; it now drops that record instead of appending it. `cell_cancel_ignored` zeroed only the final cancel count in `$cancel_seen`, which leaves the drain before it in place, and code_stop.hl matched the interpreter with it on seeds 0..1000; it now returns before the drain as the Rust and JavaScript plants do. Both are caught on every planned target in seeds 0..100 (`scripts/mutations.sh --diff wasm:P:T`), and the other wasm plants in `scripts/mutations.sh --diff-plans` all pass.

Merging master's payments, climb, deploy auth and MPP host into the wasm branch: the wasm runtime now lowers `Expr::Outcome` lazily by its tag (ok, failed, unknown, else the original), runs the `_with_payment` text builtin on the module's own JSON parser and encoder (a non-object input is returned unchanged, an existing `payment` key is replaced, and the object is re-sorted), and reads the wallet's name from a `$wallet` data word: a model reply's call to the wallet keeps its raw input and is refused with the interpreter's text, and the wallet's dispatched and recovered replies are checked as u64. payments.hl, paid_lanes.hl, mpp_agent.hl, the three climb snippets and autoresearch.hl match the interpreter on all five targets with the node host in seeds 0..100. The Cloudflare bundle keeps its fixed host and module and adds master's `hl-auth.mjs` entry; the Vercel route takes master's auth check. hl-agent keeps the Claude harnesses on the wasm host and runs `hl-agent pay` on `mpp_agent.hl` emitted as Rust, as Codex is; agent-drill, codex-drill, mpp-drill (seeds 1..8) and the payer drill pass.

## 2026-11-15: eval-clusters: why hl-agent fails, and a Finishing block that checks every stated behaviour

Branch eval-clusters, from master 42f9a62. It establishes:

- **The per-task diff over every finished confirmation row** (output/eval/c60 on the confirmation branch, 234 rows, and the suite-1 rerun's s1r, 240 rows; pass letters per attempt, hl-agent at effort low, claude-sonnet-4-6):

| Task | hl-agent | nanocode | Claude Code |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| build-pmars | 2/3 | 3/3 | 2/3 |
| build-pov-ray | 2/3 | 3/3 | 3/3 |
| cancel-async-tasks | 2/3 | 1/3 | 0/3 |
| chess-best-move | 0/3 | 0/2 | 0/3 |
| fix-git | 2/3 | 1/3 | 3/3 |
| polyglot-c-py | 2/3 | 3/3 | 2/3 |
| adaptive-rejection-sampler | 3/3 | 0/3 | 0/1 (2 errored) |
| largest-eigenval | 3/3 | 0/3 | 2/3 |
| caffe-cifar-10 | 2/2 | 1/2 | 1/1 |

  Every other finished task passed for hl-agent on every attempt. Where another agent passes and hl-agent fails: build-pmars, build-pov-ray, fix-git, polyglot-c-py. Where nanocode fails (the room for a lead over it): adaptive-rejection-sampler, largest-eigenval, caffe-cifar-10, cancel-async-tasks, fix-git and chess-best-move; hl-agent already passes the first three every time, so the lead depends on the last three.
- **The failing traces fall into four clusters.**
  1. A stated behaviour checked another way or not at all (cancel-async-tasks.3: the self-test cancelled the task inside its own event loop, while the verifier sends SIGINT to a separate process with more tasks than the limit; build-pmars.3: the task names the debugger, the agent never stepped through it). Also polyglot-c-py.3: python3 was missing, the agent ran only the C half and finished with "the file is correct" although the Finishing block already says to install the interpreter.
  2. Scratch files in the deliverable's directory or a partial layout (build-pmars.3 wrote its own flashpaper.red and rave.red into /app, which shadowed the real warriors the verifier runs there; build-pov-ray.3 extracted the archives to /tmp and copied only some parts to /app/povray-2.2, so file_id.diz was missing).
  3. Asking instead of finishing (fix-git.1 listed two commit chains and asked which to merge; the passing attempts read git reflog and cherry-picked the commit that was checked out last).
  4. Reading a position from an image (chess-best-move, 0 of 3 for hl-agent and 0 for both other agents; one attempt timed out). Not addressed here.
- **Fixes, both default, no added request.** The Finishing block (agent/prompts/claude-code-system.json) gains three sentences: check every requirement the task states and exercise each named behaviour for real (step through a named debugger, deliver a keyboard interrupt as SIGINT to a separate process, run above and below any limit); keep test inputs and scratch files out of the task's directories and extract an archive in full where the task says; finish instead of asking, taking the reading the task's files support (git reflog for lost work). examples/claude_code.hl appends a one-line system-reminder to a Bash result that contains `command not found` (install it and run the check again), through two small functions `shown` and `reported` that replace the four repeated result-pushing blocks. No new state or invariant.
- **Measurement** (2026-11-15, 12:40 to 15:10 UTC, `--parallel 1`, `nice -n 10`, beside the 60-task confirmation run): the failing tasks x3 and a regression sample x2 (openssl-selfsigned-cert, git-leak-recovery, log-summary-date-ranges, largest-eigenval, sqlite-db-truncate, kv-store-grpc, extract-todos, adaptive-rejection-sampler, build-cython-ext, cobol-modernization), against the master rows of the same tasks and attempt numbers. Cost from raw usage in responses.jsonl with cache tokens (5-minute writes $3.75, reads $0.30, output $15 per million); no row had a cut jsonl record.

| Set | This branch | Master, same attempts | nanocode, same attempts |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| failing tasks (15 attempts) | 14/15, $0.089 | 10/15, $0.079 | 11/15, $0.518 |
| finished total (27 attempts) | 26/27, $0.086 | 22/27, $0.085 | 21/27, $0.397 |

  Per task: build-pmars 3/3 (master 2/3), build-pov-ray 3/3 (2/3), fix-git 3/3 (2/3), polyglot-c-py 3/3 (2/3), cancel-async-tasks 2/3 (2/3; the failing attempt still tested cancellation inside its own loop). Regression rows all passed. Paired by attempt: 5 gained, 1 lost (the cancel-async-tasks attempt that master passed), sign test p = 0.22. Cost per attempt +1% on the 27 attempts; build-pov-ray costs more ($0.222 against $0.164) because it now checks the extracted layout. hl-agent's cost is 0.22x nanocode's on the same attempts. The regression run was stopped before its last rows (extract-todos, adaptive-rejection-sampler, build-cython-ext, cobol-modernization) to stay inside the session; spend $2.3 metered.

Commands: `cargo build --release` at the root and in `agent/`, the binary patched with setup.sh's patchelf into output/eval/root with the confirmation root's lib; `nice -n 10 python3 evals/run.py --suite output/eval/fail.txt --tasks <confirmation tasks60> --agent-root output/eval/root --agents hl-agent --attempts 3 --model claude-sonnet-4-6 --parallel 1 --limits none --ca-bundle /etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt --meter-port 19440 --relay-port 14440 --out output/eval/fail`, the same with output/eval/reg.txt and `--attempts 2`; `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice -n 15 scripts/check.sh`.

## 2026-11-15: eval-lean-io: where hl-agent's prompt tokens go; a cut Bash stream keeps its tail; the static system guidance carries a cache breakpoint

Branch eval-lean-io, from master ffbc1dd. It establishes:

- **Where the input side goes**, from the raw responses of 159 finished hl-agent attempts (the eval-rerun lane's s1r suite-1 rows and the eval-confirm lane's c60 rows of suites 2 and 3, effort low, claude-sonnet-4-6): per attempt 12 uncached input, 7,303 cache-write, 106,633 cache-read and 3,833 output tokens over 11.0 requests. Priced: output $0.057 (49%), cache reads $0.032 (27%), cache writes $0.027 (23%), uncached input $0.0000. Cache reads are 93.6% of all prompt tokens. 28.6% of output tokens are thinking.
- **The prompt cache already reads each prior prefix.** Of 1,756 requests, 21 read less than the previous request's read plus write; all but one are child-session requests interleaved in the same meter record (a parent request after a subagent's), and the one real miss is a 377 s gap past the 5-minute cache life (gcode-to-text). Across every hl-agent row on the Hand (1,278 rows, 13,726 request pairs) 28 gaps exceed 270 s and they rewrote 51,398 tokens in all, about 40 tokens an attempt. No breakpoint invalidates a turn: the cache-write tokens of an attempt are its context's growth (5,867 tokens an attempt, 3,253 of them the previous replies including thinking, the rest tool results and notices, measured on the 153 attempts without children) plus the first request's uncached prefix.
- **The fixed prefix is half the reads.** The first request is 4,522 tokens (tools 9,096 characters, system 6,237); read on every request it is about 50,000 of the 106,633 read tokens an attempt ($0.015). Tool descriptions are out of scope (a lane that rewrote them lost); the three subagent tools are 2,201 of the 9,096 tool characters.
- **Tool output is small.** At the end of an attempt text tool results are 19,327 characters, of which images (base64 PNGs from Read, billed by pixels) are 12,774 and Bash 5,371. Counterfactual on the final contexts (each result's tokens written once at $3.75 and read by every later request at $0.30 per million): all text tool results cost $0.0116 an attempt; capping every result at 16,000, 8,000, 4,000 or 2,000 characters would save $0.00015, $0.00049, $0.0018 or $0.0039. Bash results: median 257 characters, 95th percentile 3,412, 99th 6,478; the results over 4,000 characters are mostly what the model asked for (setup.py, git show, objdump). Identical repeated results over 300 characters: 2 in 1,470 ($0.00002 an attempt). A tighter cap or deduplication was therefore not built: its best case is under 3% of cost and it hides output the model requested.
- **A cut Bash stream keeps its head and tail** (`agent/src/tools.rs` `captured`). A stream over `STREAM_BYTES_MAX` (30,000 bytes, Claude Code's cap, unchanged) kept only its first 30,000 bytes, so a failing build's error at the end of a long stdout never reached the model (`truncated_error` only cut what was kept). Now the first and last 15,000 bytes are kept, with the marker `[stdout: N of M bytes elided from the middle. To see them, rerun the command with its output sent to a file and read parts of it with grep -n, sed -n 'START,ENDp' or tail]` between them; the tail is read by seeking, so the stream is never read whole. The size of what reaches the model is unchanged. Real run (claude-sonnet-4-6, `seq 1 20000`, 108,894 bytes): the model quoted first line 1, last line 20000 and the marker with 78,894 of 108,894 bytes elided.
- **The static system guidance carries the cache breakpoint** (`agent/prompts/claude-code-system.json`). The first system block's breakpoint was redundant (the second block's breakpoint covers it); it moves to the third block, the task guidance that is the same in every session, so a session reads that block from a cache another session wrote within five minutes and writes only the environment block and the instruction. Measured on real runs: a fresh session's first request read 3,344 and wrote 1,005 tokens before, read 3,907 and wrote 404 after (563 tokens moved from write to read, $0.0019 a session). Four breakpoints in all, as before.

- **Measured on suite 1 (interim, the run was stopped at 21 of 60 attempts; one attempt, regex-log 1, was lost to a host restart and rerun).** On the same 21 (task, attempt) pairs: this branch 18/21 at $0.0640 an attempt and 5.29 requests; master's s1r rows 19/21 at $0.0648 and 5.33 requests; nanocode's rows 17/21 at $0.1635. Discordant pairs: cancel-async-tasks 2 (lost here), cancel-async-tasks 3 (won here), log-summary-date-ranges 1 (lost here): 1 to 2, not significant. Cost is 1% lower, within noise: 0.39x nanocode on these pairs against master's 0.40x. The six suite-3 tasks were not run: the confirmation run had finished hl-agent rows for only three suite-3 tasks (caffe-cifar-10, bn-fit-modify, build-pov-ray) when the lane ended. No meter record was cut in these rows.
- **Verdict.** The input side is not where hl-agent's cost can fall by the factor asked (0.25x nanocode): caching already serves 93.6% of prompt tokens, tool output is 9% of cost at most and its largest results are ones the model asked for. Output tokens (49%) and the request count (which multiplies the 4.5k-token prefix and the context) are the levers; both belong to other lanes (effort, model, tool shape). The two changes stay as hygiene: the tail fix shows errors a long stream used to drop, and the breakpoint move saves about $0.002 a session.
- Spend (metered, claude-sonnet-4-6): the 21 suite-1 rows $1.34, the three smoke runs under $0.15.

Commands: the analysis scripts in `output/an2.py` to `output/an8.py` over `/home/hand/wt/eval-rerun/output/eval/s1r/rows` and `/home/hand/wt/eval-confirm/output/eval/c60/rows`; `cargo build --release` in `agent/`, the binary patched with setup.sh's patchelf into `output/eval/root` with root-r4's lib; `nice -n 10 python3 evals/run.py --suite evals/suite.txt --tasks <eval-confirm tasks60> --agent-root output/eval/root --agents hl-agent --attempts 3 --model claude-sonnet-4-6 --parallel 1 --limits none --ca-bundle /etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt --meter-port 19470 --relay-port 14470 --out output/eval/s1-lean`; `CHECK_JOBS=2 nice scripts/check.sh`.

## agent-drill: a child killed before its first record is an orphan, not a crash of the checker (2026-11-15)

With `AGENT_DRILL_SEED=1`, a kill can land right after the parent journals `spawn_agent` and before the child writes its first usage line. The checker then opened the child's missing `usage.jsonl` and failed with FileNotFoundError: 2 of 10 runs on master d474d32 and the agent stage of one full `scripts/check.sh` run. The dispatch count now skips a child with no usage log, since it dispatched nothing. The per-journal pass allows a missing `journal.jsonl` or `usage.jsonl` only for a child whose spawn the parent's recovery left unknown, and fails with a named error otherwise. `for i in $(seq 1 24); do AGENT_DRILL_SEED=1 scripts/agent-drill.sh; done`: 24 of 24 pass, every run with one child, against 2 failures in 10 on master.
