## 2026-11-15: eval-luna: hl-agent on gpt-6-luna through a ChatGPT plan login, metered at list price

Branch eval-luna, from master 31f1ab4. It establishes:

- **The meter has a ChatGPT plan route.** `/run/<id>/chatgpt/<path>` forwards to `https://chatgpt.com/backend-api/codex/<path>` (a leading `/v1` is dropped, so a client whose base URL is `<run>/chatgpt` and that posts `/v1/responses` lands on `/backend-api/codex/responses`). `NANOEVAL_CHATGPT_LOGIN` names a Codex login file in ChatGPT mode; the meter refuses one readable by group or others, and reads `tokens.access_token` and `tokens.account_id` from it on every request, sending them as `authorization: Bearer` and `chatgpt-account-id` (the two headers the existing plan path in the TypeScript eval system sends; nothing else was needed). The token is never in an environment variable, a command line, a log or a record. `NANOEVAL_CHATGPT_UPSTREAM` replaces the origin like the other two routes. Usage is metered exactly as on the OpenAI route.
- **The ChatGPT backend streams without a content type.** Its responses carry no `content-type` header, so the meter took the SSE body for a non-JSON error and metered zero tokens. A body that does not parse as JSON and starts with `event:` or `data:` is now read as an event stream. Measured on one request (model gpt-6-luna, effort low, "Reply with the single word: pong"): status 200, 21 input and 5 output tokens metered, stop reason completed.
- **The meter stops the plan route on its first 401, 403 or 429.** It answers every later ChatGPT request with 503 and writes `chatgpt-halted.json` in the runs directory; `run.py` cancels the rows it has not started when it sees that file. A plan login is someone's daily-use plan: a usage limit stops the screen instead of being retried by every row. No such status occurred in this entry.
- **`run.py --openai-route chatgpt`** points the OpenAI-speaking arms (hl-agent, nanocode, codex) at that route; `NANOEVAL_CHATGPT_LOGIN` counts as their key.
- **`run.py --meter-host ADDRESS`** binds the meter to an address other than the Docker bridge gateway and gives it to the containers. A host whose firewall drops input to every local address but loopback and its tailnet address (the machine of this entry, whose INPUT chain accepts only 127.0.0.0/8 and 100.64.0.0/10 destinations) cannot serve the containers on 172.17.0.1: the meter listened there and every connection timed out, so `run.py` reported that the meter did not start. With the meter on the tailnet address the containers reach it through the bridge. Since that address is reachable from the tailnet, the meter then serves only clients in the bridge's subnet (`NANOEVAL_METER_CLIENTS`, set by `run.py` from `docker network inspect bridge`) and answers anyone else 403.
- **`report.py` prices gpt-6-luna and gpt-6.1-terra** at the standard-context list rates in `agent/src/openai.rs` (luna $0.10 input, $0.01 cached, $0.50 output per million; terra $1, $0.05, $5).

Setup: hl-agent built at this commit (`cargo build --release` in `agent/`), patched to the agent root's loader with patchelf and run from an agent root laid out like the confirmation run's (`bin/hl-agent` and the glibc `lib/`). The eight d8 tasks of Terminal-Bench 2.1 ran on a 32-core Linux machine with Docker (load average 12 to 22 from other work), two rows at a time, `--limits none` as the Sonnet reference ran, with:

    NANOEVAL_CHATGPT_LOGIN=$HOME/.codex/auth.json nice -n 10 python3 evals/run.py --suite d8.txt --tasks tasks \
      --agent-root root --agents hl-agent --attempts 2 --model gpt-6-luna --parallel 2 --limits none \
      --meter-port 19700 --relay-port 14700 --openai-route chatgpt --meter-host <tailnet address> --out out/luna1

and the same with `--attempts 1 --hl-agent-arg=--effort --hl-agent-arg=high --out out/lunahigh`. hl-agent's default effort for this host is medium. The Sonnet reference is the confirmation run's rows of the same tasks (3 attempts each). Costs are list price from the meter's tokens (luna's cached tokens are billed at the cached rate, the rest of the input at the input rate).

| Arm (d8, Terminal-Bench 2.1) | Attempts | Passed | $/attempt (list) | Requests/attempt | Agent wall/attempt |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| nanocode, claude-sonnet-4-6 (reference) | 24 | 10 | 0.331 | 13.5 | 475 s |
| hl-agent, claude-sonnet-4-6 (reference) | 24 | 18 | 0.181 | 14.6 | 281 s |
| hl-agent, gpt-6-luna, effort medium | 16 | 7 | 0.0092 | 11.0 | 100 s |
| hl-agent, gpt-6-luna, effort high (7 of 8 tasks; query-optimize still running when this was written) | 7 | 3 | 0.0256 | 16.6 | 172 s |

Effort high, the one cheap tweak tried, does not help: 3 of 7 (build-pmars, adaptive-rejection-sampler, count-dataset-tokens pass; db-wal-recovery, which medium passed twice, fails) at 2.8 times the cost of medium, with 3,506 reasoning tokens an attempt against 859. Thinking longer per turn did not make luna verify its work before ending; configure-git-webserver, extract-elf and gcode-to-text fail the same way at both efforts.

Per task, luna at medium: adaptive-rejection-sampler 2/2, count-dataset-tokens 2/2, db-wal-recovery 2/2, build-pmars 1/2, configure-git-webserver 0/2, extract-elf 0/2, gcode-to-text 0/2, query-optimize 0/2. It passes three tasks nanocode on Sonnet fails or rarely passes (adaptive-rejection-sampler 0/3, db-wal-recovery 0/3, count-dataset-tokens 1/3) and fails three nanocode passes (configure-git-webserver 3/3, extract-elf 2/3, build-pmars 3/3 against 1/2).

What the failures look like: luna stops early, not late. No row timed out; the mean attempt used 11 requests and 100 s against Sonnet hl-agent's 14.6 and 281 s. On configure-git-webserver it wrote a setup script and a README, checked the script's syntax and ended without running it, so nothing the verifier probes existed. On extract-elf it wrote the extractor, ran it once on the sample and ended. Cost is not the constraint: at $0.009 an attempt, luna spends 2.8% of nanocode's list price, under a twentieth of the $0.05 budget.

The prompt cache helps less here than on the Anthropic route: 53K cached against 71K uncached input tokens per attempt. The per-attempt input is small enough that this changes the cost by cents per hundred attempts.

Not measured: gpt-6.1-terra (luna passed more than 3 of 8 on its first attempt, so the plan's fallback did not trigger), any task outside d8, and whether a "verify before you finish" change to the Codex host's instructions fixes the early stop, which is what the failures point at. The plan's target (nanocode's pass rate plus 20 points at 0.15 of its cost) is not met: luna matches nanocode on d8 (44% against 42%) at 0.03 of its cost, and is 31 points below hl-agent on Sonnet on the same tasks.

## 2026-11-15: eval-luna2: a finish gate and a relayed Sonnet review for the Codex host on gpt-6-luna

Branch eval-luna2, from eval-luna cd12631. It establishes:

- **Finish gate in the Codex host** (`agent/src/codex_host.rs`). When a prompt turn ends `completed`, the host starts another prompt turn with a fixed message (`FINISH_CHECK`): check each stated behaviour by running commands (paths, programs with the named interpreter, services on the named port and account, output formats), fix and continue if anything is unverified, and end only with a report of the checks run. At most `FINISH_CHECKS_MAX` (2) gate turns per task. The gate lives in the host, not in `examples/codex_agent.hl`: the program is unchanged, so no new invariant or mutation was needed.
- **The Claude host's Finishing block** is ported into `agent/prompts/codex-instructions.md` (plus one sentence: a script you wrote is not done until you ran it; test a program on more than the one sample).
- **`--reviewer-relay <dir>`** (Codex host only, `agent/src/reviewer.rs`). After the gate, the host asks claude-sonnet-4-6 for a review of the task and the last 16 context entries (brief bounded at 6,000 tokens, answer at 1,500), at most `REVIEWS_MAX` (2) per task; a reply starting `NOT DONE` becomes the next prompt turn. The request is written to `<dir>/request-N.json` and the host waits (at most 900 s) for `response-N.json`. The relay is file-based because the hosted machine (which holds the Anthropic key) and the eval machine (which holds the plan login) have no network path to each other in either direction over the tailnet (curl timed out both ways); the orchestrating agent copied each request to the hosted machine, posted it through a standalone `evals/meter.py` on 127.0.0.1:19710, and copied the answer back. Neither credential crossed machines. Sonnet tokens are recorded in the ledger at list price ($3 in, $15 out, $0.30 cache read, $3.75 cache write per million).

Measurement (2026-11-15, 19:45 to 20:45 UTC, the eval-luna setup copied to `~/hl-luna2`, `--parallel 2 --limits none --openai-route chatgpt`, ports 19710/14710, gpt-6-luna effort medium, 8 d8 tasks of Terminal-Bench 2.1; cost is list price from the ledger):

| Arm | Attempts | Passed | $/attempt (luna + Sonnet) | Requests/attempt | Wall/attempt |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| eval-luna baseline (no gate) | 16 | 7 | 0.0092 | 11.0 | 100 s |
| A: gate + Finishing block | 16 | 10 | 0.021 | 21.6 | 188 s |
| B: A + Sonnet review, on A's 3 all-failing tasks only | 6 | 0 | 0.0565 (0.030 + 0.026) | 27.5 (1.33 Sonnet) | 272 s |

Per task, arm A: adaptive-rejection-sampler 2/2, build-pmars 2/2, configure-git-webserver 2/2 (0/2 before), count-dataset-tokens 2/2, db-wal-recovery 2/2, extract-elf 0/2, gcode-to-text 0/2, query-optimize 0/2. The gate fixed the "wrote a setup script, never ran it" failure: configure-git-webserver now passes both attempts, and build-pmars went from 1/2 to 2/2. It doubled requests and cost per attempt and is still a third of the $0.05 budget.

Arm B ran only on extract-elf, gcode-to-text and query-optimize (A's 0/2 tasks), so its rows measure the review's marginal effect where the gate did not help: none. Sonnet said DONE on both extract-elf and both query-optimize attempts (the transcripts showed checks that passed, but the outputs were wrong against hidden tests), and NOT DONE twice on each gcode-to-text attempt (luna never decoded the printed text); luna then worked another ~300 s and still failed. The review cannot see what the hidden verifier checks, so on these tasks it adds cost without passes. Arm B was not run on the five passing tasks; on the full d8 it would be at most A's 10/16.

Incidents: the eval machine's root disk filled to 100% (others' work) at 19:53; two rows wrote cut JSONL lines and the run was stopped, the containers removed, and resumed at 20:03 once 16 GB were free. adaptive-rejection-sampler attempt 2 was scored from its verifier reward (1) after dropping the one cut `requests.jsonl` line. Load average on that machine ran 20 to 41 from other work (our two containers were a small share). Plan rows used this lane: 27 of 50; no 401/403/429.

Not met: the target (Sonnet hl-agent 18/24 = 75% on d8) — A reaches 62.5% at 0.12 of Sonnet hl-agent's cost and 0.06 of nanocode's. The remaining failures (extract-elf, gcode-to-text, query-optimize) are capability, not early stopping.

Commands: `cargo build --release` in `agent/`; binary patched with patchelf to `/opt/nanoeval-agent/lib`; `sh go.sh out/gateA gpt-6-luna 2` (go.sh = eval-luna's with ports 19710/14710); `sh go.sh out/revB2 gpt-6-luna 2 --suite d3.txt --agent-root ~/hl-luna2/rootB --hl-agent-arg=--reviewer-relay --hl-agent-arg=/opt/nanoeval-journal/hl/review`.

## 2026-11-15: full-suite run of the Codex host on gpt-6-luna with the finish gate

Arm A of eval-luna2 (gate binary `~/hl-luna2/root` on the eval machine, built from ef9d652) on the 60 tasks of `evals/suite-60.txt`, 3 attempts each, against the published Sonnet rows (`output/eval/snap/c60-all`, 147 attempts each).

Where it ran: the hosted Hand and the eval machine are not tailnet peers of each other (`tailscale ping <tailnet address>` from the hosted Hand: no matching peer; TCP to a test listener on <tailnet address>:19740 timed out both ways), so the token-bearing relay could not be split from the task containers. Everything runs on the eval machine under its rules: `evals/luna-full.sh` starts a two-task batch only while the 1-minute load is at most 26 and other lanes' containers leave room within 3 rows on the plan, stops below 12 GB free, removes the images it pulled after each batch, and stops after three failed batches in a row or when the meter writes `chatgpt-halted.json`. Meter and relay ports 19740/14740, `--openai-route chatgpt --meter-host <tailnet address>`, `--limits none`.

Reused attempts (same binary and settings): the 16 d8 gate rows from `~/hl-luna2/out/gateA` (8 tasks x 2) and 24 rows from `~/hl-luna3/out/A` (24 tasks x 1).

Self-check signal (`evals/selfcheck.py`, no grader input): `unknown` when the agent exited non-zero or timed out or left no final report; `fail` when the last 4000 characters of its final report contain a doubt cue (cannot, unable, unverified, mismatch, does not match, depends on, blocked, failed and the like); `pass` otherwise. It is written into each row's result.json as `self_check` and `self_check_cue`. The cue list was written after reading the 40 reused rows, so only later rows measure it out of sample.

Final, all 180 rows, 2026-11-16 (`python3 evals/lunafull.py output/luna-full/rows output/eval/snap/c60-all output/luna-full/snap output/luna-full/design.txt`):

| Agent | Attempts | Pass rate (95% Wilson) | Cost/attempt | Wall/attempt | Requests/attempt |
| --- | ---: | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| hl-agent gpt-6-luna gate | 180 | 74.4% (67.6-80.3%) | $0.027 at list rates | 183 s | 22.3 |
| hl-agent Sonnet | 147 | 81.0% (73.8-86.5%) | $0.197 | 272 s | 13.9 |
| nanocode Sonnet | 147 | 66.0% (58.0-73.2%) | $0.346 | 499 s | 15.7 |
| Claude Code Sonnet | 147 | 69.8% (61.7-76.8%) | $0.330 | 440 s | 14.8 |

- On the 147 attempts paired with the Sonnet rows the Luna arm passes 114 (77.6%): against nanocode 28 Luna-only vs 11 nanocode-only passes (sign test p = 0.009), +11.6 points; against hl-agent Sonnet 13 vs 18 (p = 0.473). Its 33 extra suite-3 attempts (tasks the Sonnet run took once) pull the unpaired rate to 74.4%.
- Cost: 0.078x nanocode per attempt at list rates; the median attempt is about $0.011, and long sessions (caffe-cifar-10 $0.21, compile-compcert $0.13, build-pov-ray and build-cython-ext about $0.065) carry the mean.
- Luna fails every attempt on chess-best-move, circuit-fibsqrt, dna-assembly, dna-insert, extract-elf, feal-differential-cryptanalysis, gcode-to-text, pypi-server, query-optimize and raman-fitting.
- Self-check on all 180 rows: fail on 24 rows, 19 of them failed; it caught 19 of 46 failures.

Self-check against the verifier on 85 rows: pass 70 passed / 9 failed, fail 0 / 5, unknown 1 / 0. Escalating on fail: precision 5/5, recall 5/14; on fail or unknown: precision 5/6, recall 5/14. Out of sample (45 later rows): nothing flagged, recall 0/2. The final report alone rarely admits a miss; a later lane should read the gate's check results from the journal rather than the prose.

Resume: `cd ~/luna-full && setsid nohup sh -c 'sh go.sh; echo EXIT=$? >> out/go.exit' > out/go.out 2>&1 < /dev/null &` (it skips rows that have result.json), then `python3 evals/selfcheck.py out/rows` and the report command above.


## 2026-11-16: codex drill answers the finish gate

The finish gate runs its checks as extra journal turns after the prompted one, so `hl-agent run --turn N` with the gate uses turns N+1 and N+2 when turn N completes. The drill's scripted model had no answers for those turns: the main run asked for a step past the end of its script, and the control run's steer and cancel turns 2 and 3 collided with the gate's turns. The drill now scripts two short answers for the gate after each completed turn and drives the control's cancel turns as 4 and 5. `CODEX_DRILL_SEED=1 scripts/codex-drill.sh` passes (kills=12, control steer and cancel ok), and the planted `batch_flush_after_dispatch` is still caught.

## 2026-11-16: three Codex arms on one ChatGPT plan

Stock Codex, nanocode's Codex backend and hl-agent's Codex host (`examples/codex_agent.hl`) ran suite 1 on one model through one meter, so the comparison isolates the harness. Setup on the eval machine: an agent root with `codex` 0.169.0 and `codex-code-mode-host` 0.169.0 (the release's musl builds, checked against their sha256), nanocode c7679e3e8 built with `cargo build --release --locked -p nanocode-cli --bin nanocode`, and hl-agent from this branch, both patched to the root's loader as `evals/setup.sh` does. Each row: `NANOEVAL_CHATGPT_LOGIN=$HOME/.codex/auth.json python3 evals/run.py --suite <one task> --tasks <task dirs> --agent-root <root> --agents codex,hl-agent,nanocode --attempts 1 --model gpt-5.6-sol --parallel 2 --limits none --openai-route chatgpt --meter-host <docker bridge address> --out <dir>`, started only while fewer than five eval containers ran machine-wide, the load was at most 26 and 12 GB were free, and each task's images were removed after its rows.

What the wiring needed, found by a one-task smoke run of each arm against a meter on loopback:

- The ChatGPT backend lists models only for a `client_version` query and in its own shape (`models[].slug`); nanocode's custom endpoint asks `GET /v1/models` and reads `data[].id`, and failed with "failed to list models". The meter now adds Codex's `client_version` to a models request on the `chatgpt` route that has none and returns the list in the platform API's shape; a request that carries one passes through unchanged.
- A Responses-lite header without the matching `reasoning.context: all_turns` in the body was refused upstream (400). The meter drops the header on the `chatgpt` route when the body does not ask for that context, so a client's request reaches the backend as the body describes it.
- `codex exec` on a code-mode model needs `codex-code-mode-host` beside it, or every turn fails before a request. `evals/setup.sh` installs it from the same release with its sha256.
- gpt-5.5 answers "not supported when using Codex with a ChatGPT account" (400); the models list's newest Codex model, gpt-5.6-sol, ran instead. `evals/report.py` prices it at its list price ($4, $20, $0.40 cached per million; the plan bills none).

Results (one attempt per task and arm; evals/RESULTS.md, Three Codex arms): stock Codex 18 of 20, $0.136 and 57 s an attempt; nanocode 20 of 20, $0.095, 56 s; hl-agent 20 of 20, $0.618, 198 s. Mined with `python3 evals/gaps/mine.py <out>`, which now reads Responses items (`function_call`, `custom_tool_call`, commands inside a code-mode `exec` cell); the gaps are in docs/GAPS.md, Gaps measured against stock Codex.

Two fixes, each rerun on the hl-agent arm alone over the same 20 tasks:

- Stock Codex sends the model's catalog effort, measured through the meter as `low` for gpt-5.6-sol and gpt-6.1-terra; hl-agent sent codex_agent.hl's default `medium`. `openai::default_effort` now gives the sol and terra families `low` when `--effort` is not given. Rerun: 19 of 20, $0.457, 154 s, 5,465 output tokens an attempt (from 7,640). The failure is largest-eigenval's speedup threshold, which the base run and the next rerun passed.
- The finish gate's two turns after every completed turn were 49% of hl-agent's model spend and 9.9 of 20.7 requests an attempt, and edited something in 1 of 20 attempts. `hl-agent run --finish-checks N` (Codex harness only, at most `FINISH_CHECKS_MAX`) sets how many run. Rerun with `--finish-checks 1` and low effort: 20 of 20, $0.363, 119 s, 13.6 requests an attempt. The default stays two: twenty single attempts cannot show the second gate turn never catches anything, and the drill covers two.

Open: hl-agent still costs 2.7 times stock Codex an attempt; its cache reads are 70 to 73% of prompt tokens against Codex's 87%, and it spends 11 s an attempt between a reply and the next request against Codex's 2.7 s. Attempts 2 and 3 of the three-arm run (to 180 rows) were still running when this entry was written.

## 2026-11-15: luna-lean: cache affinity, detached services and earlier compaction for the Codex host on gpt-6-luna

Where the money went in the full gpt-6-luna gate run (180 rows of `~/luna-full/out/rows` on the eval machine, list rates $0.10/M input, $0.01/M cached input, $0.50/M output; `python3 split.py` and `python3 ideal.py` in `~/luna-lean`): $0.0273 per attempt, of which uncached input $0.0212 (78%), cached input $0.0032, output $0.0029 (reasoning about 1,800 of 5,700 output tokens). Main work turns cost $0.0149, the two finish-gate turns $0.0124 (8.5 of 22.3 requests). Of 3,842 follow-up requests only 187 hit the cache on the whole previous prefix; 2,289 hit only the static ~5.5K prefix (instructions, tools, preamble) and 1,366 part of it. The prefix was byte-stable and `prompt_cache_key` was set, so the misses were routing: the ChatGPT backend derives cache affinity from the `session-id` header the Codex CLI sends (`codex-rs/core/src/client.rs`, `codex-api/src/requests/headers.rs` send `session-id` and `thread-id`, with `prompt_cache_key` equal to the session id), and hl-agent sent neither. With every follow-up hitting its previous prefix the same rows would cost $0.0098 per attempt. Tool outputs are not the lever: outputs over 4,000 characters hold a third of output characters and output tokens are $0.0029 in all. The second finish check costs $0.0039 per attempt; it applied a patch in 14 of 157 rows, 7 of which passed, so dropping it puts at most about 4.5 points at risk. Per-attempt cost is a tail: the top 10% of attempts carry 51% of it; attempts over 40 requests pass 15/17, so a request bound below the largest passing attempt (129 requests) would cost passes, and no bound was added.

Changes: `agent/src/openai.rs` sends `session-id` and `thread-id` equal to the cache key, which `codex_host.rs` now writes as a UUID like the Codex CLI's; `examples/codex_agent.hl` compacts once the context passes `compact_tokens` = 48,000 (p95 of the per-attempt peak context in the full run is 49K; the window-based trigger at 9/10 of 272K never fired, peak 108K); `agent/prompts/codex-instructions.md` says that `exec_command` sessions end with the turn, so services are started detached. The last came from the first screen rows: with the headers alone kv-store-grpc failed 0/2 (luna-full 3/3) because both attempts ran `python3 /app/server.py` as a session that died with the agent.

Measured, headers only (`out/rows-headers-only`, kv-store-grpc x2): $0.0052 per attempt against $0.0092, cache hit ratio 85% against 62%, every request extends the cached prefix. Full build 459f257 (`out/rows`): caffe-cifar-10 attempt 1 passed at $0.0559 against $0.2064 (95% hit ratio, one compaction from 48K to 6K tokens, 124 requests); the rest of the 16-task screen (caffe-cifar-10, compile-compcert, build-cython-ext, sanitize-git-repo and the 12 tasks of `suite.txt`, x2) was still running when this was written; `python3 compare.py ~/luna-full/out/rows out/rows suite.txt` prints the table against the same tasks' luna-full rows.

`scripts/check.sh`: docs-check needed the quoted `listing_unchecked` sim line requoted (the earlier compaction changes the trace; the mutation is still caught). codex-drill fails the same way on the luna-full base 9a81112 (the finish gate asks for a model step the drill script does not have), so that failure predates this change.

Commands: `cargo build --release` in `agent/`, binary patched with patchelf to `/opt/nanoeval-agent/lib` into `~/luna-lean/root`; `cd ~/luna-lean && setsid nohup sh -c 'sh go.sh; echo EXIT=$? >> out/go.exit' &` (one row at a time on ports 19760/14760, waits for load <= 26 and >= 12 GB free, removes the task images it pulled, stops on a 401, 403 or 429).

Verdict on 55 tasks, 2026-11-16: 71 attempts paired by task and attempt with the full-suite gate run. The cost-fix build passes 51 (71.8%, 60.5-81.0% Wilson) against 50 (70.4%, 59.0-79.8%) on the same attempts: 4 passes only with the fix (break-filter-js-from-html, build-pov-ray, git-multibranch, pypi-server), 3 only without (caffe-cifar-10, fix-git, largest-eigenval), sign test p = 1.0. Mean cost $0.0116 per attempt against $0.0284 on the same attempts (0.41x), 0.033x nanocode on Sonnet. The fix keeps the pass rate and cuts cost by 59%, so it becomes the default.

## 2026-11-16: CI, branch protection and the wasm ratchet

Changes now land through pull requests. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs on every push to master and every pull request: job `check` runs `scripts/check.sh` on a GitHub-hosted runner (4 cores, `CHECK_JOBS=4`) with `SEEDS=0..300`, `TWICE_SEEDS=0..60`, `RUST_DIFF_SEEDS`, `JS_DIFF_SEEDS` and `WASM_DIFF_SEEDS` at `0..40` and `WASM_NATIVE_DIFF_SEEDS=0..10`, so every stage still runs, every program's wasm module is diffed against the interpreter on every target on both the node and the native host, and the census still fails a program that lowers without a wasm run; the default seed counts and `HL_LONG=1` stay local. It then runs `hl prove` on every program in examples/, examples/inlined/ and examples/sound/ for all five targets (`scripts/gate.sh --prove`). Job `gate` runs `scripts/gate.sh`. Job `CI Gate` passes only when both did, and master's branch protection requires it. Actions are pinned by full commit SHA (checkout v7.0.1, setup-node v7.1.0, cache v6.1.0), the workflow token reads contents only and checkout keeps no credentials. The Rust toolchain is pinned to 1.100.0, the one the Hands build with.

`scripts/gate.sh` measures three counts from the code and compares them with `gate.txt`; a count that rises fails the job, and one that falls fails it too until the change commits the lower number (`scripts/gate.sh --write`), so the ratchet only tightens:

- `emitted_source_lines`: lines of `hl emit --format source` summed over the 27 programs in examples/*.hl, examples/inlined/ and examples/sound/ on all five targets. When the source backend is gone (`hl emit --format source` answers with usage) it counts 0.
- `host_lines_beyond_fixed`: lines of the files git tracks under the per-program hosts, today `agent/`.
- `programs_not_on_wasm`: program-target pairs whose deploy path is not compiler-emitted wasm on a fixed host, plus the hl-agent programs `agent/build.rs` builds from emitted source (each `emit_source(` call). A pair is on wasm when its platform has a fixed host in `hosts/` (`hosts/cloudflare.mjs`, `hosts/vercel.mjs`, `hosts/native/`) and `hl bundle` builds it, since every bundle carries the module `emit::wasm` writes.

It also prints each platform's fixed host size.

Measured at a80bd7f: emitted_source_lines 967,299; host_lines_beyond_fixed 11,255; programs_not_on_wasm 94: 81 native pairs (no `hosts/native/` yet), 3 Cloudflare and 7 Vercel pairs `hl bundle` refuses (code cells on code_agent, code_tasks and inlined/code_tasks on both; events on autoresearch, claude_agent, claude_code and codex_agent on Vercel), and mpp_agent.hl, codex_agent.hl and autoresearch.hl in hl-agent. The earlier hand count of 84 left out the 10 refused Cloudflare and Vercel pairs. Fixed hosts: cloudflare 769 lines, vercel 619 in 2 files, native none.

Commands: `scripts/gate.sh`, `scripts/gate.sh --prove` (33 s on the 5-core Hand).


## 2026-11-16: the wasm differential checks what hl prove proved

`hl prove` proves durability (a call's result is flushed before the next external dispatch), external calls at most once, and per-handler worst cases (journal records and outbound requests per run); until now only the interpreter's simulator checked these, and the wasm path was held to them only through `backend_equal` against the interpreter's trace. `hl wasm-sim` now checks them on what the compiled module itself does: an external dispatch (a tool call or the before_run hook of an external dispatch) while a tool or model answer is unflushed fails `proved_durability`; the same external call (turn, seq, tool, nested path) dispatched twice across a seed's runs fails `proved_external_once`; and `--records-max HANDLER=N` / `--requests-max HANDLER=N` fail `proved_worst_case` when one run flushes more journal records or makes more model and tool requests than N. The ok line reports external_once, records_per_run_max, requests_per_run_max and bounded_runs. `scripts/wasm-diff.sh` with `WASM_DIFF_HOST=native` runs `hl prove` on each program and target and passes every proved bound to `hl wasm-sim`, so CI's native-host wasm run of every program on every target asserts the proofs on the module's own execution counts. All 135 pairs pass at seeds 0..20 (47 s on the 5-core Hand); agent.hl on cloudflare peaks at 87 records and 43 requests per run.


Event intake records (`"record":{"event":…}`, a steer or other event taken into a run) are left out of the per-run record count, as `hl prove`'s worst case leaves them out: docs/snippets/events.hl's run handler is proved at 19 records per run and flushes 22 when three steers arrive, on the interpreter and the module alike, so the prover's records bound does not yet cover taken events. `src/generate/body.rs`'s statement budget is renamed `GENERATED_STATEMENTS_MAX`: docs-check matched it against LANGUAGE.md's `STATEMENTS_PER_HANDLER_MAX` = 65,536 and failed or passed depending on file order.


`proved_external_once` mirrors the fixed host's epoch fence: a dispatch from an owner whose epoch is below the one that last dispatched the call is refused by the host (the trace answers it failed) and is not counted, as the interpreter's world does; and a second owner taking over inside a hook leaves the outer run's epoch, unflushed answer and per-run counts as they were when it returns.
## 2026-11-16: a native host stores its journal through a Store

`harnesslang::wasm::native::run` and `idle` take any `Store` (`read`, `append(epoch, bytes)`, `keep(length)`, `epoch`) instead of the jsonl `Journal`, and pass the store's epoch to `hl_run` and `hl_idle`. `Journal` is the jsonl store with one owner at epoch 0, so every caller that passes a `Journal` is unchanged. A store that fences takeovers (SQLite, Postgres) refuses an append whose epoch is no longer the session's, and the run stops there; that is the seam the SQL drills and the fixed native host's SQL storage plug into, now that native-sqlite and server-postgres ship compiled wasm.


The SQL crash and takeover drill (`scripts/rust-sql-drill.sh`) now runs the compiled `program.wasm` through `native::run` on a SQL `Store` (`scripts/rust/module_sql_drill_host.rs`) instead of the emitted Rust source: the store renders the journal catalogue of `src/emit/sql` (acquire with a lease, load, the fenced commit) against real SQLite and Postgres 16, kills the host and the Postgres backend with `kill -9`, and fences a paused owner after a takeover. `flush_after_dispatch` is the wasm plant; `no_fence` drops the fence predicate from the catalogue (`Mutation::NoFencePredicate`); both are caught (SQLite: external ran before its call record was durable; the paused owner exits 0, not fenced). The host starts each run at the turn after the last acknowledged one, and the kill window is 0..60 ms (was 0..30) because a wasm run replays the journal in the interpreter: at 30 ms SQLite session 1 never got past turn 5 in 400 kills. Measured: SQLite 4 sessions, 35 kills; Postgres 2 sessions, 26 kills (12 of the backend); takeover at externals 1..6 on both. `scripts/rust/sql_drill_host.rs` (the Rust-source host) is gone.


## 2026-11-16: gate.sh without agent/

`scripts/gate.sh` counts hl-agent's emitted-source programs only while `agent/build.rs` exists, so the gate keeps working once agent/ is removed (its lines and programs then count 0, and the change lowers gate.txt).

A target whose `hl emit --format source` answers that the harness has no source form there (the JS backend deleted, wasm only) counts 0 emitted source lines.

## No CI and no pull requests

Georgios replaced CI with agent-driven verification. Each agent runs `scripts/ci.sh` (build, `check.sh` with every target's differential wasm run, `hl prove` on every program, the `gate.sh` ratchet) on a sandbox it gets itself, then pushes to master with the result in the commit message. `.github/workflows`, the `CI Gate` status and master's branch protection are gone. The ratchet and differential tests stay as scripts.
## hl-agent hosts every program as compiled WebAssembly; the MPP and climb hosts are parked

`agent/build.rs` emitted `examples/codex_agent.hl`, `examples/mpp_agent.hl` and `examples/autoresearch.hl` as native-jsonl Rust source, the last caller of the Rust source backend outside `hl build`. `codex_agent.hl` now compiles to WebAssembly like `claude_code.hl` and runs on the std-only wasm interpreter through `harnesslang::wasm::native::run`, with the same `World` answers the Claude host gives (tool, model, inbox, answer, recover, clock, sleep). Its drill is unchanged except the planted fault, which is the compiler's `batch_flush_skipped` plant instead of a text plant on emitted source: `CODEX_DRILL_SEED=1 scripts/codex-drill.sh` passes (12 kills, steer and cancel), and the plant is caught (`external calls dispatched more than once`). MPP and climb are parked, so their hosts leave rather than move: `hl-agent pay`, `agent/src/mpp.rs`, `mpp-server`, `mpp-payer`, `hl-climb`, their prompts and `scripts/mpp-drill.sh` and `scripts/mpp-payer-drill.mjs` are deleted, and both programs stay checked, simulated and compiled to every target. This is the first step to one fixed native host under `hosts/native/`.

## 2026-11-16: the Luna to Terra acceptance eval as a script

The 80.0% Luna to Terra baseline (108 of 135 scored attempts, Wilson 95% 72.5 to 85.9, 136 rows with one errored, of a planned 180) ran on the dev host as `~/terra-check/go-full.sh`. Its binary, `~/terra-check/root-esc2/bin/hl-agent`, was built from a worktree that was never pushed: no branch of this repository holds its `--escalate-model` flag. Its rows say what that flag did. Each attempt ran on `gpt-6-luna` at effort `medium`; when the finish check failed, the same session went on with `gpt-6.1-terra`, also at `medium`, with its whole history. 21 of the 135 scored rows reached Terra and 11 of those passed.

The recipe is now three files:

- `evals/agent-root.sh BINARY ROOT` makes the agent root `run.py` mounts at `/opt/nanoeval-agent`: the binary with its interpreter and runpath pointed at the mount, and the loader and libraries it needs copied from the build host. It fetches a pinned, checksummed patchelf into `output/eval/tools`, so the host needs none installed.
- `evals/luna-terra.sh [HL-AGENT ARGS]` runs `evals/suite-60.txt` (60 tasks, 3 attempts each) one task at a time through `run.py` on the ChatGPT route at `--parallel 2`, waits while the one-minute load is above 26, fewer than 12 GB are free or 4 nanoeval containers run, removes each task's images after it, skips rows that already have a `result.json`, and stops on a 401, 403 or 429. Its arguments go to hl-agent; the baseline's were `--escalate-model gpt-6.1-terra --effort medium`.
- `evals/lunaterra.py RUN_ROWS [BASELINE_ROWS]` prices every request of a row at its own model's list rate (`report.py`, the rates `agent/src/openai.rs` carries), so the Terra part of an escalated row costs Terra's rate, and prints the pass rate with its Wilson interval, $ per scored attempt, the escalated rows, and the paired comparison by source, task and attempt with its sign test.

Recomputed this way the baseline costs $0.0157 per scored attempt ($2.12 in all), against the $0.0153 the run reported, which priced each row from the agent's own usage report. Paired against the Luna-only luna-full rows on the same 135 attempts it passed 108 to 105 (14 only here, 11 only there, sign test p = 0.69) at $0.0157 against $0.0211.

The ChatGPT plan bills none of it: the dollars are the list price of the tokens metered, as before.

Smoke on master a80bd7f (hl-agent built with `cargo build --release` in `agent/`, root from `evals/agent-root.sh`), Luna only since master has no escalation: `SUITE=<two rows> ATTEMPTS=1 PARALLEL=1 evals/luna-terra.sh --effort medium` on the dev host passed nanoeval write-greeting (19 s, 6 requests) and terminal-bench-2.1 build-cython-ext (5 min, 51 requests), 2/2 at $0.0130 per attempt, every request `gpt-6-luna` at `medium` and HTTP 200, each task's images removed after it.

The acceptance run on a build whose Codex harness runs as compiled wasm on the native fixed host is `evals/luna-terra.sh` with that build's root and the same arguments, compared with `evals/lunaterra.py <its rows> ~/terra-check/out-full/rows`. Escalation to Terra is a harness decision, so it has to come back as an option of `codex_agent.hl`, not of the host; until it does, a run is Luna only and pairs with the luna-full rows.

The same smoke on the pull request that moves `codex_agent.hl` onto compiler-emitted wasm (its head 4133119, before it merged) passed both rows too, 2/2 at $0.0099 per attempt, every request `gpt-6-luna` at `medium` and HTTP 200, so the recipe runs a wasm-hosted Codex harness unchanged.
## The three agent programs build their own provider requests

`examples/claude_code.hl`, `examples/claude_code_child.hl` and `examples/codex_agent.hl` now declare their model `wire anthropic` or `wire openai` and compute every request themselves, so the host only sends what the program built. What hl-agent decided in Rust moved into the programs:

- A `configure` handler takes what the command line knows. Claude: `configure(model_name, requested_effort, cwd)`; codex: `configure(model_name, requested_effort, finish_checks, reviewer, cwd)`. `requested_effort` is `"default"` or a level. The Claude model id is a text enum of the max_tokens table and codex `finish_checks` is `"2" | "1" | "0"`, so the host's argument check rejects an unknown model or a count above 2, as hl-agent's usage error did. It fails only for a working directory holding a single quote. Handlers that call the model require `configured`. It is idempotent; the host calls it on every run, and passes the parent's arguments to a child's `configure`.
- Environment facts come from one tool call the program makes the first time it is configured (`Bash` in the Claude programs, `exec_command` in codex): a POSIX `sh` probe that prints marked, JSON-escaped fields (working directory, git repository, platform, shell, OS version, date, and for codex the time zone, a fresh UUID for the prompt cache key and the shape of `OPENAI_BASE_URL`) and AGENTS.md in chunks of 24000 escaped bytes, at most `agents_chunks_max` = 4 calls. The escaping is `od` plus `awk` so the probe needs nothing past POSIX tools; UTF-8 becomes `\\u` escapes. The journal therefore gains one to four tool records in the first turn of a session. The facts stay in state, so later runs and resumes do not probe again. A probe that fails, ends unknown or prints no end marker leaves the facts empty and the next `configure` probes again, as hl-agent left out an environment line it could not read.
- url and headers carry `${ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL:-https://api.anthropic.com}`, `${ANTHROPIC_API_KEY}`, `${OPENAI_BASE_URL...}` and `${OPENAI_API_KEY}` for the host to expand. hl-agent stripped a trailing `/` and `/v1` from `OPENAI_BASE_URL`; the probe reports which of those shapes the variable has and the program picks the matching url template. OpenAI headers add `session-id` and `thread-id` with the cache key, as hl-agent did; hl-agent sent no ChatGPT account header, so neither do the programs.
- head and tail are the request body around the rendered messages or input, in hl-agent's key order. Claude: model, max_tokens from nanocode's table (128000 or 64000), the system blocks of `agent/prompts/claude-code-system.json` plus the environment block and the AGENTS.md block, the tools of `tools.json` with the parent or child half of `subagent-tools.json` and a cache marker on the last tool, messages, stream, `tool_choice` none for `text_only`, then adaptive thinking with `output_config.effort` for adaptive models and disabled thinking otherwise. Refresh sends max_tokens 0 and stream false. Effort is pinned per turn as before; compaction uses effort low as before. Codex: model, the instructions of `codex-instructions.md`, input (the AGENTS.md and `<environment_context>` items as the wire's `preamble`), the tools of `codex-tools.json`, `tool_choice` auto, parallel tool calls, reasoning effort (low by default for `-sol` and `-terra` models, else medium), store false, stream, encrypted reasoning, `prompt_cache_key` and `text.verbosity` low for `gpt-5` and `gpt-6` models.
- The codex prompt handler now runs the finish checks and reviews that codex_host.rs ran as extra prompts: after the model ends a turn, up to `finish_checks` (default and most 2) turns with the FINISH_CHECK text, then up to `reviews_max` = 2 reviews. All passes share the prompt's one round loop of `rounds_max` = 512 rounds, which keeps the worst case under Cloudflare's 10000 outbound requests per invocation (9728); each pass restarts the round budget the host passes, counts a turn and pins effort again.
- The reviewer is a second call of the same model: the reviewer system prompt as instructions, the agent's context plus the task in `<task>` tags as input, tool_choice none, effort low, max_output_tokens 8000. A harness has one model, so the reviewer no longer runs on claude-sonnet-4-6, and the file relay is gone: `reviewer` is `""` (no review), `"on"` (the agent's own Responses url) or a Responses url. A first line of DONE ends the task; any other answer comes back as REVIEWED plus the review; a failed review call ends reviewing, as before.
- Text literals are one line each, generated from `agent/prompts` and codex_host.rs by `scripts/program-literals.py`, which rewrites the generated fns in place and is idempotent. Run it after changing a prompt.
## 2026-11-16: hl-host, the fixed native host under hosts/native

`hosts/native` (crate `hl-host`, std only, depends only on `harnesslang`) runs any native-jsonl module and knows no provider or harness. It answers the syscalls (`Bash`, `Read`, `Edit`, `Write`, `exec_command`, `write_stdin`, `apply_patch`, `update_plan`, `view_image`, `list_agents`, `submit_result`, spawns), the `World` requests (jobs, hold, sleep, clock, inbox, answer, listing, recover, resume), the control directory (`steer`, `cancel`) and the usage ledger. A model request is answered only when it carries the wire language's `http` request: the host expands `${NAME}` and `${NAME:-default}` in its url and headers from the environment (an empty value counts as unset), sends it with the std plain-HTTP client for `http://` and curl otherwise, and answers `{status, headers, events}` for a 2xx `text/event-stream`, `{status, headers, body}` otherwise, `{transport}` when it could not complete and `{stopped}` when a cancel arrived. Pricing, request bodies, retries and stream decoding stay in the program. `usage.json` keeps its token, request, tool and wall fields and drops `cost_usd`.

- Commands: `hl-host run --module M --journal D --handler H --args JSON` (any program; `--spawn tool=program:handler` names a spawn the tool request does not carry), `hl-host steer|cancel --journal D`, `hl-host wire --request FILE --scratch DIR` (one wire request, for probes), and the hl-agent compatible front `hl-host run --instruction F --cwd D --model M --journal D [--harness] [--max-turns] [--effort] [--reviewer-relay] [--finish-checks] [--keep-warm] [--turn]`, which runs `configure(cwd, model_name, requested_effort[, finish_checks, reviewer])` then `prompt(input, rounds)` once, and the child program's `configure` with the parent's arguments before its handler.
- Modules come from `--module-dir`, else beside the binary, else the three embedded at build time from `examples/`.
- The mock servers move to `scripts/fixtures` (own crate). `DRILL_HOST=hl-host` points both drills at hl-host; the default stays hl-agent until `agent/` is deleted.
- Checked: `cargo test` and clippy in `hosts/native`; `scripts/fixtures/wire-probe.sh` sends Messages and Responses wire requests to the mocks (events split, `:-` default, unset name, refused port); fixture programs `scripts/fixtures/programs/syscalls*.hl` exercise Write, Edit, apply_patch, Bash, an unknown syscall and a spawn with join through `hl-host run`. The drills under `DRILL_HOST=hl-host` need the wire models (lane1-wire).
## 2026-11-16: codex_agent.hl escalates a failed finish check to a second model

Escalation from Luna to Terra comes back as an option of the harness, not the host: `configure` gains `escalate_model: text` as its last parameter, `""` for none, and the hl-agent compatible front passes `--escalate-model <model>` through it. It reproduces the unpushed binary behind the 80.0% baseline, as reconstructed from its rows:

- The FINISH_CHECK text now asks the model to end its report with the line `VERDICT: PASS` when every check passed, or `VERDICT: FAIL` when any failed or a stated behaviour is unverified.
- When an escalate model is configured and a finish-check turn ends with a report whose last line, after trimming, is not exactly `VERDICT: PASS` (a failed or missing verdict), the same session goes on with its whole context on the escalate model, at the same effort, with the ESCALATE prompt. Every later request of the session, the remaining finish checks, reviews and compaction included, goes to that model. It escalates at most once per session.
- The model id lives in state as `request_model`; `compose` rebuilds `head` and `review_head` from it, so a resumed or re-configured session keeps the escalated model. `configure` never clears `escalated`, since the host calls it on every run. Text verbosity follows the model the request goes to.
- The host logs nothing about it: the switch shows in the journal as the ESCALATE user message and the model id of the requests after it.
- The verdict is read with `contains` and `after` in a loop of at most `verdict_lines_max` = 8 marks; a report repeating the mark more often counts as not passed. The extra pass shares the prompt's one round loop, so the proved worst cases are unchanged.
